\begin{split}&\frac{1}{4}\|\partial_{s}B\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}^{2}+\|\partial_{s}\Phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}+\|\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{+}}(B,\Phi)\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}^{2}\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}\frac{R_{g}}{4}|\Psi|^{2}-\frac{i}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}F_{A}\wedge*_{4}w_{r}-\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}ds\,F_{B}\wedge(*_{Y}\nu_{+}).\\
\end{split}

Inserting both the preceding formula and (
4.32
) into
the following inequality

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{4}\|\partial_{s}B\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}^{2}+\|\partial_{s}\Phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}+\|\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{+}}(B,\Phi)\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\partial_{s}B\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}^{2}+\|\partial_{s}\Phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}+\|\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(B,\Phi)\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}(A,\Psi)\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}^{2},\end{split}

we have

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\quad\leq-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(B,\Phi)+r\wp_{i}(L-l)-2\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(\partial_{s}\,f_{\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)})(B,\Phi)\\
&\qquad-\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}\frac{R_{g}}{4}|\Psi|^{2}+\frac{i}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}F_{A}\wedge*_{4}w_{r}+\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge F_{B_{0}}\\
&\qquad+\|\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}(A,\Psi)\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.33)

Argue as in (
4.25
), (
4.20
), (
4.21
), (
4.22
) to bound the
fourth to the last terms on the right hand side of the preceding
formula and rearranging, we get:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{8}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\quad\leq-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(B,\Phi)+r(\wp_{i}^{+}+z_{i})|\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}|+\zeta r|\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}|_{1}+\zeta^{\prime}|\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}|\\
&\quad\quad-2\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(\partial_{s}\,f_{\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)})(B,\Phi),\\
\end{split}

(4.34)

where
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}
are positive constants that depend only on
the choice of reference connection
A_{0}
, the metric,
\nu
, and the constants

\varsigma_{w}
,
\textsl{z}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Note that over

\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}
,

\begin{split}&-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\nu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(B,\Phi)-{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})(A,\Psi)\\
&\quad=-\frac{i}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}F_{B_{0}}\wedge\mu_{r}\leq\zeta_{e}r\,e^{-\kappa_{i}l}|\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}|_{1},\end{split}

(4.35)

where
\zeta_{e}
is a positive constant depending
only on
B_{0}
and
\nu
. Thus, the bound (
4.34
) is equivalent to

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{8}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\quad\leq{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})(A,\Psi)+r(\wp_{i}^{+}+z_{i})|\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}|+\zeta_{o}r|\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}|_{1}+\zeta^{\prime}_{o}|\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}|\\
&\quad\quad-2\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(\partial_{s}f_{\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}})(B,\Phi),\\
\end{split}

(4.36)

where
\zeta_{o},\zeta^{\prime}_{o}
are likewise positive constants that depend only on
the choice of reference connection
A_{0}
, the metric,
\nu
, and the constants

\varsigma_{w}
,
\textsl{z}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

We now estimate the last term above,

-2\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(\partial_{s}\,f_{\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)})(B,\Phi)
. This vanishes unless
\hat{Y}_{i}
is a vanishing end; so we assume
from now on that
\hat{Y}=\hat{Y}_{i}
is a vanishing end.
According to (
3.6
) (and recalling the definitions and
assumptions on
\chi_{i}
,
\lambda_{i}
there),

(\partial_{s}f_{\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}})(B,\Phi)=\chi^{\prime}_{i}(s)\,f_{{\mathfrak{q}}_{i}}(B,\Phi)+\lambda^{\prime}_{i}(s)\,f_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(B,\Phi).

This is supported on
\hat{Y}_{[{\mathfrak{l}}_{i},{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}^{\prime}]}
, and by our assumption on the cutoff functions
\chi_{i}
and
\lambda_{i}
, its absolute value is bounded by

|f_{{\mathfrak{q}}_{i}}(B(s),\Phi(s))|+|f_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(B(s),\Phi(s))|
. At each
s\in[{\mathfrak{l}}_{i},{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}^{\prime}]
,

|f_{{\mathfrak{q}}_{i}}(B(s),\Phi(s))|
and
|f_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(B(s),\Phi(s))|
can
be bounded via a variant of Lemma
3.8
: Applying the
triangle inequality differently in the last step of
(
3.12
) and recalling Assumption
3.4
4d), one may arrange so that:

\begin{split}&|f_{{\mathfrak{q}}_{i}}(B(s),\Phi(s))|+|f_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(B(s),\Phi(s))|\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{1}{32}\|F_{B}-F_{B_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(Y_{i:s})}^{2}+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\big(\|\Phi\|_{L^{2}(Y_{i:s})}^{2}+1\big),\end{split}

where
\zeta^{\prime}_{2}
is a positive constants depending only on
\textsl{z}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and the metric on
Y_{i}
. Integrating the preceding
inequality over
I:=[l,L]\cap[{\mathfrak{l}}_{i},{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}^{\prime}]\subset{\mathbb{R}}
, and
appealing again to (
4.18
), we get

\begin{split}&\Big|-2\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(\partial_{s}f_{\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}})(B,\Phi)\Big|\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{16}\|F_{B}-F_{B_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{I})}^{2}+\zeta^{\prime}_{3}\big(\|\Psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{I})}+|\hat{Y}_{I}|\big)\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{16}\|F_{B}-F_{B_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{I})}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\Big\|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu^{+}_{r})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{I})}+\zeta^{\prime}_{4}r|\hat{Y}_{I}|,\end{split}

(4.37)

where
\zeta_{3}^{\prime}
is a positive constant depending only on

\textsl{z}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and the metric on
Y_{i}
, and
\zeta_{4}^{\prime}
is a
positive constant depending only on
\nu
, and
\varsigma_{w}
,

\textsl{z}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and the metric.
Insert the preceding estimate into (
4.36
),
we arrive at
(\ref {eq:E-top-bdd0})
(b).
\Box

4.4
Lower bounds for
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{[l,L]})

We need a lower bound that
is independent of
\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}
,
[l,L]\subset[0,\infty]

being arbitrary. When
\hat{Y}=\hat{Y}_{i}
is a vanishing end,
suppose in addition that
l\geq{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}^{\prime}
, so that the last term of
(
4.32
) vanishes on such
\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}
. Then, for
an admissible solution
(A,\Psi)=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
to
{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(A_{r},\Psi_{r})=0
, we have:

\begin{split}&-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}(B,\Phi)\\
&\quad\quad=\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{s}B\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})}^{2}+2\|\partial_{s}\Phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})}-\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge(F_{B}-F_{B_{0}})\\
&\quad\quad\geq-\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge(F_{B}-F_{B_{0}}).\end{split}

(4.38)

Ideally,
1/r
times the last term above should be bounded
independently of both

r
and
\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}
. Unfortunately, this is far from
straightforward. Note that similar terms do not appear in the setting
of
[
LT
]
or
[
HT3
]
. What follows is a first attempt to
tackle this trouble-making term.

To proceed, use (
4.32
), (
4.28
), (
4.29
),
Lemma
3.11
to get that for
r\geq r_{0}
,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{s}B\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})}^{2}+2\|\partial_{s}\Phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})}\\
&\quad=-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l,\infty]}}(B,\Phi)+\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,l}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge(F_{B_{i}}-F_{B_{0}})\\
&\qquad\quad+\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,l}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge(F_{B}-F_{B_{i}})\end{split}

(4.39)

The last term above may be re-expressed as follows:

\begin{split}&\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,l}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge(F_{B}-F_{B_{i}})\\
&\qquad\qquad=\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,l}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge d_{Y}(B-B_{i})\\
&\qquad\qquad=\frac{ir}{4}\int_{l}^{\infty}ds\int_{Y_{i,s}}d(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge(B-B_{i})\\
&\qquad\qquad=-\frac{ir}{4}\int_{l}^{\infty}ds\int_{Y_{i,s}}d(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge\int_{s}^{\infty}\partial_{t}B(t)\,dt\\
&\qquad\qquad=\frac{ir}{4}\int_{l}^{\infty}ds\int_{Y_{i,s}}(\partial_{s}\xi_{\nu})\wedge\int_{s}^{\infty}\partial_{t}B(t)\,dt.\\
\end{split}

(4.40)

The second equality above uses the Stokes’ theorem. The third equality
uses the facts that
\int_{s}^{\infty}\partial_{t}B(t)\,dt=B_{i}-B(s)
. The last equality follows from (
4.28
).

The last expression may in turn be estimated as follows:

\begin{split}&\Big|\frac{ir}{4}\int_{l}^{\infty}ds\,\int_{Y_{i:s}}(\partial_{s}\xi_{\nu})\wedge\big(\int_{s}^{\infty}\partial_{t}B(t)\,dt\big)\Big|\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq\zeta r\int_{l}^{\infty}ds\,e^{-\kappa_{i}s}\int_{s}^{\infty}dt\int_{Y_{i}}|\partial_{t}B(t)|\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq\zeta^{\prime}r\int_{l}^{\infty}ds\,e^{-\kappa_{i}s}\int_{s}^{\infty}dt\,\|\partial_{t}B(t)\|_{L^{2}(Y_{i,t})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq\zeta^{\prime\prime}r\int_{l}^{\infty}ds\,e^{-\kappa_{i}s}\|\partial_{s}B\|_{L^{2}(Y_{i,s})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\partial_{s}B\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})}+\zeta_{2}r^{2}e^{-2\kappa_{i}l}.\end{split}

(4.41)

The third inequality above uses the fact that
(B_{i},\Phi_{i})
is by the
assumption (0) in Theorem
1.8
regular, and therefore

\|\partial_{t}B(t)\|_{L^{2}(Y_{i,t})}
decays exponentially to

0
.

Combining (
4.39
), (
4.40
), (
4.41
), one has

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{4}\|\partial_{s}B\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})}^{2}+2\|\partial_{s}\Phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})}\\
&\quad\leq-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l,\infty]}}(B,\Phi)+\zeta_{i}r\,e^{-\kappa_{i}l}+\zeta^{\prime}_{i}r^{2}e^{-2\kappa_{i}l},\end{split}

where
\zeta_{i}
,
\zeta_{i}^{\prime}
are positive constants;
\zeta_{i}
depends only on
\nu
,
B_{0}
, the constant
\textsl{z}_{i}

in Lemma
3.11
;
\zeta_{i}^{\prime}
depends only on
\nu
.
Rearranging, we get

-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l,\infty]}}(B,\Phi)\geq-\zeta_{i}^{\prime\prime}(r^{2}e^{-2\kappa_{i}l}+1),

and by (
4.35
),
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})(A,\Psi)={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})(A,\Psi)
satisfies
a similar inequality:

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})(A,\Psi)\geq-\underline{\zeta}_{i}(r^{2}e^{-2\kappa_{i}l}+1).

In particular, setting
\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i}:=\kappa_{i}^{-1}
, and choosing

r_{0}
to be greater than

\max_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}}\exp\,(4{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}^{\prime}/\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i})
, we have that for
r\geq r_{0}
,

\begin{split}-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial Y_{i,[l,\infty]}}(B,\Phi)&\geq-2\,\zeta_{i}^{\prime\prime}r\qquad\forall l\geq\big(\frac{\ln r}{2}\big)\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i};\\
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})(A,\Psi)={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,l})(A,\Psi)&\geq-2\underline{\zeta}_{i}r\qquad\forall l\geq\big(\frac{\ln r}{2}\big)\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i}\end{split}

(4.42)

since
r^{2}e^{-2\kappa_{i}l}\leq r
when
l\geq\big(\frac{\ln r}{2}\big)\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i}
.
In the above, the positive constants
\zeta_{i}^{\prime\prime},\underline{\zeta}_{i}>0
depend only on
\nu
,
B_{0}
, and the constant
\textsl{z}_{i}

in Lemma
3.11
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}}\colon\thinspace{\mathfrak{Y}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
be the function given by

\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}}(i)=(\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i})_{i}
, and write
{\bf l}_{r}:=(\ln r)\,\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}}
.
Under the assumption (
4.13
) (b), the preceding pair of
inequalities then implies that for
r\geq r_{0}
and
\forall{\bf l}\geq\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}}_{r}/2
with
{\bf l}(i)=:l_{i}
,

\begin{split}{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})(A,\Psi)&={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(X)(A,\Psi)-\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,l_{i}})(A,\Psi)\\
&\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+2\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}\underline{\zeta}_{i})\quad\text{assuming (\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (b); similarly, }\\
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})(A,\Psi)&\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+2\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}\underline{\zeta}_{i})\quad\text{assuming (\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (a).}\end{split}

(4.43)

Combining the preceding upper bounds for

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})
,

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})
with (
4.16
), we
have for any
r\geq r_{0}
and
\forall{\bf l}\geq\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}}_{r}/2
with
{\bf l}(i)=:l_{i}
,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{8}\int_{X_{{\bf l}}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{X_{{\bf l}}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{X_{{\bf l}}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\qquad\quad\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+\zeta)+\zeta^{\prime}\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}r^{\mathrm{a}_{i}}l_{i}\quad\text{assuming (\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (a)};\\
&\frac{1}{16}\int_{X_{{\bf l}}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{X_{{\bf l}}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\int_{X_{{\bf l}}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\qquad\quad\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+\zeta_{p})+\zeta^{\prime}_{p}\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}r^{\mathrm{a}_{i}}l_{i}\quad\text{assuming (\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (b).}\end{split}

(4.44)

(As in Lemma
3.12
,
\mathrm{a}_{i}=1
when
\hat{Y}_{i}
is a
Morse end, and
\mathrm{a}_{i}=0
when it is a vanishing end.)
In particular, given
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}
, by taking
{\bf l}
above to be
such that
{\bf l}(j)=(\frac{\ln r}{2})\,\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{j}
when

j\neq i
and
{\bf l}(i)=l\geq(\frac{\ln r}{2})\,\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i}
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{1}{16}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l-1,l]}}|F_{A}|^{2}&<\frac{1}{16}\int_{X_{{\bf l}}}|F_{A}|^{2}\\
&\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+\zeta_{1}\ln r)+\zeta_{2}r^{\mathrm{a}_{i}}\big(l-\frac{\ln r}{2}\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i}\big)\qquad\forall\,l\geq\frac{\ln r}{2}\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i}.\end{split}

The positive
constants
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}
, as well as
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
in
(
4.44
), depend only on the metric, the
\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}\nolimits^{c}

structure,
\nu
,
\varsigma_{w}

B_{0}
, and
\max_{j\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}\textsl{z}_{j}
,
\textsl{z}_{j}
being the constants
in Lemma
3.11
. In particular, they are
independent of
r
,
l
, and
i
.

Combining the preceding bound with (
4.38
), and
choosing
r_{0}
to be sufficiently large (depending on
e
), we have for all

l\geq\frac{\ln r}{2}\,\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i}

and any
L\geq l
,

\begin{split}&-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}}(B,\Phi)\\
&\qquad\qquad\geq-\zeta r\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\kappa_{i}(l+n)}\|F_{B}-F_{B_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l+n,l+n+1]})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\geq-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\kappa_{i}(l+n)}\big(\zeta^{\prime}r^{2}+r\,(\textsc{e}+\zeta_{1}\ln r)+\zeta_{2}r^{\mathrm{a}_{i}}(l+n)\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad\geq-\zeta_{3}\,r^{2}e^{-\kappa_{i}l}-\zeta_{4}\,re^{-\kappa_{i}l/2}\quad\text{when $r\geq r_{0}$.}\end{split}

In particular, for any
r\geq r_{0}
,
l\geq(\ln r)\,\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}_{i}
, and
L\geq l
, we have

\begin{split}-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}}(B,\Phi)&\geq-\zeta_{5}\,r\quad\text{and equivalently (by (\ref {diff:CSD-E})), }\\
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A,\Psi)&={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A,\Psi)\geq-\zeta^{\prime}_{5}\,r.\quad\end{split}

(4.45)

The positive constants
\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4},\zeta_{5},\zeta_{5}^{\prime}

above depend only on the metric, the
\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}\nolimits^{c}

structure,
\nu
,
\varsigma_{w}
,
B_{0}
, and
\max_{j\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}\textsl{z}_{j}
.

4.5
Lemma
4.6
and some of its variants

In this part we prove Lemma
4.6

and some of its variants.

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

The rightmost inequalities in (
4.14
) (i) and the left
most
inequalies in (
4.14
) (ii) follow respectively from
(
4.43
) and
(
4.45
). To get the remaining two inequalities in
(
4.14
), we need a lower bound on
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})(A,\Psi)
or
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})(A,\Psi)
. Here is preliminary bound from (
4.16
): for all
{\bf l}\colon\thinspace{\mathfrak{Y}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
,
{\bf l}(i)=:l_{i}
.

\begin{split}&{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})(A,\Psi)\geq-\zeta_{p}(r+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}r^{\mathrm{a}_{i}}l_{i});\\
&{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})(A,\Psi)\geq-\zeta(r+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}r^{\mathrm{a}_{i}}l_{i}),\end{split}

where
\zeta
,
\zeta_{p}
are positive constants depending only on
the parameters listed in (
4.15
).
Combined with (
4.45
), this implies: For any
r\geq r_{0}
,

X_{\bf l}
,
{\bf l}\geq{\bf l}_{r}
and
l_{i}:={\bf l}(i)
(possibly

\infty
),

\begin{split}{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})(A,\Psi)&={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{{\bf l}_{r}})(A,\Psi)+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,[{\bf l}_{r}(i),l]})\\
&\geq-\zeta^{\prime}r\ln r;\quad\text{similarly,}\\
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(X_{\bf l})(A,\Psi)&\geq-\zeta_{p}^{\prime}r\ln r,\end{split}

(4.46)

where
\zeta^{\prime}
,
\zeta^{\prime}_{p}
are positive constants depending only on
the parameters listed in (
4.15
),

B_{0}
, and the constants
\textsl{z}_{i}

in Lemma
3.11
. This is precisely what was asserted in the
leftmost inequalities in (
4.14
) (i).
Meanwhile, combining (
4.46
) and (
4.43
),
one has: For any
\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}\subset X-\mathring{X}_{{\bf l}_{r}}
,
L
possibly
\infty
, we have:

\begin{split}{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A,\Psi)&={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{{\bf L}_{i}})(A,\Psi)-{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{{\bf l}_{i}})(A,\Psi)\\
&\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+\zeta_{e}\ln r),\\
\end{split}

(4.47)

where
{\bf l}_{i},{\bf L}_{i}\colon\thinspace{\mathfrak{Y}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
are such that

{\bf l}_{i}(j)={\bf L}_{i}(j)={\bf l}_{r}(j)
for
j\neq i
, and

{\bf l}_{i}(i)=l
;
{\bf L}_{i}(i)=L
. In the above,

\zeta^{\prime}
depends only on
the parameters listed in (
4.15
),

B_{0}
, and the constants
\textsl{z}_{i}

in Lemma
3.11
. The preceding inequality leads directly to
the second inequality in (
4.14
) (ii).
\Box

A straightforward corollary of Lemma
4.6
is:

Corollary 4.10
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
. There is a positive constant
\zeta
independent of

r
,
X_{\bullet}
, and
(A,\Psi)
such that

\begin{split}\text{\rm(i) }&{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\geq-\zeta\,r\quad\text{for all
$r\geq r_{0}$ and $X_{\bullet}\subset X-X^{\prime\prime}=X_{v}$; }\\
&\qquad\text{consequently}\\
\text{\rm(ii) }&{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X^{\prime\prime})(A,\Psi)\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+\zeta);\end{split}

similarly for
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)
.

Proof.
We shall prove only the statement for

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)
, since the proof for

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)
is the same.

According to (
4.16
), for all

X_{\bullet}\subset X_{v}

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\geq-(\zeta^{\prime}r+\zeta_{0}|X_{\bullet}|).

It follows that for any
X_{\bullet}\subset X_{v}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\\
&\quad={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet}\cap X_{{\bf l}_{r}})(A,\Psi)+{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet}-X_{{\bf l}_{r}})(A,\Psi)\\
&\quad\geq-(\zeta^{\prime}r+\zeta^{\prime}_{0}\ln r)-\zeta^{\prime\prime}r\geq-\zeta r.\end{split}

In the above, we applied the preceding inequality to bound the first term in
the second line, and used the first inequality in (
4.14
)
(ii) to bound the second term. This proves Inequality (i)
asserted by the corollary. To obtain Inequality (ii), simply
write
{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X^{\prime\prime})(A,\Psi)={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X)(A,\Psi)-{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X-X^{\prime\prime})(A,\Psi)
, then combine with the bounds from (i)
and (
4.13
).
\Box

For future reference, note that the arguments above in fact establishes the following
generalization of Lemma
4.6
:

Lemma 4.11
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
.
Suppose
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
is a function from
[r_{0},\infty)

to
(1,\infty)
such that the lower bound on

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})
in (
4.45
) holds for any
given

r\geq r_{0}
and
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{\prime}-X_{{\mathfrak{t}}(r)\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}},m}
. That is, assume that

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\geq-\zeta^{\prime}_{5}\,r\quad\forall X_{\bullet}\subset X^{\prime}-X_{{\mathfrak{t}}(r)\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}},m},r\geq r_{0}.

(4.48)

(In particular, it follows from Lemma
4.6
that this holds
with
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)=\ln r
.)
Then there exist positive constants
\zeta
,
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{2}

such that for any

,

\begin{split}\text{\rm(i) }&-r(\zeta_{1}+\zeta_{2}{\mathfrak{t}}(r))\leq{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+\zeta)\quad\text{$\forall X_{\bullet}\supset X_{{\mathfrak{t}}(r)\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}},m}$};\\
\text{\rm(ii) }&-\zeta_{1}r\leq{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\leq r\,(\textsc{e}+\zeta_{2}{\mathfrak{t}}(r)+\zeta_{3})\quad\text{ $\forall X_{\bullet}\subset X-\mathring{X}_{{\mathfrak{t}}(r)\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}},m}$}.\end{split}

(4.49)

The positive constants

,

,
\varsigma_{w}
,
B_{0}
, and the constants

in Lemma

.
In particular, these constants
are independent of
X_{\bullet}
and
r
.

A similar statement holds for
{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)
, with the constants in the
inequalities depending additionally on
\textsl{z}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Proof.
We shall again prove only the inequalities for

{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)
, since the proof for
{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)
is identical.

The first inequality in (
4.49
) (ii) follows from the
assumption (
4.48
) and Inequality (i) in Corollary
4.10
.
Replacing (
4.45
) and (

) by this inequality, the arguments in
the previous subsection and in the proof of Lemma

above can be
repeated to replace (

), (

) (
4.47
)
with their respective companion versions. These are respectively the
second inequality in (

) (i), the first inequality in
(
4.49
) (i), and the
second inequality in (
4.49
) (ii).

\Box

4.6
L^{2}_{1,loc/A}
-bounds for
(A-A_{0},\Psi)

It follows from the discussion in the preceding subsection that:

Proposition 4.12
.

Adopt the notation and assumptions of Lemma
4.6
.
Let
\zeta_{0}
,
\zeta^{\prime\prime}
,
\zeta^{\prime\prime}_{p}

be the constants from Lemma

4.7
,
Then there exist constants
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{1}^{\prime}

such that the following hold for any
r\geq r_{0}
, any
(A,\Psi)
, and any compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X

satisfying either
\partial X_{\bullet}\subset X-\mathring{X}_{{\bf l}_{r}}
or
X_{\bullet}\subset X_{{\bf l}_{r}}
:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{8}\int_{X_{\bullet}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{X_{\bullet}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{X_{\bullet}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq r\,\big(\zeta_{0}|X_{\bullet,m}|+\textsc{e}+\zeta_{1}\ln r\big)+\zeta^{\prime\prime}|X_{\bullet}|\quad\text{assuming
(\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (a)};\\
&\frac{1}{16}\int_{X_{\bullet}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{X_{\bullet}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\int_{X_{\bullet}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq r\,\big(\zeta_{0}|X_{\bullet,m}|+\textsc{e}+\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\ln r\big)+\zeta^{\prime\prime}_{p}|X_{\bullet}|\quad\text{assuming
(\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (b)}.\end{split}

(4.50)

The constants
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{1}^{\prime}
above depend only on the parameters listed in
(
4.15
), together with
B_{0}
and the constants
\textsl{z}_{i}
in
Lemma
3.11
.
In particular, it is independent of
r
and

X_{\bullet}
.

Moreover, if the assumption (
4.48
) in Lemma
4.11
holds, then
the statement above holds with all appearances of
\ln r
replaced by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
.

Proof.
When
X_{\bullet}
is such that

\partial X_{\bullet}\subset X-\mathring{X}_{{\bf l}_{r}}
, the claim of
the proposition
follows directly from Lemmas
4.6
,
4.11
and

4.7
. The case when
X_{\bullet}\subset X_{{\bf l}_{r}}
follows from the preceding
case, together with the observation that when
X_{\bullet}\subset X_{{\bf l}_{r}}
,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{8}\int_{X_{\bullet}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{X_{\bullet}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{X_{\bullet}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{1}{8}\int_{X_{{\bf l}_{r}}}|F_{A}|^{2}+\int_{X_{{\bf l}_{r}}}|\nabla_{A}\Psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{X_{{\bf l}_{r}}}\big|\frac{i}{4}\rho(\mu_{r}^{+})-(\Psi\Psi^{*})_{0}\big|^{2};\\
\end{split}

\Box

As a consequence,

Proposition 4.13
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
, and recall the reference connection
A_{0}
from (
3.3
). Fix a compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X
and an
r\geq r_{0}
,
r_{0}
being
as in the previous proposition. Let
u_{r}\in C^{\infty}(X_{\bullet},S^{1})
be such that
u_{r}\cdot(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
is in the gauge specified in

[
KM
]
’s Equations (5.2) and (5.3). Then there exist positive
constants
\zeta_{2}
,
\zeta_{2}^{\prime}
depending only on the parameters listed in
(
4.15
), such that

\begin{split}&\|(u_{r}\cdot A_{r}-A_{0},\Psi_{r})\|^{2}_{L_{1,A_{r}}^{2}(X_{\bullet})}\leq\zeta_{2}r\,\big(|X_{\bullet,m}|+\textsc{e}+\ln r\big)|X_{\bullet}|+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}|X_{\bullet}|^{2}\end{split}

(4.51)

Again, if the assumption (
4.48
) in Lemma
4.11
holds, then
the statement above holds with
all appearances of
\ln r
replaced by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
.

Proof.
Combining (
4.50
) with standard elliptic estimates
(cf. e.g. pp. 101-104 of
[
KM
]
and noting that the lowest absolute
value of non-zero eigenvalues of
*d+d*
on
X_{\bullet}
has a
lower bound propotional to
|X_{\bullet}|^{-1}
),
one may find a positive
constant
\zeta
depending only on the metric on
X
such that

\|u_{r}\cdot A_{r}-A_{0}\|^{2}_{L_{1}^{2}(X_{\bullet})}+\|\nabla_{A}\Psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(X_{\bullet})}\leq\zeta|X_{\bullet}|\cdot(\text{RHS of (\ref {eq:L^2_1}) (b)}).

Meanwhile, a combination of (
4.50
) with (
4.18
)
(with
{\mathfrak{f}}
set to be 1) implies that

\begin{split}\|\Psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(X_{\bullet})}&\leq 4\cdot(\text{RHS of
(\ref {eq:L^2_1}) (b)})+|X_{\bullet}|+\frac{1}{2}\int_{X_{\bullet}}|\mu_{r}|\\
&\leq\zeta_{3}r\,\big(|X_{\bullet,m}|+\textsc{e}+\ln r\big)+\zeta_{3}^{\prime}|X_{\bullet}|,\end{split}

where
\zeta_{3},\zeta_{3}^{\prime}
only on the parameters listed in
(
4.15
). Together with the preceding inequality, we arrive
at (
4.51
).

\Box

Remark 4.14
.

The gauge transformation
u_{r}
in (
4.51
) depends on
X_{\bullet}
. Specifically, it is
determined, via Equations (5.2) and (5.3) in
[
KM
]
, by
A_{0}
together with a choice of
\{q_{1},q_{2},\ldots,q_{b^{1}(X_{\bullet})}\}
, where each
q_{k}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,b^{1}(X_{\bullet})\}
is
a closed 3-form supported in a compact set in the interior of

X_{\bullet}
and
\{[q_{k}]\}_{k}\subset H^{3}(X_{\bullet},\partial X_{\bullet};{\mathbb{R}})
forms a basis of
H^{3}(X_{\bullet},\partial X_{\bullet};{\mathbb{Z}})/\text{Tors}
. As in
[
KM
]
, we say that a
(A^{\prime}_{r},\Psi^{\prime}_{r})=u_{r}\cdot(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
or
A^{\prime}_{r}=u_{r}\cdot A_{r}
is in a
Coulomb-Neumann
gauge
(with respect to
A_{0}
) if it satisfies Equation (5.2) of

[
KM
]
. We say that it is in the
normalized Coulomb-Neumann
gauge
(with respect to
A_{0}
and
\{q_{k}\}_{k=1}^{b^{1}(X_{\bullet})}
) if it
satisfies both Equations (5.2) and (5.3) in
[
KM
]
. Note that if

(A^{\prime}_{r},\Psi_{r}^{\prime})
is in a Coulomb-Neumann gauge with respect to

A_{0}
, then
(B^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})=(A^{\prime}_{r},\Psi_{r}^{\prime})|_{\partial X_{\bullet}}

is in a Coulomb gauge with respect to
B_{0}=A_{0}|_{\partial X_{\bullet}}
.
In the case
when
X_{\bullet}
is of the form
\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}
, for some

Y=Y_{i}
,
H^{3}(X_{\bullet},\partial X_{\bullet};{\mathbb{Z}})\simeq H^{2}(Y;{\mathbb{Z}})
, and we choose
\{q_{k}\}_{k}
so
that
q_{k}=d(\chi_{+}(s)*_{3}h_{k})
, with
h_{k}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,b^{1}(Y)\}
being the harmonic
1-forms on
Y
appearing in (
3.10
), and
\chi_{+}\colon\thinspace[l,L]\to[0,1]
is a smooth non-decreasing function that is 0 on a
neighborhood of
\{l\}\subset[l,L]
and is 1 on a neighborhood of

\{L\}\subset[l,L]
. If
(A_{r}^{\prime},\Psi_{r}^{\prime})
is in the normalized
Coulomb-Neumann gauge with respect to such
\{q_{k}\}_{k}
(and

A_{0}
), then
(A_{r}^{\prime},\Psi_{r}^{\prime})|_{\hat{Y}_{:L}}
is in the
normalized Coulomb gauge with respect to
\{h_{k}\}_{k}
(and

B_{0}=A_{0}|_{\hat{Y}_{:L}}
).

4.7
Some integral estimates

This subsection contains some integral estimates which will be
instrumental to the pointwise estimates in next section.

Lemma 4.15
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
and recall from (
3.3
) that
A_{0}=\hat{B}_{0,i}
on the

\hat{Y}_{i}
-end. Fix
r\geq r_{0}
and
L_{i}\geq 1
. Let
(B,\Phi)
denote
the restriction of
(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
to
Y_{i:L_{i}}\subset X
and
let
(B^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})
be the representative of the gauge equivalence
class
[(B,\Phi)]
in the normalized Coulomb gauge. Let

\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}|(B,\Phi)
denote the restriction of
\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}(A,\Psi)
to
Y_{i:L_{i}}\subset X
.
Then there exist positive constants
\zeta^{\prime},\zeta_{0},\zeta_{w},\zeta^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}
such that

\begin{split}{\rm(1)}&\,\|(B^{\prime}-B_{0,i},\Phi)\|_{L^{2}_{1/2,B}(Y_{i:L_{i}})}^{2}\leq\zeta^{\prime}r\,(\textsc{e}+\ln r);\\
{\rm(2)}&\,|\operatorname{CSD}^{Y_{i}}_{0}(B^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})|\leq\zeta_{0}\,r\,(\textsc{e}+\ln r);\\
&\,|\operatorname{CSD}^{Y_{i}}_{w_{r}}(B^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})|=|\operatorname{CSD}^{Y_{i}}_{w_{r}}(B,\Phi)|\leq\zeta_{w}\,r\,(\textsc{e}+\ln r);\\
{\rm(3)}&\,|\,f_{\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}|}(B,\Phi)\,|\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}\,r\,(\textsc{e}+\ln r)\end{split}

(4.52)

The positive
constants
\zeta^{\prime},\zeta_{0},\zeta_{w},\zeta^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}
depend only on the parameters listed in
(
4.15
), together with
B_{0,i}
and the constants
\textsl{z}_{i}
in
Lemma
3.11
. As before, the factors of

\ln r
in (
4.52
) can be replaced by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)

if the assumption of Lemma
4.11

holds.

Proof.

(1)
: Let
(A^{\prime}_{r},\Psi^{\prime}_{r})=u_{r}\cdot(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
be in
the normalize Coulomb-Neumann gauge on
\hat{Y}_{i,[L_{i}-1,L_{i}]}
, with respect to the choices specified in Remark

4.14
. As remarked above, with such choices,
(A^{\prime}_{r},\Psi^{\prime}_{r})|_{Y_{i:L_{i}}}=(B^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})
. Note that

\|(B^{\prime}-B_{0,i},\Phi)\|_{L^{2}_{1/2,B}(Y_{i:L_{i}})}\leq\zeta_{b}\|(A_{r}^{\prime}-A_{0},\Psi^{\prime}_{r})\|_{L^{2}_{1,A}(\hat{Y}_{i,[L_{i}-1,L_{i}]})},

then appeal to Proposition
4.13
.

(2)
:
This is a
direct consequence of
(1)
above.

(3)
: Recall the proof of Lemma
3.8
and the notation therein. From the first
inequality in (
3.12
), (
3.4
) and
(1)
above, we have:

\begin{split}|f_{\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}|}(B,\Phi)|&\leq\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}\|(\delta B,\Phi)\|_{L^{2}}(\|\Phi_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+1)\quad\text{for a certain $t\in[0,1]$}\\
&\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}\,r\,(\textsc{e}+\ln r),\end{split}

where
\delta B:=B^{\prime}-B_{0,i}
.
\Box

Lemma 4.16
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
.
Let
r_{0}
,
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
be as in Lemma
4.11
. Fix
r\geq r_{0}
.
Then there are positive constants
\zeta
,
\zeta_{h}
independent of
r
,
and
X_{\bullet}
such that

\Big|\int_{X_{\bullet}}iF_{A_{r}}\wedge\omega\Big|\leq\zeta_{h}\,(\textsc{e}+\ln r)+\zeta\,|X_{\bullet,m}|\quad\text{for compact
$X_{\bullet}\subset X$}

(4.53)

The constants
\zeta,\zeta_{h}
above only depend on the parameters listed in (
4.15
).

Moreover, if the assumption (
4.48
) in Lemma
4.11
holds, then
the statement above holds with
the factor of
\ln r
replaced by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
.

Proof.
Write

\begin{split}&\int_{X_{\bullet}}iF_{A}\wedge\omega\\
&\qquad\quad=i\int_{X_{\bullet}}F_{A}\wedge*\nu+i\int_{X_{\bullet}}F_{A}\wedge\nu\\
&\qquad\quad=i\int_{X_{\bullet}}F_{A_{0}}\wedge*\nu+4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)-4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi).\\
&\qquad\quad=i\int_{X_{\bullet}}F_{A_{0}}\wedge*\nu+4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)-4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi).\\
\end{split}

(4.54)

Consider the second equality of (
4.54
) in the case of
(
4.13
)(a), the second equality in the case of
(
4.13
) (b).
We bound the right hand side of these formulas term by term. The
first term on the right hand side in both cases is
bounded via (
4.22
) as:

\Big|i\int_{X_{\bullet}}F_{A_{0}}\wedge*\nu\Big|\leq\zeta_{1}\,|X_{\bullet,m}|+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}

For the second term, use (
4.14
)
to obtain:

\begin{cases}\big|4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\big|\leq 4\textsc{e}+\zeta_{2}\ln r&\text{assuming
(\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (a)};\\
\big|4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\big|\leq 4\textsc{e}+\zeta^{\prime}_{2}\ln r&\text{assuming
(\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (b)}.\\
\end{cases}

To bound the last term,
note that by our
assumptions on
A_{0}
and
w_{r}
,

\begin{split}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)&=\frac{1}{4}\int_{X_{\bullet}}F_{A_{0}}\wedge(F_{A_{0}}+iw_{r})-2\,\operatorname{CSD}^{\partial X_{\bullet}}_{w_{r}}(B,\Phi)\\
&=\frac{1}{4}\int_{X_{\bullet}\cap X_{c}}F_{A_{0}}\wedge(F_{A_{0}}+iw_{r})-2\,\operatorname{CSD}^{\partial X_{\bullet}}_{w_{r}}(B,\Phi);\\
{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)&=\frac{1}{4}\int_{X_{\bullet}\cap X_{c}}F_{A_{0}}\wedge(F_{A_{0}}+iw_{r})-2\,\operatorname{CSD}^{\partial X_{\bullet}}_{w_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}|}(B,\Phi).\end{split}

Combined with (
4.52
) (2) and (3), this gives

\begin{split}4r^{-1}\big|{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\big|&\leq\zeta_{3}\,(\textsc{e}+\ln r)\qquad\text{assuming
(\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (a)};\\
4r^{-1}\big|{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\big|&\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{3}\,(\textsc{e}+\ln r)\qquad\text{assuming
(\ref {assume:EtopX-ubdd}) (b)}.\end{split}

(4.55)

Plugging all these back to (
4.54
), we have the asserted
inequality (
4.53
).

The assertion regarding the general case assuming the
condition of Lemma
4.11
follows from the same argument, with
the role of Lemma
4.6
above played by Lemma

4.11
instead.

\Box

The next lemma is an analog of
[
Ts
]
’s Lemma 3.1. Let
\psi:=(r/2)^{-1/2}\Psi
.

Lemma 4.17
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6

and let
X_{\bullet}\subset X
be arbitrary. Then
there exists a positive constant
\zeta
independent of
r
and

\bullet
, such that

\displaystyle r\int_{X_{\bullet}}(|\nu|-|\psi|^{2})^{2}\leq\zeta(\textsc{e}+\ln r+|X_{\bullet,m}|).

If in addition,
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{\prime\prime}
, then

r\int_{X_{\bullet}}|\nu|\Big||\nu|-|\psi|^{2}\Big|\leq\zeta(\textsc{e}+\ln r+|X_{\bullet}|)

for a positive constant
\zeta^{\prime}
independent of
r
and

\bullet
. The constants
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}
above only depend on the parameters listed in (
4.15
).
Moreover, the factor of

\ln r
above can be replaced by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
if the assumption of Lemma
4.11

holds.

Proof.
Using the first line of the Seiberg-Witten equation, the fact that
\omega
is
self-dual, our assumption on
\mu_{r}
, and (
4.53
), one has:

\begin{split}\frac{r}{2}\int_{X_{\bullet}}|\nu|(|\nu|-|\psi|^{2}-\zeta^{\prime}r^{-1})&\leq\int_{X_{\bullet}}iF_{A}\wedge\omega\\
&\leq\zeta_{h}(\textsc{e}+\ln r)+\zeta|X_{\bullet,m}|).\end{split}

(4.56)

Meanwhile, arguing as Equation (3.4) of
[
Ts
]
, one has:

\begin{split}2^{-1}d^{*}d|\psi|^{2}+|\nabla_{A}\psi|^{2}+4^{-1}r|\psi|^{2}(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|)\leq\zeta_{2}|\psi|^{2}.\end{split}

(4.57)

Hence, with
u:=|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|
,

2^{-1}d^{*}d|\psi|^{2}+4^{-1}r|\nu|u+4^{-1}ru^{2}\leq\zeta_{2}|\psi|^{2}.

Integrating this and using (
4.56
) and Proposition
4.13
, one has

\begin{split}r\int_{X_{\bullet}}u^{2}&\leq\zeta_{4}\,(|X_{\bullet,m}|+\ln r)+2\Big|\int_{\partial X_{\bullet}}\partial_{s}|\psi|^{2}\Big|.\\
\end{split}

Meanwhile, using the Seiberg-Witten equation and item (1) of
(
4.52
),

\Big|\int_{\partial X_{\bullet}}\partial_{s}|\psi|^{2}\Big|\leq\zeta_{4}\,\|\psi\|_{L^{2}_{1/2}(\partial X_{\bullet})}^{2}\leq\zeta_{5}\,\ln r.

These together imply the first inequality asserted in the Lemma.

To derive the second inequality in the lemma, follow Taubes’ argument in

[
Ts
]
. The harmonicity of
\nu
implies that over
X^{\prime\prime}
,

-\zeta|\nu|\leq d^{*}d|\nu|+|\nu|^{-1}|\nabla\nu|^{2}\leq\zeta|\nu|,

(4.58)

and the analog of Equation (3.9) in
[
Ts
]
reads:

\begin{split}r\int_{X_{\bullet}}|\nu|u_{+}&\leq\zeta\int_{X_{\bullet}}(|\nu|^{-1}|\nabla\nu|^{2}+\zeta)+\zeta^{\prime}\Big|\int_{\partial X_{\bullet}}\partial_{s}u\Big|\\
&\leq\zeta^{\prime\prime}\Big(|X_{\bullet}|+\Big|\int_{\partial X_{\bullet}}\partial_{s}|\psi|^{2}\Big|+\Big|\int_{\partial X_{\bullet}}\partial_{s}|\nu|\Big|\Big)\\
&\leq\zeta_{3}\,(|X_{\bullet}|+\ln r),\end{split}

where
u_{+}:=\max\,(u,0)
. Combine this with (
4.56
).

The assertion regarding the general case assuming the
condition of Lemma
4.11
follows from the same
argument.
\Box

5
A priori pointwise estimates

This section consists largely of refinements and extension of the
pointwise estimates in Section 3 of
[
Ts
]
, which is in turn based
on Section I.2 in
[
T
]
. Familiarity with
these references is assumed. We begin with some preliminaries.

First, note that by assumption, there exist positive constants
\textsl{z}_{v}\geq 1
,

\textsl{z}_{v}^{\prime}\geq 1
that depends only on
\nu
, such that

\begin{split}(\textsl{z}^{\prime}_{v})^{-1}\leq&\inf_{x\in X^{\prime\prime}\cap\nu^{-1}(0)}|\nabla\nu|\leq\sup_{x\in X^{\prime\prime}\cap\nu^{-1}(0)}|\nabla\nu|\leq\textsl{z}^{\prime}_{v};\\
&\textsl{z}^{-1}_{v}\tilde{\sigma}\leq|\nu|\leq\textsl{z}_{v}\,\tilde{\sigma},\quad\text{
over $X^{\prime\prime}$, }\end{split}

(5.1)

where
\tilde{\sigma}
is a function on
X^{\prime\prime}
defined as follows:
Suppose that
|\nu|^{-1}(0)\neq\emptyset
. Let
\sigma(\cdot)
denote the distance function to

\nu^{-1}(0)
on
X^{\prime\prime}
, and set

\tilde{\sigma}:=\chi(\sigma)\,\sigma+(1-\chi(\sigma)).

When
\nu^{-1}(0)=\emptyset
, let
\sigma=\infty
and

\tilde{\sigma}=1
.

Let
\gamma_{a}
be a smooth
cutoff function on
X
that equals
1
on
X^{{}^{\prime}a}
and agrees
with
\chi(\text{{\small s}}_{j}-a+1)
over each vanishing end
j\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}
.

Recall also that
Z^{{}^{\prime}a}:=X^{{}^{\prime}a}\,\cap\nu^{-1}(0)
. Given

\delta>0
,

X_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}:=\{x\,|\,x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a},\sigma(x)\geq\delta\}
, and
Z_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}:=X^{{}^{\prime}a}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
.

In the case when
a=0
(resp.
a=10
), the spaces
Z^{{}^{\prime}a}
,

Z_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}
,
X_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}
introduced above are
alternatively denoted by
Z^{\prime}
,

Z^{\prime}_{\delta}
,
X^{\prime}_{\delta}
(resp.
Z^{\prime\prime}
,

Z_{\delta}^{\prime\prime}
,
X_{\delta}^{\prime\prime}
). We use
{\mathscr{X}}^{a}_{\delta}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
to denote a manifold with smooth boundary obtained by
“rounding corners”. In particular,
\partial{\mathscr{X}}^{a}_{\delta}
is smooth
and
\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\partial{\mathscr{X}}^{a}_{\delta},\partial X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta})\ll 2^{-8}
. Let

\gamma_{a,\delta}
be a smooth cutoff function on
X
that is
supported on
{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta}
and equals 1 on

X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta}
, such that
\|\gamma_{a,\delta}-\gamma_{a}\,(1-\chi(\sigma/\delta))\|_{C^{2}}\ll 2^{-8}
.

Remarks 5.1
.

(1) As in
[
Ts
]
, the pointwise estimates provided in this section
typically
hold over domains of the form
X_{\delta}
(or more generally,

X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
). They are
\delta
-dependent, and constants appearing
the relevant inequalities usually depend on
\delta
. In
[
Ts
]
, the dependence
of the constants on
\delta
are left unspecified. For our
purpose, this dependence is important and therefore shall be made explicit
below. As mentioned in Section
1.4
, in what follows the
notation
\zeta
and its decorated variants such as
\zeta^{\prime}
,

\zeta_{i}
, are reserved for constants
independent of
\delta
(and also independent of
r
and
(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
).

(2) Typically, we improve the pointwise estimates in Section 3 of
[
Ts
]
by replacing
factors of constants
\delta^{-1}
therein by the function

\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\leq\delta^{-1}
. This is often made possible with the
help of the following observation: Given
{\rm m}>0
, there are
constants
\zeta_{\rm m}
,
\zeta_{\rm m}^{\prime}
depending only on
\nu
, the
metric, and
{\rm m}
, such that

d^{*}d(\tilde{\sigma}^{-k})+\frac{r|\nu|}{{\rm m}}(\tilde{\sigma}^{-k})\geq\zeta_{\rm m}\,(-\zeta^{\prime}_{\rm m}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-k-2}+r\tilde{\sigma}^{-k+1})>\zeta_{\rm m}\,r\tilde{\sigma}^{-k+1}/2

(5.2)

over
X^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}
when
r\delta^{3}>\zeta^{\prime}_{\rm m}
. (In what follows,
{\rm m}
is typically
taken to be
2^{k}
,
k=1,2,3,4
.) This enables one to replace

\delta^{-k}
by
\tilde{\sigma}^{-k}
as comparison functions in various
comparison principle arguments.

The pointwise estimates in this section are made simpler thanks to the
next lemma, which motivated the introduction of Item (5) in Assumption
3.4
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
(X,\nu)
be an admissible pair, and let
w_{r}

and
\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{r}
respectively be a
r
-parametrized family of
closed 2-forms and nonlocal
perturbations on
X
satisfying Assumption

3.4
. Let
\mu_{r}=r\nu+w_{r}
as before, and let
(A,\Psi)=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
be as in Lemma

4.6
. Then for any given
\textsc{e}>0
, there is a constant

r_{\textsc{e}}\geq 8
such that

\|\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{r}\,(A,\Psi)\|_{C^{k}_{A}(X)}\leq\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}

(5.3)

for all
r\geq r_{\textsc{e}}
and admissible solutions
(A,\Psi)=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
to the Seiberg-Witten equation

{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{r}}(A,\Psi)=0
satisfying the energy
bound (
4.13
).

Proof.
Invoke Assumption
3.4
(5). If
X
is cylindrical, then

{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}=\emptyset
by our assumption on
X
, and so

\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{r}=\hat{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{r}\equiv 0
. When
X
is non-cylindrical,
\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}(A,\Psi)
is
supported on
\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}}\hat{Y}_{i,[{\mathfrak{l}}_{i},{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}^{\prime}]}
,
it suffices to examine it on each
\hat{Y}_{i,[{\mathfrak{l}}_{i},{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}^{\prime}]}
. In the present setting,
\upsilon(r)=\zeta r
. Meanwhile, applying Proposition
4.12
to
X_{\bullet}=\hat{Y}_{i,[{\mathfrak{l}}_{i},{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}^{\prime}]}
, we see that there is an
r_{\textsc{e}}\geq\max(2^{8}\textsc{e},8)
such
that Conditions (i) and (ii) in Assumption
3.4

(5) holds for
(A,\Psi)=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
when
r\geq r_{\textsc{e}}
. The assertion (
5.3
) follows directly
from Assumption
3.4
(5) and the properties of

\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{r}
and
w_{r}
prescribed in items (4) and (1) in
Assumption
3.4
.

\Box

We shall apply he preceding lemma to those
(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
from the statement of Theorem

1.8
, with the constant
e
taken to be that
given by (
4.8
).
The value of each occurrence of
r_{0}

in the rest of this article will be taken to be larger or equal to all
its predecessors and the version of

r_{\textsc{e}}
corresponding to this value of
e
.

Throughout the rest of this section, we tacitly invoke
the bound (
5.3
) to omit terms arising from the
nonlocal perturbation
\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}
by adjusting the coefficients in
the inequalities.

Write
\Psi=(r/2)^{1/2}\psi
, and write
\psi=(\alpha,\beta)
with
respect to the decomposition
{\mathbb{S}}^{+}\simeq E\oplus E\otimes K^{-1}
over
X-\nu^{-1}(0)
. Throughout this section,
(A,\Psi)=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
is an admissible solution to the Seiberg-Witten equation

{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(A_{r},\Psi_{r})=0
satisfying the assumptions
of Lemma

.

5.1
Estimates for
|\psi|^{2}

With our assumption on
\nu
,
w_{r}
, and
\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}
, an

L^{\infty}
-estimate on
\psi
over
X
may be established easily.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
(A,\Psi)
,
\psi
be as described immediately preceding this
subsection. Then

\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(X)}\leq\zeta_{\infty}\quad\text{over $X$},

(5.4)

where
\zeta_{\infty}
is a positive constant depending only on
\sup_{X}R_{g}
,
\sup_{X}|\nu|
, and the constants
\varsigma_{w}
,

\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}
from Assumption
3.4
.

Proof.
We argue as in the
proof of the Morse-end case of Lemma

3.9
.

By the first line of the Seiberg-Witten equation

{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(A,\Psi)=0
, one has

\langle\psi,\bar{\mbox{\mbox{$\partial\mkern-7.5mu\mbox{/}\mkern 2.0mu$}}}^{-}_{A}\bar{\mbox{\mbox{$\partial\mkern-7.5mu\mbox{/}\mkern 2.0mu$}}}^{+}_{A}\psi\rangle=-r^{-1/2}\langle\psi,\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}(A,\Psi)\rangle.

It then follows from the Weitzenböck formula, the
second line of the Seiberg-Witten equation and (
5.3
) that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}d^{*}d|\psi|^{2}&+|\nabla_{A}\psi|^{2}+\frac{r}{4}|\psi|^{2}(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu+r^{-1}w_{r}|)+\frac{R_{g}}{4}|\psi|^{2}\\
&\leq\zeta_{1}|\psi|^{2}+\zeta_{2}\,r^{-1},\end{split}

(5.5)

where
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{2}
are positive constants depending only
on
\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
The smooth function
|\psi|^{2}
must have a maximum at a certain
point
x_{M}\in X
, or it is
bounded by
r^{-1}\|\Phi_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(Y_{i})}
for certain
i
, where
(B_{i},\Phi_{i})
is the
Y_{i}
-end limit of
(A,\Psi)
. In the former
case, consider the previous inequality at
x_{M}
and rearranging to
get

|\psi(x_{M})|^{2}\big(|\psi(x_{M})|^{2}-|\nu(x_{M})|^{2}\big)\leq\zeta_{3}|\psi(x_{M})|^{2}+\zeta_{4},

where
\zeta_{3}
,
\zeta_{4}
are positive constants depending only
on
\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\varsigma_{w}
, and
\sup_{X}R_{g}
.
Hence,
|\psi(x_{M})|^{2}\leq\zeta_{5}
for a positive constant
\zeta_{5}
depending only
on
\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\varsigma_{w}
, and
\sup_{X}R_{g}
, and
\sup_{X}|\nu|
. In the latter case, invoke
Lemma
3.9
. Either way, Equation (
5.4
) holds.

\Box

Over
X^{{}^{\prime}9}
, a better pointwise bound on
|\psi|^{2}
may be obtained from the

bound in Lemma

.

.

\displaystyle\leq|\nu|+\zeta r^{-1/3};

\displaystyle(b)

\displaystyle|\psi|^{2}

(5.6)

Proof.
This is an analog of Lemma 3.2 of
[
Ts
]
3
3

3

of

]
contains some errors/typos which are easy to

.

From Equation (

\frac{1}{2}d^{*}d|\psi|^{2}+|\nabla_{A}\psi|^{2}+\frac{r}{4}|\psi|^{2}(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|)\leq\zeta_{0},

We may find positive constants

]

over the region

arguments
over this region as in the proof of (

on the boundary of this region.

When

in this

\sigma

]
.
4
4

4

(5.14)

Fix an

&\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-2}\int_{A_{\tilde{\sigma}_{x}}}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,y)^{-2}\exp\big(-\zeta_{2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,y)(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x})^{1/2}\big)\Big)dy\\
&\leq\zeta_{4}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{2}\exp\big(-\zeta^{\prime}_{2}(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{3})^{1/2}\big)\leq\zeta_{5}r^{-2}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-4}\end{split}

when

.
Combining this with the bound

when

5.3
Estimating

Proposition 5.6
.

\text{(ii)}\quad|F_{A}^{-}|&\leq\zeta_{1}r\delta+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\delta^{-2}\ln\,(\delta/\sigma)\quad\text{over $Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$ for any $\delta\geq\delta_{0}$},\\
\end{split}

Fix

,

. Let

.
Multiplying both sides of the preceding differential inequality by

on a ball of radius

\begin{split}q(x)&\leq c_{0}\,\rho_{0}^{-4}\int_{B(x,2\rho_{0})-B(x,\rho_{0})}q_{+}+c_{1}\int_{B(x,2\rho_{0})}\xi\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2}\\

Now suppose

,

&\leq\zeta^{\prime}\varepsilon^{-1}r\delta_{0}^{2}\,\sigma_{x}^{-1}\quad\text{when $x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta}$}.\end{split}

To bound the third integral in (

when

.

(5.33)

Put together, we have for

\delta_{2}>0
, and devide

B(x,2\rho_{0})\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta}
into two regions:
\sigma\geq\delta_{2}
on the
first region, and
\sigma\leq\delta_{2}
on the second. (The second
region is empty when
\delta_{2}\leq 2\delta
.)
Over the first region, use Lemma
4.17
together with the
observation that
\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(y,x)\geq\sigma_{y}/2\geq\delta_{2}/2
for any
y
in
this region.
Over the second region, use the fact that
by Proposition
5.4
,

\eta_{2}\leq\zeta r\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}
over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta}
.
Then, setting
\rho_{2}=r^{-1/7}\delta^{1/7}(\ln r+\textsc{e})^{1/7}
, one has:

\begin{split}&\int_{B(x,2\rho_{0})}\eta_{2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2}\\
&\qquad\leq\zeta^{\prime}\,\delta_{2}^{-2}\delta_{2}^{-3}(\ln r+\textsc{e})+\zeta_{1}\int_{2\delta}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{-2\rho_{0}}^{2\rho_{0}}\frac{r\sigma_{y}\,dz\,d\sigma_{y}}{(\sigma_{y}-\sigma_{x})^{2}+z^{2}}\,\\
&\qquad\leq\zeta^{\prime}\,\delta_{2}^{-5}(\ln r+\textsc{e})+\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\delta^{-1}\int_{2\delta}^{\delta_{2}}r\sigma_{y}\,d\sigma_{y}\\
&\qquad\leq\zeta^{\prime}\,\delta_{2}^{-5}(\ln r+\textsc{e})+\zeta r\,\delta^{-1}\delta_{2}^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq\zeta_{4}\,r^{5/7}\delta^{2/7}(\ln r+\textsc{e})^{2/7}\leq\zeta_{4}^{\prime}r\delta\quad\text{when $x\in Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$}.\end{split}

Gathering all the termwise bounds obtained,
we have:

q_{\delta}\leq\zeta\,r\delta+\zeta^{\prime}\delta^{-2}\ln\,(\delta/\sigma)\quad\text{over $Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$},

where
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
are positive constants independent of

r
and
\delta
. Hence, by Proposition
5.4

\textsc{s}=q_{\delta}+c_{1}r\,u-\zeta_{2}r|\beta|^{2}\leq\zeta_{1}r\delta+\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\delta^{-2}\ln\,(\delta/\sigma)\quad\text{over $Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$, }

as asserted by
the second inequality in (
5.19
).

Having established Item (b) of the assertions of the proposition, item
(c) follows directly. Given
x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}}
, set the parameter

\delta
in (
5.19
) (ii) as
\delta=\sigma_{x}
, and
observe that over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}}
,
r^{2/3}\leq\textsc{o}^{-1}r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}
,
one arrives at the first inequality in Item (c). The second inequality
asserted in Item (c) follows from a combination of the first
inequality and (
5.19
) (ii).

Lemma 5.8
.

on

Proof.
From (

(2^{-1}d^{*}d+\frac{r}{4}|\psi|^{2})(-u)\geq-\zeta_{0}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\quad\text{over $X^{\prime\prime}$}.

\zeta^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-k+1}&\text{on $X^{*}_{\delta}$.}\end{cases}\\
\end{split}

(5.37)

Now take

.

Proof of Proposition
5.7
.

&\leq\zeta^{\prime\prime}_{4}\,r\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}(-u)+\zeta^{\prime\prime}_{1}r\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\quad\text{on $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$.}\end{split}

(5.40)

Given

, let

independent

, such that the function

&\quad q^{\prime}\leq 0\quad\text{over $\hat{Y}_{i,L}$, $\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$; }\\
&\quad q^{\prime}\leq\zeta K^{\prime}_{1,\delta}\quad\text{ over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$},\quad 0\leq a\leq\frac{17}{2},\quad\delta\geq\delta_{0}.\end{split}

(5.42)

(The number

(5.43)

(q^{\prime}_{\delta})_{+}
is supported within the compact space
U:={\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta}-\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\hat{Y}_{i,L}\subset X^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}

on

Let

be a solution to the following

\big(\frac{d^{*}d}{2}+\frac{r|\nu|}{4}\big)\,q_{1}=\xi^{\prime}_{\delta}\,\,\,\text{ over $V$};\qquad q_{1}|_{\partial V}=0.

The (Dirichlet) Green’s function for

, denoted

below, satisfies

|G_{r}(x,\cdot)|+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)\,|dG_{r}(x,\cdot)|\leq\zeta_{g}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)}

for certain constants
\zeta_{g}^{\prime}
,
\zeta_{g}
. Thus,

\begin{split}&|q_{1}(x)|\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\zeta_{1}\int_{U}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)}|\xi^{\prime}|\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\,\,+\zeta_{2}\int_{X^{{}^{\prime}a+1}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}}K^{\prime}_{1,\delta}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-3}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\,\,+\int_{(Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta})\cap U}K^{\prime}_{1,\delta}\big(\zeta_{3}\,\delta^{-2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2}+\zeta^{\prime}_{3}\,\delta^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-3}\big)e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\delta^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}(x)+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\,K^{\prime}_{1,\delta}(x)\,(r\delta)^{-1/2}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\,X^{{}^{\prime}a+1}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta})}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\zeta_{3}^{\prime\prime}K^{\prime}_{1,\delta}(x)\,(r\delta^{3})^{-1/2}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\,Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta})}.\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\delta^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}(x)+\zeta_{4}\,K^{\prime}_{1,\delta}(x)\,(r\delta^{3})^{-1/2},\end{split}

where the terms in the second and third lines above are obtained via

r_{0}>8
,

\zeta_{O}
,
\zeta^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and ,
\zeta_{4}^{\prime}

, and

Assume

from now on. Then the preceding estimates for

and

,

Choose

to find positive constants

It follows from the preceding three inequalities that

over

\begin{split}q_{0}&\leq\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\delta^{-1}\tilde{r}^{\epsilon}K_{1,\delta}\,(-u+\zeta r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2})+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\delta^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\\

over

,
and

. Given

(5.44)
