\tilde{r}_{x}=\tilde{r}(x)=r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}
, and note that

1/2\leq\tilde{\sigma}/\tilde{\sigma}_{x}\leq 3/2\quad\text{over $B\,(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/2)$. }

(5.45)

Fix
x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta_{0}^{\prime}}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{13}{2}
. Use (
5.44
), (
5.13
),
(
5.14
), Proposition
5.5
and (
5.45
) to find constants
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
,
\zeta^{\prime\prime}
,
\zeta_{1}
that are independent of
r
,
x
,
and
(A,\Psi)
, such that

\begin{split}&\quad d^{*}dy_{x}+4^{-1}r|\nu|y_{x}\leq 0\quad\text{over $B\,(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/2)$, with }\\
&y_{x}:=\max\,\big(|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}+\zeta r\tilde{\sigma}^{2}_{x}|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}-\zeta^{\prime}r\varpi+\zeta^{\prime\prime}r^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{3}_{x}|\beta|^{2}-\zeta_{1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2},0\big).\end{split}

(5.46)

Note that by Propositions
5.4
and
5.5
and (
5.45
),

y_{x}\leq\zeta_{2}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}|\nabla_{A}\alpha|^{2}+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}r\tilde{\sigma}^{2}|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}+\zeta_{3}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}r\varpi.

(5.47)

Multiply both sides of the differential inequality (
5.46
) by
the function

\chi(4\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)/\tilde{\sigma}_{x})\,G_{r}
,
where
G_{r}
denotes the
Green’s function of
d^{*}d+4^{-1}r|\nu|
on
B\,(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/2)
with Dirichlet
boundary condition, then integrate over
B\,(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/2)
. This
Green’s function satisfies a bound of the form:

\Big|G_{r}(x,x^{\prime})\Big|+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,x^{\prime})\,\Big|\,dG_{r}(x,x^{\prime})\Big|\leq\zeta_{0}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,x^{\prime})^{-2}\,e^{-2\zeta^{\prime}_{0}\tilde{r}_{x}^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,x^{\prime})}.

It follows from integration by parts that

\begin{split}y_{x}(x)&\leq\zeta_{3}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-4}e^{-\zeta^{\prime}_{0}\tilde{r}_{x}^{1/2}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/2}\int_{A_{x}}y_{x},\\
\quad\end{split}

(5.48)

where
A_{x}=B(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/2)-B(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/4)
.

By (
5.47
), (
5.45
), Propositions
4.12
,

5.5
,
and Lemma
4.17
,

\int_{A_{x}}y_{x}\leq\zeta^{\prime}\,\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1}\,(\ln r+\textsc{e})+\zeta r\tilde{\sigma}^{2}_{x}\int_{A_{x}}|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}.

(5.49)

To estimate the last term above, multiply both sides of (
5.13
) by
\chi\big(2\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)/\tilde{\sigma}_{x}\big)
, then
integrate over
B(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x})
. This gives

\begin{split}\int_{A_{x}}|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}&\leq\zeta_{4}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-2}\int_{B\,(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x})}|\beta|^{2}+\zeta_{4}^{\prime}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-3}\int_{B\,(x,\tilde{\sigma}_{x})}|\nabla_{A}\alpha|^{2}+\zeta_{4}^{\prime\prime}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{3}\\
&\leq\zeta_{5}\,r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-3}(\ln r+\textsc{e}).\end{split}

In the above, (
5.45
), Propositions
5.5
,

4.12
,
and Lemma
4.17
are used to derive the second line from the
first line. Inserting the preceding inequality into
(
5.49
) and combining with (
5.48
),
we have

y_{x}(x)\leq\zeta_{6}\,\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-5}e^{-\zeta_{6}^{\prime}(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{3})^{1/2}}(\ln r+\textsc{e}),

where
\zeta_{6}
,
\zeta_{6}^{\prime}
are independent of
r
,
x
, and
(A,\Psi)
.
This implies the existence of constants
r_{1}
,
\zeta_{7}
,
\zeta_{7}^{\prime}
,
also independent of
r
,
x
, and
(A,\Psi)
,
such that
y_{x}(x)\leq\zeta_{7}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}_{x}
when
r\geq r_{1}
and
\sigma_{x}\leq\zeta_{7}\,r^{-1/3}(\ln r)^{2/3}
. Rename
\zeta_{O}

to be the larger of this
\zeta_{7}
and twice of the version from Proposition

5.7
, and re-set
\delta_{0}^{\prime}=\zeta_{O}\,r^{-1/3}(\ln r)^{2/3}
.
\Box

Remark 5.10
.

It is basically equivalent to estimate either of

|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|
and
|\nabla_{A}\alpha|
, as they
are related by

\Big||\nabla_{A}\alpha|^{2}-|\nu|\,|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\Big|\leq\zeta\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\quad\text{on
$X_{\delta_{0}}^{{}^{\prime}a}\supset X_{\delta_{0}^{\prime}}^{{}^{\prime}a}$}.

In fact, a slight modification of the argument in the preceding proof
would yield a direct, similar pointwise bound for

|\nabla_{A}\alpha|
. However, the bound for
|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|

given in the previous proposition is slightly better, and it is more
amenable to our applications later.

6
Monotonicity and its consequences

This section contains variants and counterparts of Steps (3) and (4) of
Taubes’ proof, as outlined in
Section
3.6
above. Intermediate steps of these
arguments are used to improve various integral bounds and pointwise
estimates in the previous two sections.

Let
(A,\Psi)=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
be as in the statement of Lemma

4.6
, and let
B\subset X
be an open set. Following Taubes, we define the
(local)
energy
of
(A,\Psi)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Conn}}\nolimits({\mathbb{S}}^{+})\times\Gamma({\mathbb{S}}^{+})
on
B
to
be:

\mathcal{W}_{B}(A,\Psi):=4^{-1}r\int_{B}|\nu|\,\Big||\nu|-|\psi|^{2}\Big|.

Taubes’ proof of the monotonicity formula hinges on a bound for

\mathcal{W}_{X}
. In contrast to
[
Ts
]
,
\mathcal{W}_{X}
can be infinite in our
situation. However, Lemma
4.17
provides us with bounds on

\mathcal{W}_{X_{\bullet}}
for any compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{\prime}
.

6.1
Bounding
\mathcal{W}_{Z_{\delta}}

Recall the notation

\tilde{r}=r\tilde{\sigma}
.

Let
\delta_{1}:=2r^{-1/6}(\ln r+\textsc{e})^{3/4}
. Over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{1}/2}
,
\tilde{\sigma}^{-5}\leq\tilde{r}\,(\ln r+\textsc{e})^{-9/2}
. This ensures that there is an
r
-independent
constant
\zeta_{e}
such that the functions
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\zeta\varepsilon_{1}
from Proposition

5.7
have the property that

(\ln r+\textsc{e})\,(\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon^{\prime})\leq\zeta_{e}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{1}/2}$}.

(6.1)

Fix an
x\in\nu^{-1}(0)\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}
. Choose local coordinates
(t,x_{1},x_{1},x_{3})
centered at
x
so that

\nu^{-1}(0)
is identified with the
t
-axis, and that

\omega=2\nu^{+}=d\eta
, where

\begin{split}\eta&=-\big(\frac{x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}}{2}\big)\,dt-x_{3}(x_{1}dx_{2}-x_{2}dx_{1})+O\,(\sigma^{3}),\\
|\eta|&\leq\sigma\,|\nu|/2+O\,(\sigma^{3}).\end{split}

(6.2)

Let
Z_{x}(\delta,l)\subset X^{\prime\prime}
denote the cylinder consisting of points with distance no greater
than
\delta
from
\nu^{-1}(0)
, and whose
t
-coordinate
lies in the interval
[-l,l]
. The admissibility of
\nu

implies that there exist
x
-independent constants
1>\delta_{c}>0
,

1\geq l_{c}>0
such that for any given
x\in\nu^{-1}(0)\cap X^{{}^{\prime}8}
, local coordinates of the type described above are
defined over
Z_{x}(\delta,l)
for all
\delta\leq\delta_{c}
,

l\leq 2l_{c}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
r_{0}
, and
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
be as in Lemmas

4.6
and
4.11
.
There exist positive constants
r_{1}\geq r_{0}
,
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
such that
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}
depend only on

\nu
and the parameters listed in (
4.15
),
and
r_{1}
depends only on all the
above as well as on
e
(in particular, they are independent of

r
,
\delta
,
x
, and
(A,\Psi)
), with the following
significance: For any given
r\geq r_{1}
,
x\in\nu^{-1}(0)\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}
,

0\leq a\leq\frac{47}{8}
,
\delta
and
l
such that
\delta_{c}\geq\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2
,

l_{c}\geq l\geq l_{c}/4
, and
(A,\Psi)=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
satisfying
the assumption of Lemma

4.6
, one has:

\mathcal{W}_{Z_{x}(\delta,l)}(A,\Psi)\leq\begin{cases}\zeta\delta^{2}(\ln r+\textsc{e})&\\
\zeta^{\prime}\delta^{2}({\mathfrak{t}}(r)+\textsc{e})&\text{when Lemma \ref {lem:Etop-bddf} applies.}\end{cases}

Proof.
Let
l
be such that
l_{c}\geq l\geq l_{c}/4
, and let
\lambda_{l}(t)
be a smooth cutoff function which equals 1 over

[-l,l]
, and vanishes outside
[-l-l_{c}/8,l+l_{c}/8]
. Let

\eta_{l}:=\lambda_{l}\eta
and

{\mathscr{W}}_{\delta}:=4^{-1}\int_{Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)}\lambda_{l}\,r|\nu|\,\Big||\nu|-|\psi|^{2}\Big|.

Note that

\mathcal{W}_{Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)}(A,\Psi)\geq{\mathscr{W}}_{\delta}\geq\mathcal{W}_{Z_{x}(\delta,l)}(A,\Psi)
.

Suppose
r
is sufficiently large so that both
r\geq r_{0}
and

\delta_{1}=\delta_{1}(r)\leq\delta_{c}
.
Fix
\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2
. By the Seiberg-Witten equation
{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu,\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(A,\Psi)=0
, (
6.2
), Propositions

5.4
,
5.7
,
5.6
, and

5.5
, for
x\in\nu^{-1}(0)\cap X^{{}^{\prime}\frac{47}{8}}
,

\begin{split}\frac{i}{2}\int_{Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)}F_{A}\wedge d\eta_{l}&\geq\frac{i}{2}\int_{Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)}\lambda_{l}\,F_{A}\wedge\omega-\zeta_{1}\delta\int_{Z^{\prime}_{l}}|F_{A}|\,|\nu|\\
&\geq{\mathscr{W}}_{\delta}-\zeta_{2}\delta^{2}-\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\delta\,\mathcal{W}_{Z^{\prime}_{l}}(A,\Psi),\end{split}

(6.3)

where
Z^{\prime}_{l}\subset Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)-Z_{x}(\delta,l)
is the support of

\lambda^{\prime}_{l}
.
Meanwhile, by the Stokes’ theorem, (
6.1
), Propositions

5.7
,
5.6
,
5.4
and

5.5
, one has:

\begin{split}&\frac{i}{2}\int_{Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)}F_{A}\wedge d\eta_{l}=\frac{i}{2}\int_{\partial Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)}F_{A}\wedge\eta_{l}\\
&\,\,\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\partial Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)}\left(\lambda_{l}|\nu|\Big(r\Big||\nu|-|\psi|^{2}\Big|\big(1+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{\ln r+\textsc{e}}\big)+\zeta_{3}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\Big)\sigma(1+\zeta^{\prime}\sigma)\right)\\
&\,\,\leq\big(1+\zeta_{3}(\ln r+\textsc{e})^{-1}\big)\frac{\delta+\zeta^{\prime}\delta^{2}}{2}\,\frac{d}{d\delta}{\mathscr{W}}_{\delta}+\zeta_{4}\delta^{2}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for $\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2$.}\end{split}

(6.4)

Combining (
6.4
) and (
6.3
) and appealing to Lemma

4.17
, one obtains:

\begin{split}\frac{d}{d\delta}\left(e^{f_{0}}{\mathscr{W}}_{\delta}\right)&\geq-\big(\zeta_{1}^{\prime\prime}\,\mathcal{W}_{Z^{\prime}_{l}}(A,\Psi)+\zeta_{4}^{\prime}\delta\big)\,e^{f_{0}},\\
&\geq-\zeta_{5}\,(\ln r+\textsc{e})\,e^{f_{0}},\qquad\text{when $\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2$, where}\\
f_{0}(\delta)&:=-2\,(1+\zeta_{3}(\ln r+\textsc{e})^{-1})^{-1}\ln\,(\zeta^{\prime}\delta/(1+\zeta^{\prime}\delta)).\end{split}

(6.5)

Integrating the preceding differential inequality, one finds that there exist positive constants
\zeta_{w}
,
\zeta^{\prime\prime}
independent of
r
,
R
,
s
,
x
, and
(A,\Psi)
, such that for
s>\delta\geq\delta_{1}
,

\zeta_{w}{\mathscr{W}}_{s}/s^{2}\geq{\mathscr{W}}_{\delta}/\delta^{2}-\zeta^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{-1}-s^{-1})\,(\ln r+\textsc{e}).

Setting
s=\delta_{c}
and appealing to Lemma

4.17
, this gives:

\mathcal{W}_{Z_{x}(\delta,l)}(A,\Psi)\leq\zeta_{0}\,\delta(\ln r+\textsc{e})\quad\text{for $\delta_{c}\geq\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2$, $l\leq l_{c}$},

where
\zeta_{0}
is a positive constant
\zeta_{0}
independent of
r
,

\delta
,
x
,
l
, and
(A,\Psi)
. In particular, this implies
that

\mathcal{W}_{Z^{\prime}_{l}}(A,\Psi)\leq\mathcal{W}_{Z_{x}(\delta,l+l_{c}/8)}(A,\Psi)\leq\zeta_{0}\,\delta(\ln r+\textsc{e})\quad\text{for $\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2$, $l\leq l_{c}$}.

Reinsert this into the first line of (
6.5
) to get

\frac{d}{d\delta}\left(e^{f_{0}}{\mathscr{W}}_{\delta}\right)\geq-\zeta_{5}^{\prime}\,\delta^{-1+\zeta_{6}(\ln r+\textsc{e})^{-1}}(\ln r+\textsc{e}).

Integrating as before, one gets

\mathcal{W}_{Z_{x}(\delta,l)}(A,\Psi)\leq\zeta\,\delta^{2}(\ln r+\textsc{e})\quad\text{for $\delta_{c}\geq\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2$, $l\leq l_{c}$}

as asserted. The same argument shows that when
Lemma
4.11
applies, the factor of
\ln r
in the
preceding inequality may be replaced by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
.

\Box

The preceding lemma has the following corollary:

Lemma 6.2
.

Suppose
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{47}{8}
, has
length
|X_{\bullet}|=1
. Let
\delta_{c}
be as defined in the
paragraph preceding Lemma
6.1
. Then
for
-1<b\leq 1
,

\int_{X_{\bullet}\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}}r|\nu|^{b}\,\Big||\nu|-|\psi|^{2}\Big|\leq\zeta\delta_{c}^{-1+b}(\ln r+\textsc{e})\quad\text{$\forall r\geq r_{1}$ and
$\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2$.}

In the above,
r_{1}
,
\delta_{1}
are as in the previous lemma, and

\zeta
depend only on

\nu
and the parameters listed in (
4.15
).
When Lemma
4.11

applies, the factors
\ln r
in the preceding inequality can be replaced
by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
.

Proof.
Fix
r\geq r_{1}
and
\delta
,

\delta_{c}\geq\delta\geq\delta_{1}/2
. Decompose
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
as the union
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{c}}\cup\bigcup_{n=1}^{N}(Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{n}}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{n-1}})
, where

\delta_{n}:=2^{n}\delta
, and
N
is the
smallest
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{+}
such that
\delta_{n}\geq\delta_{c}
. Then
according to (
5.1
),
\textsl{z}_{v}\,\delta_{n}\geq|\nu|\geq\textsl{z}_{v}^{-1}\delta_{n-1}
over
Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{n}}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{n-1}}
.
Make use of Lemma
4.11
over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{c}}\cap X_{\bullet}
, and Lemma
6.1
over each

(Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{n}}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{n-1}})\cap X_{\bullet}
, one has:

\begin{split}&\int_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}\cap X_{\bullet}}r|\nu|^{b}\,\Big||\nu|-|\psi|^{2}\Big|\\
&\quad\leq\textsl{z}_{v}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\delta_{n-1}^{-1+b}\,\mathcal{W}_{Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{n}}\cap X_{\bullet}}(A,\Psi)+\textsl{z}_{v}\,\delta_{c}^{-1+b}\,\mathcal{W}_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{c}}\cap X_{\bullet}}(A,\Psi)\\
&\quad\leq\zeta_{1}(\ln r+\textsc{e})\sum_{n=1}^{N}\delta_{n-1}^{-1+b}\delta_{n}^{2}+\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\delta_{c}^{-1+b}(\ln r+\textsc{e})\\
&\quad\leq 4\zeta_{1}(\ln r+\textsc{e})\,\delta^{1+b}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}(2^{1+b})^{n}+\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\delta_{c}^{-1+b}(\ln r+\textsc{e})\\
&\quad\leq\zeta\delta_{c}^{-1+b}(\ln r+\textsc{e}).\end{split}

(6.6)

The same argument shows that when Lemma
4.11

applies, the factors
\ln r
in (
6.6
) can be replaced
by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
.

\Box

6.2
Integral bounds redux

We are now in a position to eliminate the undesirable factor of
\ln r
, which made its first appearance in Lemma
4.6
and
has propagated throughout our discussion so far. A closer look at the proof of Lemma
4.6
reveals that
this factor originates from the preliminary lower bound on the last
term of (
4.38
), namely

-\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{[l,L]}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge(F_{B}-F_{B_{0}}).

The bound in Section
4.2
made use of
L^{2}
-bounds of (components of)

F_{A}-F_{A_{0}}
. In what follows we argue differently, using instead a
better
L^{1}
-bound on
F_{A}-F_{A_{0}}
. The latter bound is in turn
made possible by the pointwise estimates from Section
5
and
the preliminary local energy bounds from the previous
subsection.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
r_{0}
,
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
be as in Lemmas

4.6
and
4.11
and
let
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{47}{8}
, be compact and
|X_{\bullet}|=1
.
Then there exist
r
-independent constants
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
,

r_{1}\geq r_{0}
, such that for all
r\geq r_{1}
,

\|F_{A}-F_{A_{0}}\|_{L^{1}(X_{\bullet})}\leq\begin{cases}\zeta(\ln r+\textsc{e})&\\
\zeta^{\prime}({\mathfrak{t}}(r)+\textsc{e})&\text{when Lemma \ref {lem:Etop-bddf}
applies}.\end{cases}

In the above,
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
depend only on

\nu
and the parameters listed in (
4.15
),
and
r_{1}
depends only on all the
above as well as on
e
.

Proof.
Let
\delta_{0}^{\prime}
,
\delta_{1}

be respectively as in Proposition
5.7
and Lemma

6.1
. Set
\delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}=r^{-1/4}
and assume that
r
is
sufficiently large so that
\delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}>\delta_{0}^{\prime}
. Decompose

X_{\bullet}
as the union of three regions,
{\mathscr{R}}_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
:
\sigma\leq\delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}
on
{\mathscr{R}}_{1}
;
\delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}\leq\sigma\leq\delta_{1}
over
{\mathscr{R}}_{2}
;
\sigma\geq\delta_{1}

over
{\mathscr{R}}_{3}
. Then according to Propositions
5.7

and
5.6
,
5.4
and

5.5
,

|F_{A}|\leq\begin{cases}\zeta_{1}r\tilde{\sigma}+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}&\text{over
${\mathscr{R}}_{1}$};\\
\zeta_{2}r\,(-u)_{+}+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}&\text{over
${\mathscr{R}}_{2}\cup{\mathscr{R}}_{3}$},\end{cases}

where
-u=|\nu|-|\psi|^{2}
as before.
Consequently,

\begin{split}&\|F_{A}-F_{A_{0}}\|_{L^{1}(X_{\bullet})}\\
&\quad\leq r\int_{{\mathscr{R}}_{1}}(\zeta_{1}\tilde{\sigma}+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2})+\zeta_{3}\,(\delta_{0}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{{\mathscr{R}}_{2}}(A,\Psi)+\zeta_{3}^{\prime}\int_{{\mathscr{R}}_{3}}r\,|-u|\\
&\qquad\qquad+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\int_{{\mathscr{R}}_{2}\cup{\mathscr{R}}_{3}}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\\
&\quad\leq\zeta_{1}^{\prime\prime}r\,(\delta_{0}^{\prime\prime})^{4}+\zeta_{4}\,(\delta_{0}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\delta_{1}^{2}\,(\ln r+\textsc{e})+\zeta_{4}^{\prime}\,(\ln r+\textsc{e})+\zeta_{2}^{\prime\prime}.\\
&\quad\leq\zeta_{5}+\zeta_{5}^{\prime}(\ln r+\textsc{e}).\end{split}

(6.7)

In the above, Lemma
6.1
was invoked to bound the
second term in the second line in terms of the the second term in the
third line; and Lemma
6.2
was used (with
b=0
) to
bound the third term in the second line in terms of the the third term in the
third line. When Lemma
4.11

applies, the same argument shows that the factors of
\ln r
in
(
6.7
) can be replaced by
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
.
\Box

The preceding lemma is now used to amend the bounds in Section

4
.

Proposition 6.4
.

The conclusions of Lemma
4.6
, Proposition
4.12
,
Proposition
4.13
,
Lemma
4.15
,
Lemma
4.16
, and
Lemma
4.17
remain valid with all factors of
\ln r
replaced by a positive
constant
z_{0}>1
. This constant depends only on
\nu
, the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
e
.

Proof.
Recall from (
4.38
) that when
\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}

\begin{split}&-2\operatorname{CSD}_{\mu_{+},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}}(B,\Phi)\geq-\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}}ds\,(*_{Y}\xi_{\nu})\wedge(F_{B}-F_{B_{0}}).\end{split}

Combining the preceding inequality with
(
4.35
), (
4.29
), and Lemma
6.3
, we
see that if
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)\colon\thinspace[r_{0},\infty)\to(1,\infty)
is such that
(
4.48
) holds, then for any
\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A,\Psi)\geq-\zeta\,re^{-\kappa_{i}l}(\textsc{e}+{\mathfrak{t}}(r))

(6.8)

for some positive constant
\zeta
independent of
r
and

\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
.
But this means that if (
4.48
) holds for some

{\mathfrak{t}}(r)={\mathfrak{t}}_{k}(r)
, then it holds also for

{\mathfrak{t}}(r)={\mathfrak{t}}_{k+1}(r)
, where

{\mathfrak{t}}_{k+1}(r)=\ln\,(1+{\mathfrak{t}}_{k}(r))+\ln\,(\zeta/\zeta^{\prime}_{5}).

(6.9)

We already know that (
4.48
) holds for

{\mathfrak{t}}(r)={\mathfrak{t}}_{1}(r)
,

{\mathfrak{t}}_{1}(r):=\ln r.

So in this way we get a sequence of functions
\{{\mathfrak{t}}_{1},{\mathfrak{t}}_{2},\cdots\}
and (
4.48
) holds for
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)={\mathfrak{t}}_{k}(r)

\forall k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Now, there is a number
z_{0}>1
such that

\ln\,(\textsc{e}+z)+\ln\,(\zeta/\zeta^{\prime}_{5})<z/2\quad\forall z\geq z_{0}.

Then according to (
6.9
),

{\mathfrak{t}}_{k+1}(r)<\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{t}}_{k}(r)\quad\text{if ${\mathfrak{t}}_{k}(r)\geq z_{0}$}.

Thus, for any fixed
r\geq r_{0}>1
, there exists an
n\in{\mathbb{N}}

(depending on
r
) such that
{\mathfrak{t}}_{n+1}(r)\leq z_{0}
. Consequently,

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}_{top}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\geq-\zeta^{\prime}_{5}\,r\quad\forall X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{z_{0}\,\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathop{\mathrm{l}}}}},r\geq r_{0}.

(6.10)

That is to say, the assumption (
4.48
) in Lemma

4.11
holds when
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
therein is taken to be
the constant function
z_{0}
. So the conclusion of Lemma

4.11
,
(
4.49
), also holds with

{\mathfrak{t}}(r)
replaced by
z_{0}
. This is the same as saying that Lemma

4.6
holds with all factors of
\ln r
replaced by

z_{0}
. It follows that all appearances of
\ln r
or
{\mathfrak{t}}(r)

in Proposition
4.12
,
Proposition
4.13
,
Lemma
4.15
,
Lemma
4.16
, and
Lemma
4.17
can be likewise be replaced by
z_{0}
.
\Box

Lemma 6.5
.

The conclusions of Lemmas
6.3
,

6.1
and
6.2

remain valid with all factors of
\ln r
replaced by a positive
constant
z_{0}>1
. This constant depends only on
\nu
, the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
e
.
In particular, there exist constants
r_{1}
,
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
,

\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{1}^{\prime}
, such that
\forall r\geq r_{1}
and for
any
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{47}{8}
,

\|F_{A}\|_{L^{1}(X_{\bullet})}\leq\zeta|X_{\bullet}|+\zeta_{1}\textsc{e};\quad\|A^{\prime}-A_{0}\|_{L^{1}(X_{\bullet})}\leq(\zeta^{\prime}|X_{\bullet}|+\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\textsc{e})\,|X_{\bullet}|,

(6.11)

where
(A^{\prime},\Psi^{\prime})=u\cdot(A,\Psi)
is in the normalized
Coulomb-Neumann gauge over
X_{\bullet}
, and the constants
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
,
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{1}^{\prime}
depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{1}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.

Proof.
Simply replace all usage of Lemma
4.17
in the proofs of
Lemmas
6.3
,

6.1
and
6.2
by the amended
version of Lemma
4.17
in Proposition

6.4
. The
L^{1}
-bound on
A^{\prime}-A_{0}
follows from
that for
F_{A}
via a standard elliptic estimate, noting that the lowest absolute
value of non-zero eigenvalues of
*d+d*
on 1-forms on
X_{\bullet}

with Neumann boundary condition has a
lower bound propotional to
|X_{\bullet}|^{-1}
).

\Box

The next two lemmas will be useful in Section
7
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
. Let

\hat{Y}_{[l,l+1]}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{47}{8}
, and suppose
\eta
is an exact
2-form on
\hat{Y}_{[l,l+1]}
. Then there exist positive constants

r_{0}
,
\zeta_{2}
,
\zeta_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\forall r\geq r_{0}
,

\Big|\int_{Y:l}i(A-A_{0})\wedge\eta\Big|+\Big|\int_{Y:l+1}i\,(A-A_{0})\wedge\eta\Big|\leq(\zeta_{2}+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\textsc{e})\,\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}(\hat{Y}_{[l,l+1]})}.

(Note that both integrals in the preceding formula are gauge-invariant
due to the exactness of
\eta
.) In the above, the constants
\zeta_{2}
,
\zeta_{2}^{\prime}
depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{0}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.

Proof.
Let
\chi_{l}(s)\colon\thinspace[l,l+1]\to[0,1]
be a non-increasing function that equals 1 on
[l,l+1/4]
,
and vanishes over
[l+3/4,l+1]
. Use the same notation to denote its
pull back over
\hat{Y}_{i,[l,l+1]}
under the projection
\hat{Y}_{[l,l+1]}\simeq[l,l+1]\times Y\to[l,l+1]
.

\begin{split}&i\int_{Y_{i:l}}(A-A_{0})\wedge\eta=i\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l,l+1]}}d\big(\chi_{l}(s)\,(A-A_{0})\wedge\eta\big)\\
&\quad=i\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l,l+1]}}\chi_{l}(s)\,(F_{A}-F_{A_{0}})\wedge\eta+i\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l,l+1]}}\chi_{l}^{\prime}(s)\,ds\,(A^{\prime}-A_{0})\wedge\eta,\end{split}

(6.12)

where
(A^{\prime},\Psi^{\prime})=u\cdot(A,\Psi)
is in the normalized
Coulomb-Neumann gauge over
\hat{Y}_{i,[l,l+1]}
.
The asserted bound for
\Big|\int_{Y:l}i(A-A_{0})\wedge\eta\Big|
then follows from Equation (
6.11
). The same
argument can be used to bound
\Big|\int_{Y:l+1}i(A-A_{0})\wedge\eta\Big|
, by simply replacing
\chi_{l}
in the preceding formula
by
1-\chi_{l}
.

\Box

Lemma 6.7
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
.
Suppose
X_{\bullet}
is such that
\partial X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{47}{8}
.
Then there exist positive constants
r_{0}
,
\zeta_{h},\zeta^{\prime}_{h}

such that

\Big|\int_{X_{\bullet}}iF_{A_{r}}\wedge\nu\Big|\leq\zeta_{h}\textsc{e}+\zeta_{h}^{\prime}.

(6.13)

In the above, the positive constants
\zeta_{h}
,
\zeta^{\prime}_{h}

depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{0}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.

Proof.
Write

\begin{split}&i\int_{X_{\bullet}}F_{A}\wedge\nu\\
&\quad=4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)-4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\\
&\quad=4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)-4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)\\
&\qquad\qquad-ir^{-1}\int_{\partial X_{\bullet}}(A-A_{0})\wedge(*_{4}\mu_{r})\\
&\quad=4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)-4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)-i\int_{\partial X_{\bullet}}(B-B_{0})\wedge{\mathfrak{v}}\\
&\quad=4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)-4r^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r}}(X_{\bullet})(A,\Psi)-i\int_{\partial X_{\bullet}}(B^{\prime}-B_{0})\wedge{\mathfrak{v}},\end{split}

(6.14)

where
(B,\Psi):=(A,\Psi)|_{\partial X_{\bullet}}
, and
(B^{\prime},\Psi^{\prime})
is the representative of

[(B,\Psi)]
in the normalized Coulomb gauge. We used the fact

{\mathfrak{v}}|_{\partial X_{\bullet}}
is exact to derive the last line above
from the penultimate line. Using Lemma
4.6
(or Lemma

4.11
), Lemma

4.15
,
and their amendments in Proposition

6.4
to estimate the first two terms in the last
line of the previous formula, and using the preceding lemma to bound
the last term, we arrive at the asserted bound (
6.13
).

\Box

6.3
The monotonicity formula

We begin by improving the pointwise estimates of

|F_{A}^{-}|
given in Propositions
5.7

and
5.6
. The estimates in the next lemma is only better
than that in Proposition
5.7
where
3r^{-1/6}\,(\ln r+\textsc{e})^{1/6}\geq\sigma\geq r^{-2/9}(\ln r)^{1/4}
. However,
this improvement will be useful later.
Let
u:=|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|
as before.

Lemma 6.8
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
.
Let
\delta_{1}^{\prime}:=r^{-2/9}(\ln r)^{1/4}
.
There exist positive constants

r_{0}>8
,
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
, with the following significance:
For any
r\geq r_{0}
one has:

\begin{split}|F_{A}^{-}|&\leq(2^{-3/2}+\zeta(\ln r)^{-1})\,r\,(-u)_{+}+\zeta^{\prime}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta^{\prime}_{1}}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{47}{8}$.}\end{split}

(6.15)

In the above, the constants
\zeta

and
\zeta^{\prime}
depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{0}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.
More generally, given any positive
\epsilon<1/2
,
there exists constants
r_{\epsilon}
,
\zeta_{\epsilon}
,
\zeta^{\prime}_{\epsilon}
,
such that
\forall r\geq r_{\epsilon}
,

\begin{split}|F_{A}^{-}|&\leq(2^{-3/2}+\zeta_{\epsilon}(\ln r)^{-1})\,r\,(-u)_{+}+\zeta^{\prime}_{\epsilon}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{1,\epsilon}}$,}\end{split}

(6.16)

where
\delta_{1,\epsilon}:=r^{-(1-\epsilon)/4}
.

Proof.
Let
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{0}^{\prime}

\delta_{0}
be respectively
as in the beginning of Section
6.1
, Proposition

5.7
,
and Proposition
5.6
. Assume below that
r
is sufficiently large so that
\delta_{1}>\delta^{\prime}_{1}>\delta_{0}^{\prime}>8\delta_{0}
.
Return to the proof of Proposition
5.6
. Re-examine the
bounds for each term on the right hand side of (
5.30
),
taking
\varepsilon=(8\ln r)^{-1}
. The same argument, with
(
4.50
) replaced by its amended version from Proposition

6.4
, show that
the first term on the RHS of (
5.30
) is bounded by a constant
multiple of
r^{1/2}
. Meanwhile, the second and the third terms
are respectively bounded by
constant multiples of
r^{1/3}(\ln r)\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}
and
\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}
when
x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}^{\prime}}
. Together, we have

\begin{split}&q(x)\leq\zeta_{1}r^{1/3}(\ln r)\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}+\zeta_{2}r^{1/2}+\zeta_{3}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}+\text{the last term of (\ref {eq:q-est})}\quad\end{split}

(6.17)

for
x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}^{\prime}}
.

A little more work is required to improve the bound
on the last term.
Note that by Proposition
5.7
and (
6.1
),
(
6.15
) already holds where
\sigma\geq\delta_{1}/2
. Thus, it suffices to consider
x\in Z_{\delta_{1}/2}^{{}^{\prime}a}\cap X_{\delta_{0}^{\prime}}^{{}^{\prime}a}
. Fix an

x\in Z_{\delta_{1}/2}^{{}^{\prime}a}\cap X_{\delta_{0}^{\prime}}^{{}^{\prime}a}
and reintroduce the notation
\sigma_{x}:=\sigma(x)
;
\tilde{\sigma}_{x}:=\tilde{\sigma}(x)
. This time, we divide
B(x,2\rho_{0})\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}/2}
into four regions defined from three parameters
\rho_{1},\delta_{2}
, and
\delta_{3}
. These parameters are chosen such that:

\rho_{1}\leq\sigma_{x}/4;\quad\delta_{2}\geq\max\,(2\sigma_{x},\delta_{1}/2);\quad\delta_{0}/2\leq\delta_{3}<\sigma_{x}/2.

Since
\sigma_{x}\leq\delta_{1}/2
by assumption, we take
\delta_{2}=\delta_{1}
.
Then
B(x,2\rho_{0})=\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq 4}{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{i}
,
{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{1}:=B(x,\rho_{1})\subset Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{2}}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{3}}
;
{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{2}:=X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{2}}\cap B(x,2\rho_{0})
,
{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{3}:=(Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{2}}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{3}})\cap B(x,2\rho_{0})-{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{1}
;

{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{4}:=(Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{3}}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}/2})\cap B(x,2\rho_{0})
. Note
that with the present choice of
\varepsilon
and the assumption
that
\delta>\delta_{0}^{\prime}
, we have simpler pointwise bounds for
\eta_{2}
:

\eta_{2}\leq\zeta_{2}r\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}(-u)_{+}\leq\zeta_{2}^{\prime}r\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\quad\text{on $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}^{\prime}}$}.

Integrate over each of the four regions separately to replace
(
5.33
) by:

\begin{split}&\int_{B(x,2\rho_{0})}\eta_{2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2}\\
&\quad\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\,r\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}_{x}\,\rho_{1}^{2}+\zeta_{2}\,\delta_{2}^{-2}\delta_{2}^{-3/2}+\zeta_{3}\delta_{3}^{-3}\delta_{2}^{2}\rho_{1}^{-2}+\zeta_{4}r\sigma_{x}^{-1}\delta_{3}^{2}\,\,\text{when $x\in Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{1}/2}$}.\end{split}

(6.18)

In the above, the first and the last terms on the right hand side
respectively bound the integrals over the first and the last regions,
following the same computations that respectively yield the first and
the last term on the RHS of (
5.33
). The second term on the
RHS of (
6.18
) bounds the integral over
{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{2}
, which follows from the
amended version of Lemma

6.2
(with
b=-1/2
) in Lemma

6.5
, together with the observation that

\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)\geq\delta_{2}/2
over
{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{2}
. The third term on the
RHS of (
6.18
) bounds the integral over
{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{3}
, which follows from the
amended version of Lemma
6.1
in Lemma

6.5
. Now we take
\rho_{1}=\delta_{3}/2
, and
\delta_{3}=(r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}\delta_{2}^{2})^{1/7}
. This choice of
\delta_{3}

meets the requirement that
\delta_{3}<\sigma_{x}/2
when
r
is
sufficiently large. With such choice,

\begin{split}\int_{B(x,2\rho_{0})}\eta_{2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2}&\leq\zeta\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1})^{5/7}\delta_{2}^{4/7}+\zeta_{2}\,\delta_{2}^{-7/2}\\
&\leq\zeta\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1})^{5/7}\delta_{1}^{4/7}+\zeta^{\prime}r^{7/12}(\ln r)^{-21/8}.\end{split}

(6.19)

Combining (
6.17
), (
6.19
) and make use of Propositions

5.4
and
5.5
as in the proof of Proposition
5.6
, we have:

|F_{A}^{-}|\leq\big(2^{-3/2}+(8\ln r)^{-1}\big)\,r\,(-u)_{+}+K_{1}^{\prime}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta^{\prime}_{0}}$,}

where
K_{1}^{\prime}:=\zeta_{2}\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1})^{5/7}\delta_{1}^{4/7}+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}r^{7/12}(\ln r)^{-21/8}+\zeta_{1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}r^{1/3}(\ln r)\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}.

(As observed previously, by Proposition

5.7
this inequality already hold over
X_{\delta_{1}/2}
.)
Next, re-run the proof of Proposition

5.7
, replacing the use of Proposition

5.6
by the preceding inequality. In particular, the role
of
K_{1}
therein is now played by
K_{1}^{\prime}
. The
arguments in the proof then yield:

|F_{A}^{-}|\leq\big(2^{-3/2}+(8\ln r)^{-1}+\zeta_{5}\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}\big)\,r\,(-u)_{+}+\zeta_{5}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta^{\prime}_{0}}$},

where

\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}:=K_{1}^{\prime}/\tilde{r}\leq\zeta\,\tilde{r}^{-2/7}\tilde{\sigma}^{-10/7}\delta_{1}^{4/7}+\zeta_{6}(\ln r)^{-1}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}\ln r}$}.

This leads to the asserted inequality
(
6.15
). The second assertion of the lemma is obtained
by iterating the preceding arguments. Let
\delta_{1:0}:=\delta_{1}

and
\delta_{1:1}:=\delta_{1}^{\prime}
. Suppose an inequality of the form
(
6.15
) holds over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{1:k}}
and

\delta_{1:k}\geq r^{-1/4}
, then
the preceding arguments imply that it also holds over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{1:k+1}}
(but with different constants
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
),
where
\delta_{1:k+1}=r^{-1/6}\delta_{1:k}^{1/3}(\ln r)^{7/12}
. This implies the assertion (
6.16
) by iteration.

\Box

An immediate consequence of the preceding lemma is:

Lemma 6.9
.

Lemmas
6.1
,
6.2
, and their amended
versions in Lemma

6.5
also hold with all appearances of
\delta_{1}

therein replaced by
\delta_{1}^{\prime}
or more
generally,
\delta_{1,\epsilon}
for any given

0<\epsilon<1/2
(but with the various constant coefficients

\zeta_{*}
changed).

Proof.
Simply replace the use of Proposition
5.7
in the proof of Lemma

6.1
by the preceding lemma.
\Box

We also have the following variant of Lemma
6.1
. This
may be viewed as a Seiberg-Witten version of Taubes’ monotonicity formula for
pseudo-holomorphic curves near
\omega^{-1}(0)
(cf. Section 2 of

[
Td
]
).

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
\delta^{\prime}_{1}

\delta_{1,\epsilon}
be as in Lemma

6.8
and let
\delta^{\prime}_{*}:=\delta^{\prime}_{1}
or

\delta_{1,\epsilon}
.
There exist positive constants
r_{1}
,
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
,

\zeta^{\prime\prime}
that are independent of

r
,
R
,
x
, and
(A,\Psi)
with the following
significance:
Given any
r\geq r_{1}
,
x\in\nu^{-1}(0)\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{43}{8}
,
and
R
such that
1/4\geq R\geq 2\delta_{*}^{\prime}
, one has:

\mathcal{W}_{B_{x}(R)}(A,\Psi)\leq\zeta\,R^{3}+\zeta^{\prime}(\delta_{*}^{\prime})^{2}.

In particular, when
R\geq(\delta^{\prime}_{*})^{2/3}
, then
\mathcal{W}_{B_{x}(R)}\,(A,\Psi)\leq\zeta^{\prime\prime}\,R^{3}
.

Proof.
Fix
x\in\nu^{-1}(0)\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}
and
R
with
1/8\geq R\geq 2\delta^{\prime}_{*}
.
Let
\rho
denote the function
\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)
on
X
.
Write
\omega=dq
on small neighborhood of
x
, where

q
is of the form

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{0}+O(\sigma^{2}\rho),\quad\text{with}

(6.20)

\displaystyle q_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{t}{2}d\,(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}-2x_{3}^{2})-(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}-2x_{3}^{2})\,dt-3x_{3}\,(x_{1}dx_{2}-x_{2}dx_{1})

in a certain local coordinate chart at
x
, in terms of which

\nu^{-1}(0)
is identified with the
t
-axis:
\{(t,x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})|\,x_{1}=x_{2}=x_{3}=0\}
, and
|q_{0}|=2^{-1/2}\rho|\omega|/3
. Let
\rho_{0}
be the maximal value of
R
such that
B_{x}(R)
is contained in this
local coordinate chart, and suppose
R\leq\rho_{0}
from now on. (If

R\geq\rho_{0}
, the asserted inequality already hold
for certain
\zeta
.)

Recall again the notation
-u=|\nu|-|\psi|^{2}
, and let

{\mathscr{W}}_{R}:=4^{-1}\int_{B_{x}(2R)}\chi(\rho/R)\,r|\nu|\,|-u|\geq\mathcal{W}_{B_{x}(R)}(A,\Psi).

Use the Stokes’ theorem, the Seiberg-Witten equation
{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu,\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(A,\Psi)=0
, and Proposition
5.4
to get:

\begin{split}{\mathscr{W}}_{R}-\zeta_{1}R^{3}&\leq\frac{i}{2}\int_{B_{x}(2R)}\chi(\rho/R)\,F_{A}\wedge\omega\leq-\frac{1}{2R}\int_{B_{x}(2R)}\chi^{\prime}(\rho/R)\,|F_{A}|\,|q|\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2R}\int_{B_{x}(2R)}\chi^{\prime}(\rho/R)\,|F_{A}|\,|\nu|\,(\rho/3+\zeta_{2}\rho\sigma).\end{split}

(6.21)

The last line in the preceding expression is in turn bounded via Lemma

6.8
, Propositions
5.7
,

5.6
,
5.4
and
5.5
as:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2R}\int_{B_{x}(2R)}\chi^{\prime}(\rho/R)\,|F_{A}|\,|\nu|\,(\rho/3+\zeta_{2}\rho\sigma)\\
&\quad\leq\big(1+\zeta_{8}(\ln r)^{-1}\big)\frac{R+\zeta_{4}\,R^{2}}{3}\frac{d{\mathscr{W}}_{R}}{dR}+\zeta_{4}^{\prime}\,R^{3}\\
&\qquad\quad+\zeta_{2}\int_{B_{x}(2R)-B_{x}(R)}\chi(\sigma/\delta_{0}^{\prime})\,r\,\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\\
&\qquad\quad+\int_{B_{x}(2R)-B_{x}(R)}\chi(\sigma/\delta_{*}^{\prime})\big(1-\chi(\sigma/\delta_{0}^{\prime})\big)\big(\zeta_{3}\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}+\zeta_{3}^{\prime}r\,|\nu|\,|-u|\big).\end{split}

(6.22)

Combining (
6.22
) and (
6.21
), and applying the amended
version of Lemma
6.1
in Lemma
6.9
to
estimate the last integral above, one obtains:

\begin{split}{\mathscr{W}}_{R}&\leq\big(1+\zeta_{8}(\ln r)^{-1}\big)\frac{R+\zeta_{4}\,R^{2}}{3}\frac{d{\mathscr{W}}_{R}}{dR}\\
&\qquad+\zeta^{\prime}_{1}R^{3}+\zeta_{5}r(\delta_{0}^{\prime})^{5}R+\zeta_{5}^{\prime}(\delta_{*}^{\prime})^{2}R+\zeta_{6}(\delta_{*}^{\prime})^{2}.\end{split}

This in turn leads to:

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dR}\left(e^{f_{z}}{\mathscr{W}}_{R}\right)&\geq-\big(\zeta_{7}\,R^{2}+\zeta_{7}^{\prime}\delta^{\prime\prime 2}/R\big)\,e^{f_{z}},\qquad\text{when $R\geq 2\delta^{\prime}_{*}$, where}\\
f_{z}(R)&:=-3(1+\zeta_{8}(\ln r)^{-1})^{-1}\ln\,(\zeta_{4}R/(1+\zeta_{4}R)).\end{split}

Integrating, one finds that there exist positive constants
\zeta_{w}
,
\zeta^{\prime\prime}
independent of
r
,
R
,
s
,
x
, and
(A,\Psi)
, such that for
s>R\geq 2\delta^{\prime}_{*}
,

\zeta_{w}{\mathscr{W}}_{s}/s^{3/(1+z_{r})}\geq{\mathscr{W}}_{R}/R^{3/(1+z_{r})}-R^{3z_{r}/(1+z_{r})}\big(\zeta_{9}+\zeta_{9}^{\prime}\delta^{\prime\prime 2}R^{-3}\big),

where
z_{r}:=\zeta_{8}(\ln r)^{-1}
.
Now take
s=\rho_{0}
, and appeal to the amended
version of Lemma
6.1
in Lemma
6.9

again to obtain the asserted inequality.

\Box

We are now ready to state and prove the counterpart of Proposition 4.1 of
[
Ts
]
and
Proposition I.3.1 of
[
T
]
in our context.

Let
\underline{\alpha}=|\nu|^{-1/2}\alpha
, and recall the notations

\tilde{r}=r\tilde{\sigma}
;
-u=|\nu|-|\psi|^{2}
.

Proposition 6.11
.

(the monotonicity formula)

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
.
Let
\delta^{\prime}_{1}

\delta_{1,\epsilon}
be as in Lemma

6.8
, and let
\delta_{*}:=(\delta_{*}^{\prime})^{2/3}=(\delta^{\prime}_{1})^{2/3}
or

(\delta_{1,\epsilon})^{2/3}
. There exist positive constants

r_{0}
,
\zeta
with the following
significance: Given any
r\geq r_{0}
,
x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}
,

0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}
, and an

R\in{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
satisfying
(r\tilde{\sigma}(x))^{-1/2}\leq R<\tilde{\sigma}(x)/4
, one has:

\mathcal{W}_{B_{x}(R)}\,(A,\Psi)\leq\zeta\tilde{\sigma}(x)\,R^{2}.

(6.23)

Fix a number
v
,
0\leq v<1
. If in addition,

|\underline{\alpha}(x)|\leq v
, then there exist constants
\zeta_{v}
,
\kappa_{v}<1/4
that are independent of

r
,
x
, and
(A,\Psi)
(but possibly depend on
v
), such that
for and
R
satisfying
(r\tilde{\sigma}(x))^{-1/2}\leq R<\kappa_{v}\,\tilde{\sigma}(x)
,
one has:

\mathcal{W}_{B_{x}(R)}\,(A,\Psi)\geq\,\zeta_{v}\,\tilde{\sigma}(x)\,R^{2}.

(6.24)

In the above, the constants
\zeta
,
\kappa_{v}

and
\zeta_{v}
(for each fixed
v
) depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{0}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.

Proof.
Let
\delta^{\prime}_{*}
is as in the preceding lemma, and choose
r
to
be sufficiently large so that
\delta_{*}>2\delta_{*}^{\prime}
. Fix
x\in X_{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}
. Let
\rho\,(y):=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,y)
as before. For simplicity, we frequently write

\mathcal{W}_{x,R}=\mathcal{W}_{B_{x}(R)}(A,\Psi)
and
\tilde{\sigma}_{x}=\tilde{\sigma}(x)
below.
Let
R<\tilde{\sigma}(x)/4
, so that
B_{x}(2R)\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a+1/2}_{\delta_{*}/2}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a+1/2}_{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}
,
and
\tilde{\sigma}(x)/2<\tilde{\sigma}(y)<3\tilde{\sigma}(x)/2

\forall y\in B_{x}(2R)
.
As usual, use the Seiberg-Witten equation
{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu,\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(A,\Psi)=0
and Proposition
5.4
to get:

\begin{split}\frac{i}{2}\int_{B_{x}(R)}F_{A}\wedge\omega+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}R^{4}&\geq 4^{-1}\int_{B_{x}(R)}r|\nu|\,\Big||\nu|-|\psi|^{2}\Big|\\
&=\mathcal{W}_{x,R}.\end{split}

(6.25)

This time, write
\omega=2\nu^{+}=d\theta
over
B_{x}(2R)
,
where
\theta=\frac{1}{2}(y_{1}dy_{2}-y_{2}dy_{1}+y_{3}dy_{4}-y_{4}dy_{3})+O(\rho^{2})
in a symplectic local coordinate system
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})

where
\omega=dy_{1}\wedge dy_{2}+dy_{3}\wedge dy_{4}+O(\rho)
.
In particular, it satisfies

|\theta|\leq 2^{-3/2}|\omega|(\rho+O(\rho^{2}))
. Then the usual
argument via the Stokes’
theorem,
Lemma
6.8
, Propositions
5.6
(a),
5.4
, and
5.5
gives:

\begin{split}&\frac{i}{2}\int_{B_{x}(R)}F_{A}\wedge\omega=\frac{i}{2}\int_{\partial B_{x}(R)}F_{A}\wedge\theta\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial B_{x}(R)}(|F_{A}^{+}|^{2}+|F_{A}^{-}|^{2})^{1/2}|\nu|(\rho+\zeta\rho^{2})\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\partial B_{x}(R)}\left(r\,(1+\zeta_{1}(\ln r)^{-1})\Big||\nu|-|\psi|^{2}\Big|+\zeta^{\prime}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\right)|\nu|\,(\rho+\zeta\rho^{2})/2\\
&\qquad\qquad+\zeta_{2}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1}R^{4}\\
&\quad\leq 2^{-1}(1+\zeta_{1}(\ln r)^{-1})\,(R+\zeta R^{2})\frac{d\mathcal{W}_{x,R}}{dR}+\zeta_{2}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1}R^{4}\quad\text{for $x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}$.}\end{split}

(6.26)

Combined with (
6.25
), this leads to:

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dR}\left(e^{f}\mathcal{W}_{x,R}\right)\geq-\zeta^{\prime}_{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}_{x}R^{3}e^{f},\qquad\text{for $x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}$, where}\\
f(R):=-2\big(1+\zeta_{1}(\ln r)^{-1}\big)^{-1}\ln(R/(1+\zeta R)).\end{split}

Fix a positive constant
c\leq 1
. Integrating the preceding
differential inequality, we have for
x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}
and
c\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x})^{-1/2}\leq R\leq s\leq\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/4
,

\zeta_{c}\,\mathcal{W}_{x,s}/s^{2}\geq\mathcal{W}_{x,R}/R^{2}-\zeta^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1}(s^{2}-R^{2})\geq 0,

(6.27)

where
\zeta^{\prime\prime},\zeta_{c}>0
are certain
r
-independent constants. (
\zeta_{c}
may depend on
c
).
Now take
s=\tilde{\sigma}_{x}/4
in
the preceding inequality. The assertion (
6.23
) then
follows from Lemma
6.10
.

To prove the second inequality claimed, i.e. (
6.24
), suppose that
x
satisfies
|\underline{\alpha}(x)|<v
. Take

R=c\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x})^{-1/2}
in (
6.27
), one has:

\zeta_{c}\mathcal{W}_{x,s}/s^{2}\geq c^{-2}r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}\mathcal{W}_{x,c\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x})^{-1/2}}-\zeta^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1}(s^{2}-c^{2}(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x})^{-1}).

(6.28)

Repeat the argument in Step 3 of the proof of Proposition I.3.1 in
[
T
]
with the following modifications:

(1)

Replace the use of Proposition I.2.8 in
[
T
]
by its
analog in our setting, Proposition
5.9
;

(2)

Replace
r
by
r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}
,

(3)

Replace
\alpha
by
\underline{\alpha}
.

(4)

Replace the condition
\underline{\alpha}<1/2
therein by

\underline{\alpha}<v
.

(5)

Proposition
5.9

implies that there is a constant
c<1
independent of
r
and
v
,
so that
\underline{\alpha}(y)<1-(1-v)/2
when
\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(y,x)<c\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x})^{-1/2}
.

(6)

Consequently, the counterpart of
[
T
]
’s (I.3.11) in our
context says:

\mathcal{W}_{x,c\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x})^{-1/2}}\geq\zeta_{6}\big(1-(1+v)^{2}/4\big)\,r\tilde{\sigma}^{2}_{x}c^{4}(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x})^{-2}.

Inserting the preceding inequality into (
6.27
), setting the constant
c
therein to be the constant

c
in Items (5) and (6) above. We get:
the first term on the right hand side of (
6.28
)
has a lower bound by a constant
\zeta^{\prime}
; therefore

\mathcal{W}_{x,s}\geq\zeta_{c}^{-1}s^{2}\Big(\zeta_{6}\big(1-(1+v)^{2}/4\big)c^{2}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}-\zeta^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{-1}s^{2}\Big).

This implies that the second inequality asserted, (
6.24
),
with

\zeta_{v}=\zeta_{c}^{-1}\zeta_{6}\,c^{2}\big(1-(1+v)^{2}/4\big)/2,\quad\kappa_{v}=\min\,\big(1/4,(\zeta_{v}/\zeta^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}\big).

\Box

The monotonicity formula is used primarily by way of the next corollary.

Given a set
\Lambda^{*}=\{B_{k}\}_{k}
consisting of balls
B_{k}\subset X
, and a subset
U\subset X
, we use
\Lambda^{*}_{U}\subset\Lambda^{*}
to denote the subset
\Lambda^{*}_{U}=\{B_{k}\,|\,B_{k}\cap U\neq\emptyset\}_{k}
. Given
v
,
0<v<1
, let
X_{\delta,v}^{{}^{\prime}a}:=\{x\,|\,|\underline{\alpha}|(x)\leq v,\,x\in X_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}\}
.

Corollary 6.12
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
,
and let
\delta_{*}
,
\kappa_{v}
be as in
Proposition
6.11
.
There exist
constants
r_{0}
,
\zeta_{0}
,
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
that are independent of
r
,

\rho
,
\delta
,
X_{\bullet}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{R}
,
and
(A,\Psi)
, such that the following hold for any given
r\geq r_{0}
,
1>\delta\geq\delta_{*}
,

•

Fix
\rho\in[r^{-1/2},\kappa_{0}\,\delta^{3/2}]
.
Let
\Lambda=\{B(x_{k},\rho_{k})\}_{k}
be a set of mutually disjoint
balls in
X_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}
, with

\rho_{k}:=\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k})^{-1/2}\rho
and
x_{k}\in\alpha^{-1}(0)
. Then given a compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
, the
subset
\Lambda_{X_{\bullet}}\subset\Lambda
has no more than

\zeta_{0}\,\rho^{-2}(1+|X_{\bullet}|)
elements.

•

Let
\rho
be as in the previous bullet. The set
\alpha^{-1}(0)\,\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}

can be covered by a set of balls,
\Lambda^{\rho}=\{B\,(x_{k},\rho_{k})\}_{k}
, where
\rho_{k}=\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k})^{-1/2}\rho
and

x_{k}\in\alpha^{-1}(0)\cap X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta/2}
, such that the concentric
balls
B(x_{k},\rho_{k}/2)
are mutually disjoint. Any such set

\Lambda^{\rho}
has the following
properties:

(i)

Given any compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
, the set
\Lambda^{\rho}_{X_{\bullet}}
consists of no
more than
\zeta\,\rho^{-2}(1+|X_{\bullet}|)
elements.

(ii)

Given any ball
{\mathfrak{B}}_{R}=B\,(x,R)\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
of radius

R
, with

(r\tilde{\sigma}(x))^{-1/2}\leq R<\tilde{\sigma}(x)/4
, the set
\Lambda_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{R}}^{\rho}

consists of no more than
\zeta^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}(x)\,\rho^{-2}R^{2}
elements.

•

Fix
v
,
0<v<1
and
\rho\in[r^{-1/2},\kappa_{v}\delta^{3/2}]
. Then there exists positive constants

c_{v}
,
c_{v}^{\prime}
that are independent of
r
,
\delta
,
\rho
,
(A,\Psi)
,
X_{\bullet}
and
{\mathfrak{B}}_{R}
(but possibly on
v
), such that
the following holds:
The set
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,v}
has a cover by a set of balls,

\Lambda^{v,\rho}=\{B(x_{k},\rho_{k})\}_{k}
, where
\rho_{k}=\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k})^{-1/2}\rho
, and the concentric balls

B(x_{k},\rho_{k}/2)
are mutually disjoint.
Any such set
\Lambda^{v,\rho}
has the following properties:

(i)

Given any compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
, the set
\Lambda_{X_{\bullet}}^{v,\rho}
consists of no
more than
c_{v}\,\rho^{-2}(1+|X_{\bullet}|)
elements.

(ii)

Given any ball
{\mathfrak{B}}_{R}=B\,(x,R)\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
of radius

R
, with

(r\tilde{\sigma}(x))^{-1/2}\leq R<\tilde{\sigma}(x)/4
, the set
\Lambda_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{R}}^{v,\rho}

consists of no more than
c_{v}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\sigma}(x)\,\rho^{-2}R^{2}
elements.

In the above, the constants
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}

\zeta_{0}
, and
c_{v}
,
c^{\prime}_{v}
(for each fixed
v
) depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{0}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.

Proof.
This is an
analog of Lemmas I.3.6 and I.3.8 in
[
T
]
and follows from the same
argument with the use of Proposition I.3.1 by its counterpart in our
context, Proposition
6.11
above.

\Box

6.4
(Further) refined curvature estimate

The next proposition is the counterpart of Proposition I.3.4
in
[
T
]
(and also
[
Ts
]
’s Proposition 4.2) in our context.
It improves the estimate for
|F_{A}^{-}|

given in Proposition
5.7
(and Lemma

6.8
) over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}
by eliminating the coefficient
\varepsilon^{\prime}
therein, thereby making the first
terms in the RHS of (
5.35
) and (
5.18
)
coincide.

The aforementioned improvement is made possible by the following
consequence of the monotonicity formula. This is in turn the
counterpart of
[
T
]
’s Lemma I.3.5 in our context.

Lemma 6.13
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
, and let
\delta_{*}
be as in Proposition
6.11
. Then there exist positive constants
r_{0}
,
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}

with the following significance: for
r\geq r_{0}
, there is a smooth function
{\mathfrak{u}}
on
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}
,

0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}
, satisfying the following properties:

•

d^{*}d{\mathfrak{u}}\geq\zeta r\tilde{\sigma}
where
|\underline{\alpha}|\leq 2^{-1}
;

•

\Big|d^{*}d{\mathfrak{u}}\Big|\leq\zeta^{\prime}r\tilde{\sigma}
;

•

|{\mathfrak{u}}|\leq\zeta^{\prime}
.

In the above, the constants
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{0}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.

Proof.
Modify
the proof of Lemma I.3.5 in
[
T
]
as follows.

•

Replace the parameter
r

in
[
T
]
by
\tilde{r}=r\tilde{\sigma}
, and replace balls of the form
B\,(x,\rho_{*})
in
[
T
]
by their counterparts,

B\,(x,\tilde{\sigma}^{-1/2}(x)\,\rho_{*})
, in our context.

•

Replace all appearances of
\alpha
in
[
T
]
’s proof of
its Lemma I.3.5 by
\underline{\alpha}
. Replace the use of Lemmas I.3.6, I.3.8 in
[
T
]
by Corollary

6.12
above, taking
\delta=\delta_{*}
.

•

In Step 2 of the proof of Lemma I.3.5 in
[
T
]
, replace the
set of balls
\{B_{i}\}_{i}
therein by the set of balls
\Lambda^{v,\rho}
in the third
bullet of our Corollary

6.12
; with
v=1/2
, and
\rho=r^{-1/2}
as in

[
T
]
.

•

In Step 3 of the proof of Lemma I.3.5 in
[
T
]
, a function

u_{i}
is introduced for each element
B_{i}
in
\{B_{i}\}_{i}
. As
explained in the previous bullet, The
counterpart of
[
T
]
’s
B_{i}
is a typical element,
B\,(x_{k},\rho_{k})
, where
\rho_{k}=r^{-1/2}\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k})^{-1/2}
, in

\Lambda^{1/2,r^{-1/2}}
. To this we associate a function

{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}
on
X
, which plays the part of
[
T
]
’s
u_{i}
: In
parallel to the defining equation of
[
T
]
’s
u_{i}
in (I.3.32)
of
[
T
]
,
{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}
is defined to be the solution to:

\begin{cases}\,\,\frac{1}{2}d^{*}d{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}=r\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k})\,(s_{k}-\kappa_{k}\,{\mathfrak{s}}_{k});&\\
\,\,\text{$\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}$, ${\mathfrak{u}}_{k}$ has $Y_{i}$-end limit $0$.}&\end{cases}

where
s_{i}
,
{\mathfrak{s}}_{i}
,
\kappa_{i}
are defined as in
[
T
]
,
modified according to the rules set forth by the first two bullets
above. The argument in
[
T
]
leading to its (I.3.36) shows that

{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}
obeys:

|{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}|+|d{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}|\leq\zeta_{4}\,(r\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k}))^{-3/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,x_{k})^{-3}

(6.29)

where
\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot x_{k})\geq\zeta^{\prime\prime}\rho_{k}
.

•

Take

{\mathfrak{u}}=\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{1/2,r^{-1/2}}}\chi\big(8\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,x_{k})/\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k})\big)\,{\mathfrak{u}}_{k};\qquad{\mathfrak{u}}^{\prime}:=\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{1/2,r^{-1/2}}}\,{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}.

The arguments in Step 5 of
[
T
]
’s proof of its Lemma I.3.5 (again
modified according to the previously described rules) then confirm
the bound on
{\mathfrak{u}}
asserted in the last bullet of the lemma, and
also that:

\begin{split}&\text{$d^{*}d\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{\prime}\geq\zeta r\tilde{\sigma}$ where $|\underline{\alpha}|\leq 2^{-1}$;}\qquad\Big|d^{*}d\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{\prime}\Big|\leq\zeta^{\prime}r\tilde{\sigma}.\end{split}

(6.30)

Note that given
x
and
k
, the value of
\Big|d^{*}d\Big(\chi\big(8\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,x_{k})/\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k})\big)\,{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}-{\mathfrak{u}}_{k}\Big)\Big|
at
x

vanishes unless
k
belongs to the subset

\Lambda_{x}:=\{k\,|\,x_{k}\in B_{x}(x,3\tilde{\sigma}(x))-B(x,2\tilde{\sigma}(x)/5),\,k\in\Lambda^{1/2,r^{-1/2}}\}.

When
k\in\Lambda_{x}
, (
6.29
) may be used to bound this value by

\zeta_{4}^{\prime}\,(r\tilde{\sigma}(x))^{-3/2}\tilde{\sigma}(x)^{-5}
. Meanwhile, by Corollary

6.12

\Lambda_{x}
has at most
\zeta_{5}\,r\tilde{\sigma}(x)^{3}
elements. Together, these imply that

\big|\,d^{*}d\,{\mathfrak{u}}-d^{*}d\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{\prime}\big|\,(x)\leq\zeta_{6}\,(r\delta_{*}^{3})^{-3/2}r\tilde{\sigma}(x)
. Combined with (
6.30
), this
confirms the first two bullets of the lemma.
\Box

Here is the promised improved curvature estimate.

Proposition 6.14
.

Re-introduce the notations
\varpi=|\nu|-|\alpha|^{2}
and

-u=|\nu|-|\psi|^{2}
. Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
, and let
\delta_{*}
be as in
Proposition
6.11
. Then there exist
r
-independent constants
r_{0}
,

\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
such that for all
r\geq r_{0}
,

\begin{split}|F_{A}^{-}|&\leq 2^{-3/2}r\varpi+\zeta\tilde{\sigma}^{-2};\\
|F_{A}^{-}|&\leq 2^{-3/2}r\,(-u)+\zeta^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\quad\text{over
$X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*}}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{15}{4}$}.\end{split}

(6.31)

In the above, the constants
\zeta
,
\zeta^{\prime}
depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{0}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.

Proof.
Note that the two asserted inequalities are equivalent over

X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{0}}
by way of Proposition
5.5
; so it
suffices to establish either of them.

To begin, recall (
5.23
), and this time take
\varepsilon=4\zeta_{3}^{-2}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}r^{-1}|\nu|^{-1}\leq\zeta^{\prime\prime}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-3}
. Fix

\delta\geq\delta_{0}^{\prime}
, where
\delta_{0}^{\prime}
is as in Proposition

5.7
, and let

\varepsilon_{\delta}:=\zeta^{\prime\prime}r^{-1}\delta^{-3}
. Introduce
the following variant of the function
q_{0}
in (
5.26
):

q_{0}=q_{0,\delta}:=\textsc{s}+(2^{-3/2}+\varepsilon_{\delta})\,r\,u+\zeta_{2}r|\beta|^{2}.

By Propositions

5.7
,
5.4
,
5.5
, it satisfies the following variant of
(
5.40
) and (
5.39
):

\begin{split}\big(\frac{d^{*}d}{2}+\frac{r|\psi|^{2}}{4}\big)q_{0,\delta}&\leq\zeta_{0}r\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-1};\\
q_{0,\delta}&\leq\zeta(\ln r)^{-1}r\,(-u)_{+}+\zeta^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$}.\end{split}

Combine this and Lemma

6.13
to find a constant
\zeta_{1}
that is independent of
r
,
\delta
, and
(A,\Psi)
, such
that with
{\mathfrak{q}}_{0,\delta}:=q_{0,\delta}-\zeta_{1}\delta^{-2}{\mathfrak{u}}
,
one has for
\delta\geq\delta_{*}
:

\begin{split}&\big(\frac{d^{*}d}{2}+\frac{r|\psi|^{2}}{4}\big)\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{0,\delta}\leq\zeta_{0}^{\prime}r\,\delta^{-2}|\psi|^{2};\\
&\qquad{\mathfrak{q}}_{0,\delta}\leq\zeta\,(\ln r)^{-1}r\,(-u)_{+}+\zeta^{\prime\prime}\delta^{-2}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}$. }\\
\end{split}

Together with (
5.41
) and the last bullet of Lemma

6.13
,
this in turn leads to the existence of a constant
\zeta_{1}^{\prime}
(again
independent of
r
,
\delta
, and
(A,\Psi)
), such that
the function
{\mathfrak{q}}_{\delta}:={\mathfrak{q}}_{0,\delta}-\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\delta^{-2}

on
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
satisfies:

\begin{split}&\big(\frac{d^{*}d}{2}+\frac{r|\psi|^{2}}{4}\big)\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{\delta}\leq 0;\\
&{\mathfrak{q}}_{\delta}\leq\zeta\,(\ln r)^{-1}r\,(-u)_{+}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}$. }\\
&\text{$({\mathfrak{q}}_{\delta})_{+}$ is supported on $U\subset V$,}\end{split}

where
U
,
V
are compact spaces defined as in the proof of

5.7
.
Now repeat the remaining argument in the proof of Proposition

5.7
, with
{\mathfrak{q}}_{\delta}
playing the role of
q^{\prime}

therein. To give more details: We now have

\gamma_{a,\delta}{\mathfrak{q}}_{\delta}=q_{1}+q_{2},\quad 0\leq a\leq\frac{31}{8},

where
\gamma_{a,\delta}
is as in (
5.43
), and

q_{1}
,
q_{2}
respectively satisfy:

\big(\frac{d^{*}d}{2}+\frac{r|\nu|}{4}\big)\,q_{1}=\xi^{\prime}_{\delta}\,\,\,\text{ over $V$};\qquad q_{1}|_{\partial V}=0,

where

\xi^{\prime}_{\delta}=\frac{d^{*}d}{2}(\gamma_{a,\delta}{\mathfrak{q}}_{\delta})-\gamma_{a,\delta}\frac{d^{*}d{\mathfrak{q}}_{\delta}}{2};

and

\begin{split}&\big(\frac{d^{*}d}{2}+\frac{r|\psi|^{2}}{4}\big)\,q_{2}=-\frac{ru}{4}q_{1}\\
&\qquad q_{2}|_{\partial V}\leq 0.\\
\end{split}

(6.32)

The present version of
q_{1}
is bounded by

\begin{split}|q_{1}|&\leq\zeta_{2}^{\prime}(\ln r)^{-1}r\,(-u)_{+}(r\delta)^{-1/2}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\,X^{{}^{\prime}a+1}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta})}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\zeta_{2}(\ln r)^{-1}r\,(-u)_{+}(r\delta^{3})^{-1/2}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\,Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta})}.\\
\end{split}

Plugging this back in (
6.32
), we have:

\begin{split}&\frac{d^{*}d}{2}\,(q_{2})_{+}\leq\zeta_{3}^{\prime}(\ln r)^{-1}(r\tilde{\sigma})^{2}(r\delta)^{-1/2}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\,X^{{}^{\prime}a+1}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta})}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\zeta_{3}^{\prime}(\ln r)^{-1}(r\tilde{\sigma})^{2}(r\delta^{3})^{-1/2}e^{-\zeta_{g}(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\,Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta}-Z^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta})}\\
\\
&\qquad(q_{2})_{+}|_{\partial V}=0,\qquad 0\leq a\leq\frac{31}{8}.\\
\end{split}

Noting again that the Green’s function
G
for the differential
operator
\frac{d^{*}d}{2}
with
Dirichlet boundary condition on
V
satisfies:

|G(x,\cdot)|+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)\,|dG(x,\cdot)|\leq\zeta\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2},

this leads to the following bound for
q_{2}
over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{4\delta}\subset V
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{15}{4}
:

q_{2}\leq\zeta_{4}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}(\ln r)^{-1}r^{-1}\delta^{-4}.

Combing this with the previously obtained bound for
q_{1}
and the last bullet of Lemma

6.13
, we have:

|F_{A}|\leq 2^{-3/2}\,r\,(-u)_{+}+\zeta_{4}^{\prime}\delta^{-2}\quad\text{$X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{4\delta}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{15}{4}$},

when
\delta\geq\delta_{*}
, where
\zeta_{4}^{\prime}
is independent of

r
,
\delta
, and
(A,\Psi)
. Given a fixed
x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta}
, we take
\delta=\tilde{\sigma}(x)/2
in the preceding formula. This leads
to the second line of (
6.31
), which in turn implies the
first line.
\Box

Remark 6.15
.

The preceding improvement of Proposition
5.7
and and Lemma

6.8
) is not strictly necessary for the
proof of our main theorems. (Proposition
5.7
and/or Lemma

6.8
suffice for our
purposes). Similarly, Proposition I.3.4 in
[
T
]
improves the
curvature estimate in Proposition I.2.4 therein, but the proof of the
main Theorem of part I of
[
T
]
uses only the
latter. Nevertheless, the more precise estimate in

[
T
]
’s Proposition I.3.4 plays a role in part IV of
[
T
]
. As
the present article is the counterpart of part I of
[
T
]
,
Proposition
6.14
should be useful for further studies
on the relationship between the Seiberg-Witten-Floer theory and
ECH-type Floer theories.

6.6
Exponential decay

The purpose of this subsection is to establish the counterpart of
Lemma 6.1 in
[
Ts
]
(which is a variant of
Proposition I.4.4 in
[
T
]
) in our context, the upcoming Proposition

6.19
. The proof of this proposition makes use
of a corollary of the monotonicity formula, Lemma
6.18

below,
which is in turn the counterpart of Lemma I.4.6 in
[
T
]
.

Before embarking on the aforementioned tasks, we introduce a notion of
“
Y_{i}
-end limits” for functions over (subdomains of)

X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
(or more generally, sections of bundles over

X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
). The definition is parallel to the notion of

Y_{i}
-end limits for functions/sections over (subdomains of)
X
,
previously introduced in Section
1.4
(6).

Given
\delta>0
and
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
, let
\sigma_{i}:=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\nu_{i}^{-1}(0))
, and
Y_{i,\delta}:=\{y\in Y_{i}\,|\,\sigma_{i}\geq\delta\}
. In what
follows,
Y_{i,\delta}
is often identified with
Y_{i:\infty,\delta}:=Y_{i:\infty}\cap X_{\delta}
.
Observe that the admissibility of
\nu
implies the
following: Let
g
denote the metric on
X
and recall
{\mathfrak{i}}\colon\thinspace\coprod_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}[0,\infty)\times Y_{i}\to X-X_{c}^{\circ}
, the
isometry from Definition
1.4
. Let
{\bf Y}_{i,\delta,L}:={\mathfrak{i}}\big([L,\infty)\times Y_{i,\delta}\big)
when

i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
.
Given
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
and a small

\delta<1/8
, there is an
L=L_{\delta}>8
and a diffeomorphism

{\mathfrak{i}}_{\delta}\colon\thinspace[L,\infty)\times Y_{i,\delta}\to\hat{Y}_{i,L,\delta}:=X_{\delta}\,\cap\hat{Y}_{i,L}
, such that:

\begin{split}&\qquad{\bf Y}_{i,2\delta,L}\subset\hat{Y}_{i,L,\delta}\subset{\bf Y}_{i,\delta/2,L};\\
&\qquad{\mathfrak{i}}_{\delta}\big|_{{\bf Y}_{i,2\delta,L}}={\mathfrak{i}}\big|_{{\bf Y}_{i,2\delta,L}};\quad\text{{\small s}}_{i}=\pi_{\mathbb{R}}\circ{\mathfrak{i}}_{\delta},\\
&\|{\mathfrak{i}}_{\delta}^{*}\,g-{\mathfrak{i}}^{*}g\|_{C^{2}([l,\infty)\times Y_{i,\delta})}+\|{\mathfrak{i}}_{\delta}^{*}\,\nu-\pi_{Y}^{*}\,\nu_{\infty}\|_{C^{2}([l,\infty)\times Y_{i,\delta})}\leq\zeta e^{-\kappa_{i}\,l/2}\,\,\forall l\geq L,\end{split}

where
\kappa_{i}
is the constants from
(
4.29
),
\pi_{\mathbb{R}},\pi_{Y}
respectively denote
the projection to the first factor and the second factor of the
product
{\mathbb{R}}\times Y
, and
\zeta
is a constant depending only on the metric and

\nu
.
Let
{\rm V}
be a bundle over

\overline{X_{\delta}^{\prime\prime}}
(or its subdomains such as
\overline{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}}

or
\hat{Y}_{i,L}\cap\overline{X}_{\delta}
), and let

{\rm V}_{i}:={\rm V}\big|_{Y_{i:\infty,\delta}}
.
A section
q\in\Gamma({\rm V}\big|_{X^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}})
is said to have
q_{i}\in\Gamma({\rm V}_{i})
as a
Y_{i}
-end limit
if
q
extends to be defined over

\overline{X_{\delta}^{\prime\prime}}
with (the extended)
q\big|_{Y_{i:\infty,\delta}}=q_{i}
; and

\|{\mathfrak{i}}_{\delta}^{*}\,q-\pi_{Y}^{*}\,q_{i}\|_{C^{2}([l,\infty)\times Y_{i,\delta})}+\|{\mathfrak{i}}_{\delta}^{*}\,q-\pi_{Y}^{*}q_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{1}([l,\infty)\times Y_{i,\delta})}\to 0\quad\text{as $l\to\infty$.}

Let
{\rm d}_{\alpha}\,(\cdot):=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\alpha^{-1}(0))
. When

\alpha^{-1}(0)=\emptyset
, we set
{\rm d}_{\alpha}\,(\cdot)=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\alpha^{-1}(0))\equiv\infty
. Recall the
notation
\tilde{r}=r\tilde{\sigma}
. Let
\tilde{r}_{x}:=\tilde{r}(x)
and
\tilde{\sigma}_{x}=\tilde{\sigma}(x)
.

Lemma 6.18
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
, and
let
\delta_{*}
be as in be as in Proposition
6.11
.
There exist positive constants
r_{0}>8
,
c
,
c^{\prime}\geq 2^{5}\,c
,

\zeta_{0}
,
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}
,
that are independent of

r
and
(A,\Psi)
, such that for any
r\geq r_{0}
, there exists a function

h_{r}
over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}
, satisfying

(1)

d^{*}d\,h_{r}+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|h_{r}\geq-\zeta_{0}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma}^{3})^{1/2}/c^{\prime})
where
{\rm d}_{\alpha}\geq c\tilde{r}^{-1/2}
.

(2)

h_{r}\geq\zeta{\rm d}_{\alpha}^{-2}\exp\big(-\tilde{r}^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)

(3)

h_{r}\leq\zeta^{\prime}\tilde{r}\exp\big(-\tilde{r}^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)

where
{\rm d}_{\alpha}\geq c\tilde{r}^{-1/2}
.

(4)

h_{r}
has a
Y_{i}
-end limit, denoted
h_{i,r}
, for
every Morse end
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
. This is a function over
Y_{i,\delta_{*}}
, which satisfies 3-dimensional versions of (1)–(3) above.

In the above, the constants
c
,
c^{\prime}
,

\zeta_{0}
,
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}
depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (
4.15
), and
r_{0}
depends on

e
in addition to all the above.

Proof.
To construct
h_{r}
and to verify that it satisfies Items
(2) and (3) above, repeat the arguments in the proof of (and the
paragraph preceding) Lemma I.4.6 in
[
T
]
.
(
h_{r}
is called
h
in
[
T
]
.) Modify the arguments of

[
T
]
in like manner to what was done in the proof of Lemma

:

•

in

in

•

Use respectively

6.12
and Proposition

[
T
]
.

•

Associate to each

a function,

, on

. This function

is given by the formula in

]

d^{*}d\,H_{y}+\frac{r}{32}|\nu(y)|\,H_{y}\geq 0\qquad\text{where ${\rm d}_{\alpha}\geq c\tilde{r}_{y}^{-1/2}$.}

(6.33)

(Recall the relation between
|\nu|
and
\tilde{\sigma}
from
(
5.1
).) Choose
{\rm m}\geq 8
such that for any given

\Big||\nu|/|\nu(y)|-1\Big|<1/4
over
B(y,2{\rm m}^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}_{y})
.

(6.34)

Note that given
y\in X^{\prime\prime}_{\delta_{*}}
, the function
\Big|d^{*}d\Big(\chi\big({\rm m}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,y)/\tilde{\sigma}_{y}\big)\,H_{y}-H_{y}\Big)\Big|
is supported on

(6.35)

•

Let
\Lambda_{r}:=\Lambda^{-r^{1/2}}=\{B\,(x_{k},\rho_{k})\}_{k}
denote the version of
\Lambda^{\rho}
from Corollary

6.12
with

and

. Set

Items (2) and (3) asserted by
the lemma follow from the arguments in the proof of
[
T
]
’s
Lemma I.4.6, modified as instructed in the first two bullets above.

To verify that
h_{r}
satisfies Item (1) of the lemma, argue as in
the last bullet in the proof of Lemma
6.13
: By
(

), (

), (

), and Corollary

,

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d\,h_{r}+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|h_{r}\big)(x)\,\\
&\qquad\geq-\zeta^{\prime}_{\rm m}\,r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{3}\sum_{k\in\Lambda_{r}\cap B(x,3\tilde{\sigma}_{x})}\tilde{r}_{x_{k}}^{1/2}\tilde{\sigma}_{x_{k}}^{-3}\exp\big(-\tilde{r}_{x_{k}}^{1/2}(2{\rm m}c)^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}_{x_{k}}\big),\\
&\qquad\geq-\zeta_{\rm m}^{\prime\prime}\,\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma}_{x}^{3})^{1/2}(4{\rm m}c)^{-1}\big)\quad\text{where ${\rm d}_{\alpha}\geq c\tilde{r}_{x}^{-1/2}$,}\end{split}

when
x\in X^{\prime\prime}_{\delta_{*}}
and

is sufficiently large.

Lastly, with Items (1)-(3) now confirmed, Item (4) follows from the admissibility assumption on
(A,\Psi)
and the standard compactness/properties results in

]
’s Section 10.7).

Proposition 6.19
.

Adopt the assumptions and notation of Lemma
4.6
, and let
\delta_{*}
be as in Proposition
6.11
.
There exist positive constants
r_{0}>8
,

\zeta_{i}
,
\zeta_{i}^{\prime}
,
i=1,2,3
, and
c
that are independent of

r
, and
(A,\Psi)
, with the following
significance: Suppose

, Then over

,

,

\displaystyle r|\varpi|+|F_{A}|\leq\zeta_{2}r\tilde{\sigma}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2};

(6.36)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}+r\tilde{\sigma}^{2}|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}\leq\zeta_{3}\,r\tilde{\sigma}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)+\zeta_{3}^{\prime}\,r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-5};

(6.37)

\displaystyle|\beta|^{2}\leq\zeta_{1}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\exp\,(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c)+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}r^{-2}\tilde{\sigma}^{-5}.

(6.38)

In the above, the constants

,

\zeta_{i},\zeta^{\prime}_{i}
depend only on
\nu
and the
parameters listed in (

), and

depends on

in addition to all the above.

Proof.
Return to the beginning of the proof of Proposition
5.9
.

Fix
\delta
with
1\geq\delta\geq 2\delta_{0}^{\prime}
.
Use (
5.44
)
to find constants
\zeta_{0}
,
\zeta_{1}^{\prime}
,
\zeta_{1}
that are independent of

,
\delta

and
(A,\Psi)
, such that the function

\mu=\mu_{\delta}:=\delta^{-2}\,|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}+\zeta_{0}\,r\,|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}

satisfies

\begin{split}\Big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{8}|\nu|\Big)\,\mu_{\delta}&\leq\zeta_{1}r\varpi_{+}\mu_{\delta}\,+\zeta_{2}\delta^{-1}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-7}\\
&\quad+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-6}+\zeta_{3}\delta^{-2}r\tilde{\sigma}^{-3}\varpi^{2}+\zeta_{3}^{\prime}r\tilde{\sigma}^{-4}\varpi_{+}\end{split}

(6.39)

over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}
. In particular, this implies that for all sufficiently large
r
and

\delta\geq 2\delta^{\prime}_{0}
,

\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,\mu_{\delta}\leq\zeta^{\prime}r\delta^{-3}\quad\quad\text{over
$X_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}-X_{\delta,v}^{{}^{\prime}a}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{39}{8}$,}

(6.40)

where
v=1-(9\zeta_{1})^{-1}
and
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,v}:=\{y\,|\,|\underline{\alpha}|(y)\leq v,\,y\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}\}
as in Corollary
6.12
.

The following observation will be useful.
Given
z>0
, let

U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,z}:=\{y\,|\,\,{\rm d}_{\alpha}\,(y)\geq z\,\tilde{r}^{-1/2},y\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}\}.

According to Lemma
6.17
,

X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,v}\supset U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,z_{v}}
, where

is the constant from

6.17
.
These noted,
(
6.40
) implies:

\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,\mu_{\delta}<\zeta^{\prime}r\delta^{-3}\qquad\text{over $U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,z_{v}}$. }

(6.41)

Note that by Propositions
5.9
and
5.5
, there is a positive
constant
\zeta_{4}
independent of
