,
\delta
,
(A,\Psi)
, such that

\begin{split}\mu_{\delta}&\leq\zeta_{4}\,r\delta^{-1}\quad\text{over
$X_{\delta};\quad$ in particular, }\\
\mu_{\delta}&\leq\zeta_{4}\,e^{z/c}\,r\delta^{-1}\exp\big(-\tilde{r}^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)\quad\text{over
$X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}-U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,z}$ for arbitrary $c,z>0$.}\end{split}

(6.42)

Now take
c
to be the constant from Lemma

6.18
, and set
z=\max\,(c,z_{v})
.
Then (
6.41
), (
6.42
), Lemma

6.18
can be used to find constants
\zeta_{5}
,
\zeta_{5}^{\prime}
, which are independent of
r
,
\delta
, and
(A,\Psi)
, so that:

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,(\mu_{\delta}-\zeta_{5}\,\delta^{-2}h_{r}-\zeta_{5}^{\prime}\delta^{-4})\leq 0\quad\text{over $U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,z}$;}\\
&\quad\mu_{\delta}-\zeta_{5}\,\delta^{-2}h_{r}\leq 0\quad\text{over $X_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}-U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,z}$}.\end{split}

(6.43)

To be able to apply the maximum principle to (
6.43
), we
still need another comparison function,

q_{1}
below, in order to offset the positive values of
\mu_{\delta}\big|_{\partial U_{\delta,z}}
along the
(\partial X_{\delta})\,\cap U_{\delta,z}
part of
\partial\,U_{\delta,z}
.

Recall the definition of
{\mathscr{X}}^{a}_{\delta}\subset X_{\delta}^{{}^{\prime}a}

and
\gamma_{a,\delta}
from the beginning of Section
5
.
Given any
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
, let
\mu_{\delta,i}
denote the

Y_{i}
-end limit of
\mu_{\delta}
, and let
q_{1,i}
be the function on
Y_{i,\delta}
solving the following Dirichlet boundary value problem:

\begin{split}\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu_{i}|\big)\,q_{1,i}&=d^{*}d\big((1-\chi(\sigma_{i}/\delta))\,\mu_{\delta,i}\big)-(1-\chi(\sigma_{i}/\delta))\,d^{*}d\,\mu_{\delta,i};\\
q_{1,i}\big|_{\partial Y_{i,\delta}}&=0.\end{split}

(6.44)

Then
q_{1}
is defined to be the function on
{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta}
,
0\leq a\leq\frac{31}{8}
, solving:

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,q_{1}=d^{*}d\big(\gamma_{a,\delta}\,\mu_{\delta}\big)-\gamma_{a,\delta}\,d^{*}d\,\mu_{\delta};\\
&\qquad\qquad q_{1}\big|_{\partial{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta}}=0;\\
&\text{$\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$, $\,\,q_{1,i}$ is the $Y_{i}$-end limit
of $q_{1}$. }\end{split}

(6.45)

Let

\begin{split}{\rm u}_{\delta}&:=\gamma_{a,\delta}\,\mu_{\delta}-q_{1}-\zeta_{5}\,\delta^{-2}h_{r}-\zeta_{5}^{\prime}\,\delta^{-4};\\
{\rm u}_{i,\delta}&:=(1-\chi(\sigma_{i}/\delta))\,\mu_{\delta,i}-q_{1,i}-\zeta_{5}\,\delta^{-2}h_{i,r}-\zeta_{5}^{\prime}\,\delta^{-4},\quad i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}.\end{split}

Then (
6.45
) and (
6.43
) together imply:

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,{\rm u}_{\delta}\leq 0\quad\text{over
${\mathscr{X}}_{\delta}^{a+1}\cap U^{a+1}_{\delta,z}$ ;}\\
&\qquad{\rm u}_{\delta}<0\qquad\text{over $\,\partial\,({\mathscr{X}}_{\delta}^{a+1}\cap U^{a+1}_{\delta,z})$};\\
&\text{$\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$, ${\rm u}_{i,\delta}$ is the
$Y_{i}$-end limit of ${\rm u}_{\delta}$,}\end{split}

while
{\rm u}_{i,\delta}
satisfies:

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu_{i}|\big)\,{\rm u}_{i,\delta}\leq 0\quad\text{over $U_{i,\delta,z}$;}\\
&\quad{\rm u}_{i,\delta}\big|_{\partial(U_{i,\delta,z})}<0\quad\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m},\end{split}

where
U_{i,\delta,z}\subset Y_{i,\delta}
is the 3-dimensional
analog of
U^{a+1}_{\delta,z}
:

U_{i,\delta,z}:=\{y\,|\,\,{\rm d}_{\alpha_{i}}\,(y)\geq z\,(r\tilde{\sigma}_{i}(y))^{-1/2},y\in Y_{i,\delta}\}
, and
\overline{U^{a+1}_{\delta,z}}\cap Y_{i:\infty,\delta}=U_{i,\delta,z}
.
Thus, according to the maximum principle,

\begin{split}\sup_{U_{i,\delta,z}}{\rm u}_{i,\delta}&\leq 0;\\
\sup_{{\mathscr{X}}_{\delta}^{a+1}\cap U^{a+1}_{\delta,z}}{\rm u}_{\delta}&\leq\max_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\,(\sup_{U_{i,\delta,z}}{\rm u}_{i,\delta})\leq 0,\end{split}

Together with the second line of (
6.43
), this implies in
particular that

\mu_{\delta}\leq|q_{1}|+\zeta_{5}\,\delta^{-2}h_{r}+\zeta_{5}^{\prime}\,\delta^{-4}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{2\delta}\subset{\mathscr{X}}_{\delta}^{a+1}$.}

(6.46)

We next give a pointwise bound for
|q_{1}|
, beginning by
estimating its
Y_{i}
-end limits,
|q_{1,i}|
.
First, note that the (Dirichlet) Green’s function for the elliptic
system (
6.44
), denoted
G_{i,r}
, satisfies a bound of the form:

\begin{split}|G_{i,r}(x,\cdot)|&+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)\,|dG_{i,r}(x,\cdot)|\\
&\leq{\rm c}_{i}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-1}\exp\,\big(-(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)/{\rm c}_{i}\big),\end{split}

where
{\rm c}_{i}
is a constant depending only on the metric and

\nu_{i}
. Multiply both sides of (
6.44
) by
G_{i,r}
and integrate over
Y_{i,\delta}
. The preceding bounds for
G_{i,r}
and
dG_{i,r}
,
together with the (
Y_{i}
-end limit of) the first line in (
6.42
) then leads to:

\begin{split}|q_{1,i}|\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{i}r^{-1/2}\delta^{-5/2}\quad\text{over
$Y_{i,\delta}$},\\
\end{split}

(6.47)

where
\zeta^{\prime}_{i}
is a constant independent of
r
,
\delta
,
and
(A,\Psi)
. Moreover, using (the
Y_{i}
-end limit of the bound from)
Proposition
5.9
and (
3.23
),
one has for all sufficiently large
r
and
\delta\geq\delta_{*}
:

\begin{split}|q_{1,i}|&\leq\zeta\delta^{-2}\sigma_{i}^{-1}\exp\,\big(-(2{\rm c}_{i})^{-1}(r\delta)^{1/2}\sigma_{i}\big)\int_{A_{i,\delta}}\mu_{\delta,i}\\
&\leq\zeta^{\prime}\delta^{-2}\sigma_{i}^{-1}\exp\,\big(-(2{\rm c}_{i})^{-1}(r\delta)^{1/2}\sigma_{i}\big)\,\delta^{-3}\\
&\leq\zeta^{\prime\prime}\,\exp\,\big(-(r\delta^{3})^{1/2}/{\rm c}_{i}\big)\quad\text{over $Y_{i,4\delta}$},\end{split}

(6.48)

where
A_{i,\delta}
denotes the support of
d\chi(\sigma_{i}/\delta))
, and
\zeta^{\prime\prime}
is a constant independent of
r
,
\delta
,
and
(A,\Psi)
.

These
q_{1,i}
being the
Y_{i}
-end limits of

q_{1}
, one may find an
L>8
, such that

\begin{split}&|q_{1}-({\mathfrak{i}}_{\delta}^{-1}\circ\pi_{Y})^{*}q_{1,i}|\leq r^{-1}\quad\text{over
$\hat{Y}_{i,L,\delta}$ $\forall\,i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$, and hence by
(\ref {bdd:q_1i}), }\\
&|q_{1}|\leq\zeta_{7}r^{-1}\quad\text{over
$\hat{Y}_{i,L,4\delta}$ $\forall\,i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$.}\end{split}

(6.49)

In the above,
\zeta_{7}
is a constant independent of
r
,

\delta
, and
(A,\Psi)
(though
L
may be
dependent). Let
{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta,L}\subset{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta}
be a manifold with boundary with
{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta}-\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\hat{Y}_{i,L+2,\delta}\subset{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta,L}\subset{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta}-\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\hat{Y}_{i,L+3,\delta}
, and let
\lambda_{L}
be a smooth cutoff function supported on

{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta,L}
which equals 1 over
{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta}-\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\hat{Y}_{i,L+1,\delta}
. By (
6.45
), the function
\lambda_{L}q_{1}
now solves the Dirichlet
boundary valued problem:

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,(\lambda_{L}q_{1})=d^{*}d\big(\gamma_{a,\delta}\,\mu_{\delta}\big)-\gamma_{a,\delta}\,d^{*}d\,\mu_{\delta}+d^{*}d\,(\lambda_{L}q_{1})-\lambda_{L}d^{*}dq_{1};\\
&\qquad\qquad(\lambda_{L}q_{1})\big|_{\partial{\mathscr{X}}^{a+1}_{\delta,L}}=0.\\
\end{split}

The Green’s function for the preceding elliptic system satisfies a
bound of the form:

\begin{split}|G_{r}(x,\cdot)|&+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)\,|dG_{r}(x,\cdot)|\\
&\leq c_{0}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-2}\exp\,\big(-(r\delta)^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)/c_{0}\big),\end{split}

where
c_{0}
is a constant depending only on the metric and
\nu
. As usual, multiply both sides of the first line in (
6.45
)
by
G_{r}
and integrate over
{\mathscr{X}}_{\delta}^{a+1}
.
Integration by parts then gives:

\begin{split}|q_{1}|&\leq\zeta_{1}\delta^{-2}\sigma^{-2}\exp\,\big(-(2c_{0})^{-1}(r\delta)^{1/2}\sigma\big)\int_{X^{{}^{\prime}a+1}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a+1}_{2\delta}}\mu_{\delta}\\
&\qquad+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\operatorname{\text{{\small s}}}_{*}^{-2}\exp\,\big(-(r\delta)^{1/2}\operatorname{\text{{\small s}}}_{*}/c_{0}\big)\int_{X^{{}^{\prime}a+1}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}}\mu_{\delta}\\
&\qquad+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\operatorname{\text{{\small s}}}_{L}^{-2}\exp\,\big(-(r\delta)^{1/2}\operatorname{\text{{\small s}}}_{L}/c_{0}\big)\int_{\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}(\hat{Y}_{i,L+1,\delta}-\hat{Y}_{i,L+2,\delta})}|q_{1}|\quad\end{split}

over
X^{{}^{\prime}a-\frac{1}{8}}_{4\delta}-\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\hat{Y}_{i,L,\delta}
for all sufficiently large
r
and
\delta\geq\delta_{*}
, where
\operatorname{\text{{\small s}}}_{*}\geq 1
,
\operatorname{\text{{\small s}}}_{L}\geq 1
respectively
denote distances to
X^{{}^{\prime}a+1}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}
and
\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}(\hat{Y}_{i,L+1,\delta}-\hat{Y}_{i,L+2,\delta})
; and
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{1}^{\prime}
,
\zeta_{2}^{\prime}
are constants
independent of
r
,
\delta
,
L
, and
(A,\Psi)
. Use Propositions
5.5
,

5.9
, Lemma

6.2
and its amendments in Lemmas
6.1
,

6.9
to estimate the first two integrals in the
preceding expression, and use (
6.47
), (
6.49
) to
bound the last integral. One has:

\begin{split}|q_{1}|&\leq\zeta_{7}^{\prime}\exp\,\big(-(r\delta^{3})^{1/2}/{\rm c}_{0}\big)\\
&\leq\zeta_{8}r^{-1}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a-\frac{1}{8}}_{4\delta}-\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\hat{Y}_{i,L,4\delta}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{31}{8},$ }\end{split}

for all sufficiently large
r
and
\delta\geq\delta_{*}
, where
\zeta_{7}^{\prime}
,
\zeta_{8}
are constants
independent of
r
,
\delta
,
L
, and
(A,\Psi)
.
Combine the preceding bound with the second line of (
6.49
), (
6.46
), the second line of (
6.43
), Item (3) of
Lemma
6.18
, and the second line of (
6.42
) to see that

\begin{split}&\delta^{-2}|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}+\zeta_{0}\,r\,|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}=\mu_{\delta}\\
&\quad\leq\zeta_{5}\,\delta^{-2}h_{r}+\zeta_{6}^{\prime}\,\delta^{-4}\\
&\quad\leq\zeta_{6}\,r\delta^{-1}\exp\big(-(r\delta)^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)+\zeta_{6}^{\prime}\,\delta^{-4}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{4\delta}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{15}{4}$.}\end{split}

Fix
x\in X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*}}
and take
\delta=\tilde{\sigma}(x)
in the preceding
expression. It follows from Item (3) of Lemma
6.18
that

\begin{split}&|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}+\zeta_{0}\,r\tilde{\sigma}^{2}|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}/4\\
&\qquad\leq\zeta_{5}\,h_{r}+\zeta_{6}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\quad\\
&\qquad\leq\zeta_{6}\,\tilde{r}\exp\big(-\tilde{r}^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)+\zeta_{6}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*}}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{15}{4}$}\end{split}

(6.50)

where
\zeta_{6}
,
\zeta_{6}^{\prime}
are constants
independent of
r
,
\delta
,
L
, and
(A,\Psi)
. Hold this for
the moment before we return to show
how this leads to (
6.37
).

To prove the remaining assertions of the proposition, (
6.36
)
and (
6.38
), it suffices to verify the bound for

|\varpi|
; in fact, we shall show:

|\varpi|\leq\zeta\tilde{\sigma}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)+\zeta^{\prime}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{16\,\delta_{*}}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{21}{8}$.}

(6.51)

Once (
6.51
) is established, the asserted bounds for
|F_{A}|
and
|\beta|^{2}
would follow from
Propositions
6.14
and
5.5
.

To verify (
6.51
), note that the argument for
(
5.12
) actually shows that the
absolute value
of the
left hand side of (
5.12
) is no greater than its right hand
side. Together with (
4.58
), Proposition

5.4
, and (
6.50
), this implies:

\begin{split}2^{-1}d^{*}d\,|\varpi|+4^{-1}r|\alpha|^{2}|\varpi|&\leq|\nu|\,|\nabla_{A}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}+\zeta_{1}^{\prime}|\nabla_{A}\beta|^{2}+\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}|\beta|^{2}+\zeta_{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\\
&\leq\zeta\tilde{\sigma}h_{r}+\zeta^{\prime}\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\quad\text{over
$X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*}}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{15}{4}$.}\end{split}

(6.52)

Meanwhile, by Proposition

5.4
,

\begin{split}|\varpi|&\leq|\nu|\quad\text{over
$X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}$;\quad in particular, }\\
|\varpi|&\leq\zeta_{4}\,e^{z/c}\,\tilde{\sigma}\exp\big(-\tilde{r}^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)\quad\text{over
$X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*}}-U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta_{*},z}$ for arbitrary $c,z>0$.}\end{split}

(6.53)

Recall that
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,v}\supset U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,z_{v}}
, where
z_{v}
is the constant from
Lemma
6.17
.
Let
z_{*}=\max\,(z,z_{1/4})
, where
z
is as in the paragraph
after (
6.42
), and
combine (
6.52
), (
6.53
) with Lemma
6.18
to
find constants
\zeta_{3}
,
\zeta_{3}^{\prime}
that are independent of
r
, and
(A,\Psi)
, so that:

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{8}|\nu|\big)\,\big(|\varpi|-\zeta_{3}\,r^{-1}h_{r}-\zeta_{3}^{\prime}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\big)\leq 0\,\,\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{over
$X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*}}-X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*},1/4}\supset X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*}}\cap U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*},z_{*}}$;}\\
&\quad|\varpi|-\zeta_{3}\,r^{-1}h_{r}\leq 0\quad\text{over $X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*}}-U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{8\delta_{*},z_{*}}$, $0\leq a\leq\frac{15}{4}$}.\end{split}

(6.54)

Noting that (
6.54
), (
6.53
) are respectively
parallel to (
6.43
) and (
6.42
), a
repetition of the arguments following (
6.43
) leads to
(
6.51
).

We now return to the verification of (
6.37
). Given
(
6.50
), it suffices to verify (
6.37
) over the
region where
\tilde{r}\exp\big(-\tilde{r}^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)\leq\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}
. That is, where
{\rm d}_{\alpha}\geq\frac{c}{\tilde{r}}\ln(\tilde{r}\tilde{\sigma}^{2})
. Denote this region in
X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{16\delta_{*}}
,

0\leq a\leq\frac{21}{8}
by

V_{r}
. Reconsider the inequality
(
6.39
), and the arguments that follow. Armed with (
6.52
), (
6.41
)
may be improved as:

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,\mu_{\delta}<\zeta^{\prime}_{1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-6}+\zeta_{4}^{\prime}r\tilde{\sigma}^{-1}\delta^{-2}\exp\big(-2(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)\\
&\qquad+\zeta_{5}^{\prime}r\tilde{\sigma}^{-3}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}{\rm d}_{\alpha}/c\big)\quad\text{over $U^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\delta,z_{v}}$. }\end{split}

In particular,

\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,\mu_{\delta}<\zeta^{\prime}_{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{-6}\quad\text{over $V_{r}$. }

Noting that over
\partial V_{r}
,
\mu_{\delta}\leq\zeta\delta^{-2}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}
and appealing to Lemma

6.18
again, we have the following analog of (
6.43
):

\begin{split}&\big(d^{*}d+\frac{r}{16}|\nu|\big)\,(\mu_{\delta}-\zeta_{6}\,\delta^{-2}h_{r}-\zeta_{6}^{\prime}r^{-1}\delta^{-7})\leq 0\quad\text{over $V_{r}$;}\\
&\quad\mu_{\delta}-\zeta_{5}\,\delta^{-2}h_{r}\leq 0\quad\text{over $\partial V_{r}$}.\end{split}

Repeating the arguments following (
6.43
) with straightforward
modifications then leads to (
6.37
).

\Box

7
Proofs of the main theorems

The section brings together the intermediate results obtained in
Sections 4-6 above to prove the theorems claimed in Section 1. In
this section, the parameter
a
in
X^{{}^{\prime}a}
is set to satisfy
0\leq a\leq 3/2
.

7.1
Proving Theorem
1.8

Let
\text{{\small c}}_{r}:=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
,
r\in\{r_{n}\}_{n}=:\Gamma
be as in the statement of
Theorem
1.8
. As observed before, in this case by Lemma
4.3
, both inequalities
in (
4.13
) in Lemma
4.6
hold with
the constant
e
determined by (
4.8
) via
(
4.11
). In this section, we shall repeated invoke this fact in
combination with other results in previous sections, sometimes
implicitly.

Proof of Theorem
1.8
.

(a):
To find the desired t-curve
\mathbf{C}
on
X^{{}^{\prime}a}
, associate
to each
(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
a 2-current
\mathcal{F}_{r}
on
X^{{}^{\prime}a}

as follows. Given a compactly supported smooth
2-form
\mu
on
X^{{}^{\prime}a}
, let

\mathcal{F}_{r}(\mu):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}}\frac{i}{2\pi}(F_{A_{r}}-F_{A_{K}})\wedge\mu,

where
F_{A_{K}}
is the curvature form of the canonical connection on

K^{-1}
. (As observed in
[
Ts
]
, the 2-form
iF_{A_{K}}
is
singular at
Z:=\nu^{-1}(0)
, but for any compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{\prime\prime}
, the
integral
\int_{X_{\bullet}}iF_{A_{K}}\wedge\mu
is still finite because

|F_{A_{K}}|\leq\zeta_{X_{\bullet}}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,Z)^{-2}
near
Z\cap X_{\bullet}
).
In fact, by our definition of MCE,

\|F_{A_{K}}\|_{L^{1}(X_{\bullet})}\leq\zeta_{K}|X_{\bullet}|.

(7.1)

In the above,
\zeta_{K}
is a positive
constant that depends only on the metric on
X
and
\nu
. Together with
(
6.11
) and Lemma
4.3
, this implies that there is a positive constant

\zeta_{E}
that depends only on

\hss\left\{\mkern-20.0mu\parbox{310.4979pt}{\begin{itemize}\par\itemize@item@the metric and $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}\nolimits^{c}$-structure on
$X$,
\par\itemize@item@the relative homology class ${\rm h}(\text{{\scriptsize h}})\in{\mathscr{H}}\big((X,\nu),\{\tilde{\gamma}_{i}\}_{i}\big)$ (which in turn depends
implicitly on $\{\tilde{\gamma}_{i}\}_{i}$,
\par\itemize@item@$\nu$, $\varsigma_{w}$, $\textsl{z}_{\mathfrak{p}}$, \par\end{itemize}}\right.

(7.2)

such that

\|F_{A_{r}^{E}}\|_{L^{1}(X_{\bullet})}\leq\zeta_{E}\,(|X_{\bullet}|+1).

(7.3)

Thus, by
Alaoglu’s theorem, there is a subsequence of
\{\mathcal{F}_{r}\}_{r}

which converges on
X_{\bullet}
. A diagonalization
argument then shows that a subsequence,
\Gamma_{0}
, of the former converges to a
current
\mathcal{F}
on
X^{{}^{\prime}a}
. Abusing notation, this subsequence
is also denoted by
\{\mathcal{F}_{r}\}_{r}
. With this done, the arguments in
Sections 7(b)-(d) and 7(f) in
[
Ts
]
carry through directly, with
Lemma 4.1 therein substituted by our Corollary

6.12
. This gives the t-curve
\mathbf{C}

and proves the first claim of the theorem regarding t-convergence.

(b):
By definition,

{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}(\mathbf{C})\leq{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}|_{X^{\prime}})+\sup_{X_{*}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}}{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}(\mathbf{C}|_{X_{*}}),

where
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}=\{X_{*}\,|\,X_{*}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a},|X_{*}|\leq 1\}
. Consequently, it suffices to bound

{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}})
and
{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}(\mathbf{C}|_{X_{*}})
.

We begin by bounding
{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}})
.
Fix a compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
.
Since
\{\mathcal{F}_{r}\}_{r}
converges to

\tilde{C}
as currents,

{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}|_{X_{\bullet}})=\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{1}{2}\int_{X_{\bullet}}\frac{i}{2\pi}(F_{A_{r}}-F_{A_{K}})\wedge\nu

(7.4)

Note that by (
7.1
) and (
4.29
),

\Big|\int_{X_{\bullet}}\frac{i}{2\pi}F_{A_{K}}\wedge\nu\Big|\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{K}

(7.5)

for a positive constant
\zeta^{\prime}_{K}
independent of

X_{\bullet}
. Combine this with (
7.4
) and Lemmas

6.7
,
4.3
to get

{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}|_{X_{\bullet}})\leq\zeta_{v},

where
\zeta_{v}>0
is independent of
X_{\bullet}
and
r
, and is determined
by the items listed in (
7.2
). This implies that

{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}})\leq\zeta_{v}

as well.

Fix
X_{*}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}
. We now proceed to bound
{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}(\mathbf{C}|_{X_{*}})
.
By the convergence of
\{\mathcal{F}_{r}\}_{r}
again,

{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}(\mathbf{C}|_{X_{*}})=\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{1}{2}\int_{X_{*}}\frac{i}{2\pi}(F_{A_{r}}-F_{A_{K}})\wedge\omega

(7.6)

Combine this with (
7.1
), Lemma
4.3
, Lemma
4.16
and its
amendment in Proposition

6.4
, Lemma
4.16
, we have

{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}(\mathbf{C}|_{X_{*}})\leq\zeta_{v}^{\prime}

for any
X_{*}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}
. Like
\zeta_{v}
,
\zeta_{v}^{\prime}
only depends
on the items listed in (
7.2
). Together with the bound

{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}})\leq\zeta_{v}
, this implies Assertion (b) of
Theorem
1.8
.
\Box

7.2
SW and Gr versions of Floer-theoretic energies

Compactness
results in Floer theory typically hinge on bounds on certain
“topological energy”, whose value only depends on the relative
homotopy class of the solution. In Morse theory, this is just the
change of the value of the Morse function along the gradient flow.
In the Seiberg-Witten setting, the
relevant notion of topological energy is
{\mathscr{E}}_{top}

in
[
KM
]
(cf. e.g. Equation (5.1) of
[
KM
]
). For

Gr
-variants of Floer theories (such as ECH/PFH, SFT) that do
not really have a Morse-theoretic model, local convergence and global
convergence are respectively controlled by
{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}
and

{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}
. In Theorem
1.8
, we obtained a bound on

{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}
of the limiting t-curve
\mathbf{C}
. For
convenience of later discussions, rename this
\mathbf{C}
as

\mathbf{C}_{0}
.
Before moving on to the proof of Theorem

1.10
, we need to better understand the relation between

{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}_{0})
and its counterpart on the SW side,

{\mathscr{E}}_{top}
. The two are related via the quantity:

{\mathscr{V}}_{r}(X_{\bullet}):=\int_{X_{\bullet}}\frac{i}{2\pi}F_{A^{E}_{r}}\wedge\nu=\int_{X_{\bullet}}\frac{i}{4\pi}F_{A_{r}}\wedge\nu-\int_{X_{\bullet}}\frac{i}{4\pi}F_{A_{K}}\wedge\nu.

Let

{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(X_{\bullet}):=\frac{1}{\pi r}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X_{\bullet})(A_{r},\Psi_{r})-\int_{X_{\bullet}}\frac{iF_{A_{K}}}{4\pi}\wedge\nu.

It is easier to compare averaged versions of the three notions
of energy,
{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}
,
{\mathscr{V}}_{r}
,
{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}
, defined as
follows. Suppose
X_{\bullet}
is of the following type:

X_{\bullet}=\begin{cases}\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]},&\text{ with $|\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}|\geq 2$, $l\geq 1/2$ or}\\
X_{\bf l}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}&\text{ ${\bf l}(i)\geq 1/2$ for every $i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$; ${\bf l}(i)=\textup{l}_{i}$ for $i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}$}.\end{cases}

(7.7)

Let

\begin{split}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})&:=\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l+t,L+t]})\,dt.\\
\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X_{\bf l})&:=\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}{\mathfrak{G}}_{r}(X_{{\bf l}+{\bf t}})\,dt,\end{split}

where
{\bf t}
is the function given by
{\mathfrak{t}}(i)=t

\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
;
{\bf t}(i)=0

\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}
.
Let
\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})
,
\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}|_{X_{\bullet}})
be similarly defined.
Thus defined,
\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}
(and also
\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}
,
\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}
) are
additive: Given
l<L<L^{\prime}
with
L-l
,
L^{\prime}-L
no less than 2, for

i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}

\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})+\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[L,L^{\prime}]})=\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L^{\prime}]});

given functions
{\bf l}\colon\thinspace{\mathfrak{Y}}\to[0,\infty]
,
{\bf L}\colon\thinspace{\mathfrak{Y}}\to[0,\infty]
such that
{\bf L}(i)-{\bf l}(i)\geq 2

\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
and that
X_{\bf L}
,
X_{\bf l}
are of the type
(
7.7
),

\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X_{\bf L})-\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X_{\bf l})=\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[{\bf l}(i),{\bf L}(i)]}).

From Theorem
1.8
we already know
that

\begin{split}&\lim_{r\to\infty}{\mathscr{V}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})={\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}_{0}|_{X_{\bullet}})\quad\text{for all
compact
$X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}$. }\\
&\lim_{r\to\infty}\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})=\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}_{0}|_{X_{\bullet}})\quad\text{for all
compact
$X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}$ of the type (\ref {ave-X}). }\end{split}

Meanwhile, by (
6.14
), Lemma
4.3
, Lemma

4.15
and its amendment in Proposition
6.4
, we
know that for all
X_{\bullet}
, the difference between
{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})-{\mathscr{V}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})
is bounded by a constant
independent of
r
and
X_{\bullet}
. Similarly for

\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})-\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})
. We now show that
the latter goes to 0 as
r\to\infty
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\{(A_{r},\Psi_{r})\}_{r\in\Gamma}
be as in the statement of Theorem

1.8
. Let
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
be of the type
(
7.7
).
Then there exists positive constants
R_{S}>1
,
\zeta_{S}
depending only on (
7.2
),
such that
for all
r\geq R_{S}
,

\Big|\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})-\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})\Big|\leq\zeta_{S}\,r^{-1/4}.

Proof.
We verify the preceding inequality only for the case

X_{\bullet}=X_{\bf l}
. The case when
X_{\bullet}=\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}
is
similar. Write
\partial\overline{X_{\bf l}}=\bigcup_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}Y_{i:l_{i}}
,
l_{i}=\textup{l}_{i}
when
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}
. Then

\pi\big(\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})-\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})\big)=r^{-1}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{w_{r}}(X_{\bullet})\,dt.

The right hand side of the preceding identity can be expressed as a
sum of three terms:

\begin{split}&\pi\big(\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})-\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(X_{\bullet})\big)\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{4r}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{X_{{\bf l}+{\bf t}}}F_{A_{0}}\wedge(F_{A_{0}}+iw_{r})\,dt\\
&\qquad-\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big(2\langle\psi,\mbox{\mbox{$\partial\mkern-7.5mu\mbox{/}\mkern 2.0mu$}}_{B}\psi\rangle+\partial_{s}|\nu|\big)\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{4r}\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}(B-B_{0})\wedge(F_{B}+F_{B_{0}}+iw_{r})\wedge ds.\\
\end{split}

(7.8)

These three terms are estimated as follows.

(i) For the first term on the RHS of (
7.8
),

\Big|\frac{1}{4r}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{X_{{\bf l}+{\bf t}}}F_{A_{0}}\wedge(F_{A_{0}}+iw_{r})\,dt\Big|\leq\zeta_{1}r^{-1},

where
\zeta_{1}
depends only on the choice of
A_{0}
and

w_{r}
. (In fact, it equals 0 by the assumptions (
3.3
) and
Assumption
3.4
(3).)

(ii) To estimate the second term on the RHS of (
7.8
), use
the Seiberg-Witten equation to rewrite:

\begin{split}&-\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big(2\langle\psi,\mbox{\mbox{$\partial\mkern-7.5mu\mbox{/}\mkern 2.0mu$}}_{B}\psi\rangle+\partial_{s}|\nu|\big)\\
&\quad\qquad=\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big(2\langle\psi,\partial_{s}\psi\rangle-\partial_{s}|\nu|\big)\\
\\
&\quad\qquad=\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\partial_{s}(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|).\end{split}

(7.9)

Let
\delta\geq\textsc{o}r^{-1/3}
, where
o
is as in Proposition

5.5
. Let
v_{r}:=(\textsl{z}_{v}\delta)^{-1}|\nu|
, where
\textsl{z}_{v}

is the positive
constant from (
5.1
).
In particular,
\sigma\geq\delta
where
v_{r}\geq 1
. Write

|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|=(1-\chi(v_{r}))\,(|\nu|\,(|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}-1)+|\beta|^{2})+\chi(v_{r})\,(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|).

We have:

\begin{split}&\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\partial_{s}(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|)\\
&\quad=\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\partial_{s}\big((1-\chi(v_{r}))\,(|\nu|\,(|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}-1)+|\beta|^{2})\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big(\chi(v_{r})\,(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|)\big)\\
&\quad=2\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big((1-\chi(v_{r}))\,\big(|\nu|\,\langle\underline{\alpha},\partial_{s}\underline{\alpha}\rangle+\langle\beta,\partial_{s}\beta\rangle\big)\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big((1-\chi(v_{r}))\partial_{s}|\nu|(|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}-1)\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad-(\textsl{z}_{v}\delta)^{-1}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big(\chi^{\prime}(v_{r})\,\partial_{s}|\nu|(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|)\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big(\chi(v_{r})(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|)\big).\end{split}

(7.10)

By Proposition
5.4
and the
assumptions on
\nu
, the last two terms above are bounded together
by

\begin{split}&(\textsl{z}_{v}\delta)^{-1}\Big|\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big(\chi^{\prime}(v_{r})\,\partial_{s}|\nu|(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|)\big)\Big|\\
&\quad\quad\qquad+\Big|\int_{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\big(\chi(v_{r})(|\psi|^{2}-|\nu|)\big)\Big|\\
&\quad\leq\zeta_{0}\,\delta^{3},\end{split}

(7.11)

where
\zeta_{0}
depends only on the metric,
\nu
,

\varsigma_{w}
,
\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
The other two terms on the RHS of the second equality of
(
7.10
) are together bounded by

\int_{\bar{Y}_{\delta}}\Big(2|\nu|\,|\underline{\alpha}|\,|\partial_{s}\underline{\alpha}|+2|\beta|\,|\partial_{s}\beta|+\Big|\partial_{s}|\nu|\Big|\,\Big||\underline{\alpha}|^{2}-1\Big|\Big),

where
\bar{Y}:=\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}
. Now take
\delta=2^{4}\delta_{*}\geq\textsc{o}r^{-1/3}
,
\delta_{*}
being as in Propositions
6.11
and

6.19
. The preceding
integral may be bounded using Proposition

6.19
by

\begin{split}&\int_{\bar{Y}_{\delta}}\left(\zeta_{1}\sqrt{r\tilde{\sigma}^{3}}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\alpha^{-1}(0))/(2c)\big)+\zeta^{\prime}r^{-1/2}\tilde{\sigma}^{-3/2}+\zeta^{\prime\prime}r^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-3}\right)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\zeta_{1}\int_{\bar{Y}_{\delta}}\Big(\sqrt{r\tilde{\sigma}^{3}}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\alpha^{-1}(0))/(2c)\big)\Big)+\zeta^{\prime}_{1}r^{-1/2},\end{split}

(7.12)

where
c
is the constant from Proposition

6.19
. To bound the first term on the RHS of
(
7.12
), first note that it vanishes when
\alpha^{-1}(0)=\emptyset
. Assume consequently that
\alpha^{-1}(0)\neq\emptyset
. Invoke the second bullet of Corollary
6.12
with

\rho=r^{-1/2}
to
cover
\alpha^{-1}(0)\,\cap\,X_{\delta}
by a set of balls,
\Lambda^{\rho}=\{B_{k}\}_{k}
, where
B_{k}
is centered at

x_{k}\in\alpha^{-1}(0)\,\cap\,X_{\delta}
, and is of radius

(\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k}))^{-1/2}\rho
. Let
B^{\prime}_{k}\supset B_{k}
denote the concentric
ball with twice the radius, and let
U=\bigcup_{k}B^{\prime}_{k}
. Define the
function
d_{k}(\cdot):=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,x_{k})
on
X
. Given
x\in X_{\delta}
, there exists
k_{x}\in\Lambda^{\rho}
such that

B_{k_{x}}
contains a point whose distance to
x
equals
\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\alpha^{-1}(0))
. Then

\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\alpha^{-1}(0))\geq d_{k_{x}}(x)-(\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k_{x}}))^{-1/2}\rho&\geq d_{k_{x}}(x)/2\\
&\geq(\tilde{\sigma}(x_{k}))^{-1/2}\rho\quad\forall x\in X_{\delta}-U.\end{split}

Let
\bar{\Lambda}^{\rho}:=\Lambda^{\rho}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l-1,l+1],\delta}}
. By bullet 2 of by Corollary
6.12
,

\bar{\Lambda}^{\rho}
has at most
\zeta r
elements. If
\bar{\Lambda}^{\rho}=\emptyset
, then
\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\alpha^{-1}(0))\geq 1/2
over
\bar{Y}_{\delta}
, and in this case

\begin{split}&\int_{\bar{Y}_{\delta}}\Big(\sqrt{r\tilde{\sigma}^{3}}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\alpha^{-1}(0))/(2c)\big)\Big)\leq\zeta_{2}^{\prime}(r\delta)^{-1/2}.\end{split}

Otherwise,
\bar{\Lambda}^{\rho}\neq\emptyset
, and
k_{x}\in\bar{\Lambda}^{\rho}

for any

x\in\bar{Y}_{\delta}
. In this case

\begin{split}&\int_{\bar{Y}_{\delta}}\Big(\sqrt{r\tilde{\sigma}^{3}}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\alpha^{-1}(0))/(2c)\big)\Big)\\
&\qquad\leq\int_{U\cap\bar{Y}_{\delta}}\sqrt{r\tilde{\sigma}^{3}}+\int_{\bar{Y}_{\delta}-U}\Big(\sqrt{r\tilde{\sigma}^{3}}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(\cdot,\alpha^{-1}(0))/(2c)\big)\\
&\qquad\leq\sum_{k\in\bar{\Lambda}^{\rho}}\int_{B_{k}^{\prime}}\sqrt{r\tilde{\sigma}^{3}}+\sum_{k\in\bar{\Lambda}^{\rho}}\int_{\bar{Y}_{\delta}-B_{k}^{\prime}}\sqrt{r\tilde{\sigma}^{3}}\exp\big(-(r\tilde{\sigma})^{1/2}d_{k}/(4c)\big)\\
&\qquad\leq\zeta_{2}\,(r\delta)^{-1/2}.\end{split}

Insert
the preceding bounds for the first integral on the right hand side of
(
7.12
), and combine with
Equations (
7.11
), (
7.10
) and (
7.9
),
setting
\delta=r^{-1/7}
, which is no less than
2^{4}\delta_{*}

when
r
is sufficiently large and when
\epsilon\leq 1/7
in the
definition of
\delta_{1,\epsilon}
. We then have:

\text{the second term on the RHS of (\ref {E-V:sum})}\leq\zeta_{6}\,r^{-3/7}.

(iii) To estimate the last term in (
7.8
), note that
F_{B}+F_{B_{0}}+iw_{r}
is exact by
Assumption
3.4
(2), and therefore

\begin{split}&\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}(B-B_{0})\wedge(F_{B}+F_{B_{0}}+iw_{r})\wedge ds\\
&\qquad=\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}(B^{\prime}-B_{i,0})\wedge(F_{B}+F_{B_{0}}+iw_{r})\wedge ds\end{split}

(7.13)

where
B^{\prime}(s)=A^{\prime}|_{Y_{i:s}}
, and
A^{\prime}=u\cdot A
is in the normalized
Coulomb-Neumann gauge on
\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}
.

Next, arguing as in the proof of Lemma

3.12
, one has:

\|A^{\prime}-A_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]})}\leq\zeta_{2}r^{3/4},

(7.14)

where
\zeta_{3}

depends only on the parameters in (
4.15
),
\nu
, and

e
.

To see this, let
\Omega^{\prime}
denote the space of 1-forms
a
on
\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}
satisfying the conditions
\iota_{\partial s}a=0
over
\partial(\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]})
and
c_{k}(a):=\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}q_{k}\wedge a=0
, where
q_{k}=d(\chi_{+}(s)*_{3}h_{k})
,
k=1,\ldots,b^{1}(Y_{i})
, are as chosen in Definition
4.14
. Note that the operator
d+d^{*}\colon\thinspace\Omega^{\prime}\to B^{2}\oplus\Omega^{0}
has a Green’s function
G(x,y)
that satisfies
the inequality

|G(x,y)|\leq\zeta_{g}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,y)^{-3}
, where
B^{2},\Omega^{0}

are respectively the spaces of exact 2-forms and smooth functions on
\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}
, and
\zeta_{g}
is a positive constant that
only depends on the metric on
Y_{i}
and the choice of
q_{k}
. Let

q_{k}^{*}:=\pi_{2}^{*}h_{k}
, where
h_{k}
are as in Definition

4.14
and (
3.10
). Write
A^{\prime}-A_{0}=\operatorname{\hat{a}}+\sum_{k}c_{k}(A^{\prime}-A_{0})\,q_{k}^{*}
, where

\operatorname{\hat{a}}\in i\Omega^{\prime}
.
Then for
x\in\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}
,

\begin{split}|\operatorname{\hat{a}}(x)|&\leq\zeta\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-3}\big(|F_{A}|+|F_{A_{0}}|\big)\\
&\leq\zeta\|F_{A}\|_{L^{\infty}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]})}\int_{B_{x}(\rho)\cap\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits(x,\cdot)^{-3}\\
&\qquad+\zeta\rho^{-3}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}-B_{x}(\rho)}|F_{A}|+\zeta^{\prime},\end{split}

(7.15)

where
\zeta
depends only on the metric on
Y_{i}
and the choice
of
q_{k}
, and
\zeta^{\prime}
depends on the above as well as the choice
of
A_{0}
. Use Propositions
5.6
and
5.4
to
bound
\|F_{A}\|_{L^{\infty}(X^{\prime\prime})}\leq\zeta_{1}r
, where
\zeta_{1}
depends only on the metric,
\nu
,
\varsigma_{w}
and
\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and apply this bound to bound the first term on the RHS of
(
7.15
). Use the
L^{1}
-estimate for
F_{A}
in
(
6.11
) to bound the second term on the RHS of
(
7.15
). We have, taking
\rho=r^{-1/4}
,
\|\operatorname{\hat{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]})}\leq\zeta_{3}r^{3/4}
, where
\zeta_{3}

depends only on the parameters in (
4.15
),
\nu
, and
e
.
Combining this in turn with the fact that since
A^{\prime}
is in the normalized Coulomb-Neumann gauge,
|c_{k}(A^{\prime}-A_{0})|<2\pi

\forall k
, the asserted bound (
7.14
) follows.

To proceed, combining (
7.14
) with the
L^{1}
-estimate for

F_{A}
in (
6.11
) with (
7.13
),
one has:

\frac{1}{4r}\Big|\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{i}-1/2,l_{i}+1/2]}}(B-B_{0})\wedge(F_{B}+F_{B_{0}}+iw_{r})\,ds\Big|\leq\zeta_{3}r^{-1/4}.

Finally, the claimed inequality in the proposition follows by inserting the bounds from (i), (ii), (iii) above into
(
7.8
).

\Box

In the upcoming proof of Theorem
1.10
, we make use of a
lower bound on
{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}
, which follows from an improved lower bound on
{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
.

Lemma 7.2
.

There are positive constants
r_{0}
,
\zeta_{i}^{\prime}
,
\zeta_{i}^{\prime\prime}

depending only on (
7.2
) with the following significance: For all
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}
and
l,L\geq 0
,

\begin{split}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r},,\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A_{r},\Psi_{r})&\geq-r\zeta^{\prime}_{i}e^{-\kappa_{i}l},\\
{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A_{r},\Psi_{r})&\geq-\zeta_{i}^{\prime\prime}e^{-\kappa_{i}l}\quad\text{if $\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}$.}\end{split}

Proof.
By (
4.38
), (
4.35
), and (
6.11
),

{\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A_{r},\Psi_{r})\geq-r\zeta_{i}e^{-\kappa_{i}l}.

By Lemma
6.6
and (
4.29
),

\begin{split}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A_{r},\Psi_{r})&={\mathscr{E}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A,\Psi)-\frac{ir}{4}\int_{\partial\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}}(B_{r}-B_{0})\wedge{\mathfrak{v}}\\
&\geq-r\zeta_{i}^{\prime}e^{-\kappa_{i}l}.\end{split}

So

{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})=\frac{1}{\pi r}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]})(A_{r},\Psi_{r})-\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l,L]}}\frac{iF_{A_{K}}}{4\pi}\wedge\nu\geq-\zeta_{i}^{\prime\prime}e^{-\kappa_{i}l}.

\Box

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
be as in Theorem
1.8
and
let
e
,
{\mathbb{E}}
be respectively the constants from
(
4.8
) and (
4.11
) determined (via Lemma
4.3
) by (
4.7
).
There exists a positive constant
\zeta
depending only on
(
7.2
such that

{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a})\leq\frac{\textsc{e}}{\pi}+\zeta;\quad\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a})\leq\frac{\textsc{e}}{\pi}+\zeta.

If
X
has no vanishing ends,
{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(X)=\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a})
and

\lim_{r\to\infty}{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(X)=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}}{\pi}-\int_{X}\frac{iF_{A_{K}}}{4\pi}\wedge\nu.

Proof.
Write

\begin{split}{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a})=&\frac{1}{\pi r}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X)(A_{r},\Psi_{r})-\frac{1}{\pi r}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r},,\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(X-X^{{}^{\prime}a})(A_{r},\Psi_{r})\\
&\qquad-\int_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}}\frac{iF_{A_{K}}}{4\pi}\wedge\nu.\end{split}

The assertions of the lemma then follow directly from Lemma

7.2
, (
4.8
), and (
4.11
).

\Box

7.3
Proving Theorem
1.10
and Proposition

1.13
, the non-cylindrical case

We introduce some notations for convenience before embarking on the proofs.

Given two t-curves
\mathbf{C}=[C,\tilde{C}]
,
\mathbf{C}^{\prime}=[C^{\prime},\tilde{C}^{\prime}]
in
(X,\nu)
, and a compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
, let

\text{{\small d}}_{X_{\bullet}}(\mathbf{C}^{\prime},\mathbf{C}):=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits_{X_{\bullet}}(C^{\prime},C)+\|\tilde{C}^{\prime}-\tilde{C}\|_{op,X_{\bullet}},

where
\|\cdot\|_{op,X_{\bullet}}
denotes the norm as currents on

X_{\bullet}
. The same expression is also used when either of the
the entries
\mathbf{C}
or
\mathbf{C}^{\prime}
on the left is replaced by a
general pseudo-holomorphic subvariety
C
or
C^{\prime}
. In this case
the
\tilde{C}
or
\tilde{C}^{\prime}
on the right simply denotes the
associated current of the pseudo-holomorphic subvariety.

Given a t-orbit
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
or a general multi-orbit

\gamma
in
(Y,\theta)
, let
\text{{\small d}}_{Y}(\boldsymbol{\gamma},\cdot)
,
\text{{\small d}}_{Y}(\gamma,\cdot)
be similarly defined.

Let
\text{{\small c}}_{r}:=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
be an admissible solution to

{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(A_{r},\Psi_{r})=0
on the
\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}\nolimits^{c}
admissible pair

(X,\nu)
as
before, and let
\mathbf{C}=[C,\tilde{C}]
be a t-curve. Given a
compact
X_{\bullet}\subset X^{{}^{\prime}a}
, denote:

\begin{split}\text{{\small d}}_{X_{\bullet}}(\text{{\small c}}_{r},\mathbf{C})&:=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits_{X_{\bullet}}(\alpha_{r}^{-1}(0),C)+\Big\|\frac{i}{2\pi}F_{A^{E}_{r}}-\tilde{C}\Big\|_{op,X_{\bullet}}.\end{split}

Recall from Section
2.3
(a) the definition of the set of
t-orbits,
{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{i},{\mathfrak{s}}_{i})
. Let

\text{{\small d}}_{i}:=\min_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{i}),\boldsymbol{\gamma}\neq\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{dist}\,}\nolimits_{Y_{i}}(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma)+\|\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}-\tilde{\gamma}\|_{op,Y_{i}}\big)>0.

Proof of Theorem
1.10
: the non-cylindrical
case.

Assume that
(X,\nu)

is non-cylindrical. The proof for the cylindrical case requires only little
modification and will be postponed to the next subsection.

(a):

Let
\{\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r}:=(A_{r},\Psi_{r})\}_{r\in\Gamma_{0}}
be the sequence from the
conclusion of Theorem
1.8
(a). Rename the t-curve

{\bf C}
in the statement of Theorem
1.8
by

{\bf C}_{0}
. That is to say,
\{(A_{r},\Psi_{r})\}_{r\in\Gamma_{0}}

t-converges to
{\bf C}_{0}
over
X^{{}^{\prime}a}
. Given
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
, let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0,i}
denote the

Y_{i}
-end limit of
{\bf C}_{0}
. Write the sequence
\Gamma_{0}
as
\{r_{n}\}_{n}
. Given
l\in[0,\infty]
,
let
\vec{l}\colon\thinspace{\mathfrak{Y}}_{X}\to[0,\infty]
denote the
function with
\vec{l}_{i}=l
when
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
,

\vec{l}_{i}=\textup{l}_{i}
when
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}
.

Recalling the construction of

{\bf C}_{0}
in the proof of Theorem
1.8
(a) via diagonalization, we choose the
diagonalization process such that
the subsequence
\Gamma_{0}=\{r_{n}\}_{n}\subset\Gamma
so obtained (
\Gamma,\Gamma_{0}
being
as in the statement of Theorem
1.8
) satisfies the
following: Let
\varepsilon_{n}:=e^{-n}
,
L_{n}:=e^{n}
,
r_{n}
increases with

n
,
r_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, and
\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{X_{\bullet}}(\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r_{n}},{\bf C}_{0})\leq\varepsilon_{n}

\forall X_{\bullet}\subset X_{\vec{L}_{n}}
. We find it convenient to restate the
preceding statement in a re-parametrized fashion: Let
n(r)\colon\thinspace\Gamma_{0}\to{\mathbb{N}}

denote the inverse function of the map
n\mapsto r_{n}
, and write

\varepsilon_{T}(r):=\varepsilon_{n(r)}
,

L_{T}(r):=L_{n(r)}=\varepsilon_{T}(r)^{-1}
. Then

\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{X_{\bullet}}(\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r},{\bf C}_{0})\leq\varepsilon_{T}(r)\quad\forall X_{\bullet}\subset X_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)},r\in\Gamma_{0}.

(7.16)

Given
\varepsilon>0
, let
\ell_{i}(\varepsilon)\geq 0
be the minimal

L
such that

\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l,l+3]}}({\bf C}_{0},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,0})\leq\varepsilon

\forall l\geq L
and
\int_{\tilde{C}_{0}|_{\hat{Y}_{i,L}}}\nu\leq\varepsilon/3
.

(7.17)

Such
\ell_{i}
exists because of Proposition

2.16
, and because of (
4.29
).

Let
R(\varepsilon)>1
denote the minimal
r
such that all
of the following hold:

\begin{split}&\text{(i) $L_{T}(r)\geq\ell_{i}(\varepsilon)+3$ $\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$;}\\
&\text{(ii) $\varepsilon_{T}(r)=L_{T}(r)^{-1}\leq(3\|\nu\|_{\infty})^{-1}\varepsilon$;}\\
&\text{(iii) $\zeta_{S}r^{-1/4}\leq\varepsilon/3$ and $r\geq R_{S}$, where $R_{S}$,
$\zeta_{S}$ are as in Proposition \ref {cor:E-cp}.}\end{split}

(7.18)

The preceding conditions for
R(\varepsilon)
, (
7.16
), the
condition (
7.17
) for
\ell(\varepsilon)
, and Proposition

7.1
together ensure that

\forall r\geq R(\varepsilon)
,
\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[L_{T}(r)-5/2,L_{T}(r)-1/2]})\leq\varepsilon
.

(7.19)

To proceed, we need the next two preliminary lemmas.

Lemma 7.4
.

Fix
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
. Suppose that
I_{1}\subset I_{2}\subset\cdots
is a sequence of
intervals in
{\mathbb{R}}
so that
\bigcup_{n}I_{n}=:I_{\infty}
. Assign to
each
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{+}
a triple
(r=r_{n},\text{{\small c}}_{r},m_{r})
, so
that:

•

\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r}
is a solution to the
r
-th version of
Seiberg-Witten equation

{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mu_{r},\hat{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r})=0
;

•

m_{r}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
is unbounded, and

•

I_{n}
is isomorphic by some translation

\tilde{\tau}_{n}
to an interval

I^{\prime}_{n}\subset[m_{r},\infty)
.

Let
\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{n}:=\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{*}(\text{{\small c}}_{r_{n}}|_{\hat{Y}_{i,I^{\prime}_{n}}})
. Then there is a t-curve
\mathbf{C}\subset I_{\infty}\times Y_{i}
for the cylindrical admissible pair

(I_{\infty}\times Y_{i},\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i})
, and a subsequence of
\{\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{n}\}_{n}
,
denoted by the same notation below, which (locally) t-converges to

\mathbf{C}
over
I_{\infty}\times Y_{i}
. Moreover, there is an
upper bound on
{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}(\mathbf{C})
depending only on

h
,
{\mathfrak{s}}
, and other data mentioned in the end of
Theorem
1.8
.

Proof.
Passing to a subsequence if necessary, we may assume that
m_{r}
increases with
r
and goes to infinity
as
r\to\infty
. Meanwhile,
\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{n}=:(\tilde{A}_{n},\tilde{\Psi}_{n})
is a solution to the Seiberg-Witten
equation
{\mathfrak{S}}_{\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{*}\mu_{r},0}(\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{n})=0
over

(\tilde{\tau}_{n})^{-1}\hat{Y}_{i,I^{\prime}_{n}}\simeq I_{n}\times Y_{i}
. Over this,

\|\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{*}(\nu|_{\hat{Y}_{i,I^{\prime}_{n}}})-\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i}\|_{C^{k}}\leq\zeta_{i}e^{-\kappa_{i}m_{r}}\to 0

(7.20)

as
n\to\infty
, while
\|\tilde{\tau}_{n}^{*}(w_{r}|_{\hat{Y}_{i,I^{\prime}_{n}}})-\pi_{2}^{*}w_{i,r}\|_{C^{k}}=0
by assumption.

Because of (
7.3
)
(and a diagonalization argument), there is a subsequence of

\{\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{n}\}_{n}
(again denoted by the same notation), and a
current
\tilde{C}
on
I_{\infty}\times Y_{i}
, so that over any compact subset
V\subset I_{k}\times Y_{i}
the corresponding sequence of currents

\{\frac{i}{2\pi}F_{\tilde{A}^{E}_{n}}|_{V}\}_{n\geq k}
converge to

\tilde{C}|_{V}
. Following Steps 5 and 6 of Section
3.6
’s
summary of Taubes’ arguments, the support of
\tilde{C}
is
a
J_{i}
-holomorphic subvariety
C
, where
J_{i}
is the almost complex
structure on
I_{\infty}\times(Y_{i}-\nu_{i}^{-1}(0)
defined by
(\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i})^{+}
. Moreover,

\mathbf{C}=(C,\tilde{C})
is a t-curve for the cylindrical
admissible pair

(I_{\infty}\times Y_{i},\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i})
and
\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{n}

t-converges to
\mathbf{C}
.
The claim about the
\omega
-energy of
\mathbf{C}
follows from
the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
1.8
.
\Box

Lemma 7.5
.

Fix
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
and a
positive number
\epsilon
,

\epsilon\leq\text{{\small d}}_{i}/8
.
Let
\{\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r}\}_{r\in\Gamma_{0}}
be the sequence from the conclusion
of Theorem
1.8
, chosen in the aforementioned manner, and
let
L_{T}(r)
be as in (
7.16
).
Then there
exist positive constants
h_{i}=h_{i}(\epsilon)\leq\text{{\small d}}_{i}/8
,
{\mathscr{R}}_{i}={\mathscr{R}}_{i}(\epsilon)
such that the
following holds: For any
r\geq{\mathscr{R}}_{i}
,
r\in\Gamma_{0}
, and
l\geq L_{T}(r)
, if

\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l+1/2,l+3/2]})\leq h_{i},

then
\text{{\small d}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l,l+3]}}(\text{{\small c}}_{r},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma})<\epsilon
for some
t-orbit
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
on
(Y_{i},\nu_{i})
.

Proof.
Suppose the contrary. Then for any pair of positive numbers
\delta>0
and
{\rm r}\geq 1
, there exist

r\geq{\rm r}
,
r\in\Gamma_{0}
, and
l\geq L_{T}(r)
such that

\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l+1/2,l+3/2]})\leq\delta

(7.21)

and

\text{{\small d}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l,l+3]}}(\text{{\small c}}_{r},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma})\geq\epsilon\quad\text{for all $\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{i})$.}

(7.22)

Choose a sequence of

(\delta,{\rm r})
, denoted
\{(\delta_{n},{\rm r}_{n})\}_{n}
, with

\lim_{n\to\infty}\delta_{n}\to 0
and
{\rm r}_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, and denote the corresponding
r,l
by

r^{\prime}_{n}
,
l_{n}
respectively. Note that
l_{n}\to\infty

as
n\to\infty
, since
l_{n}\geq L_{T}(r^{\prime}_{n})
and
L_{T}(r^{\prime}_{n})\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. Apply Lemma

7.4
with
I_{1}=I_{2}\cdots=I_{\infty}=[0,3]
,
and with
I_{n}^{\prime}=[l_{n},l_{n}+3]
. This gives a t-curve
\mathbf{C}=[C,\tilde{C}]
on
the cylindrical admissible
pair
(\hat{Y}_{i,[0,3]},\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i})
and a subsequence of

\{(\delta_{n},{\rm r}_{n})\}_{n}
(denoted by the same
notation), such that with
\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{n}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r_{n}}
,

\text{{\small d}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[0,3]}}(\tau^{*}_{l_{n}}(\text{{\small c}}^{\prime}_{n}|_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{n},l_{n}+3]}}),\mathbf{C})=\text{{\small d}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{n},l_{n}+3]}}(\text{{\small c}}^{\prime}_{n},\tau_{-l_{n}}\mathbf{C})\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$. }

(7.23)

Combining with Proposition
7.1
,
this implies that

\begin{split}0\leq&\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\tilde{C}|_{\hat{Y}_{i,[1/2+t,3/2+t]}})\,dt\\
&\quad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r_{n}}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{n}+1/2,l_{n}+3/2]})\\
&\quad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r_{n}}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l_{n}+1/2,l_{n}+3/2]})=0.\end{split}

The
v
-energy
{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}
for t-curves being nonnegative, this implies that

{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\tilde{C}|_{\hat{Y}_{i,[1,2]}})=0
. But by Lemma

2.9
, this in turn implies that

\mathbf{C}|_{\hat{Y}_{i,[1,2]}}=[1,2]\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}\subset[1,2]\times Y_{i}\simeq\hat{Y}_{i,[1,2]}
for some
\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{i})
, and hence
\mathbf{C}=[0,3]\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}\subset\hat{Y}_{i,[0,3]}
. This, together with
(
7.23
), contradict the
assumption (
7.22
).

\Box

To continue with the proof of Theorem
1.10
, fix
\epsilon\leq\text{{\small d}}_{i}/8
, and let

\varepsilon_{0}:=h_{i}(\epsilon)
, where
h_{i}(\epsilon)
is as in Lemma
7.5
.
Divide
[L_{T}(r)-5/2,\infty)
into
intervals
I^{*}
of length 2. For each
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
, let
\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}\subset[L_{T}(r)-5/2,\infty)\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
be the union of those
I^{*}

satisfying

\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,I^{*}})\geq\varepsilon_{0}.

(7.24)

Let
\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
be the closure of
[L_{T}(r)-5/2,\infty)-\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
.
By Lemma

7.5
, this implies that when
r\geq{\mathscr{R}}_{i}(\epsilon)
, for each of the length-2
interval,
I^{*}=[l-5/2,l-1/2]\subset\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
, there is a t-orbit
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{*}\in{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{i})
so that

\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l-3,l]}}(\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{*})\leq\epsilon.

(7.25)

Since

{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{i})
is discrete, this t-orbit
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{*}
only depends on the connected component of

\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
that
I^{*}
lies in.
Let
\Lambda_{i,r}+1
be the number of connected components of

\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
. The set of
connected components of
\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
is naturally ordered,
and we denote it by
\{\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}\}_{k=0}^{\Lambda_{i,r}}
: for any pair
k<k^{\prime}
and
any
l\in\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}
and
l^{\prime}\in\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k^{\prime})}
,
l<l^{\prime}
. Denote the t-orbit
associated to the component
\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}^{(k)}_{i,r}
by

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,r}^{(k)}
. By (
7.19
), the interval

[L_{T}(r)-5/2,L_{T}(r)-1/2]\subset\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(0)}
, and it follows from the
definition of
R(\varepsilon)
that

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,r}^{(0)}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0,i}

\forall r\geq R(\varepsilon_{0})
. Let
r_{0}
denote the maximum among

R(\varepsilon_{0})
and
{\mathscr{R}}_{i}(\epsilon)

\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
, and assume that
r\geq r_{0}
from now
on.

Correspondingly, label the connected components of
\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
by

\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{+}
, so that
\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}
lies between

\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k-1)}
and

\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}
. We claim that each

\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}
has finite length, and the number of connected components of

\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
, namely
\Lambda_{i,r}
, is bounded independently of
r
.
To see this, write:

\begin{split}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})&=\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})\\
&\quad+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\Big(\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})+\sum_{k=1}^{\Lambda_{i,r}}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})\Big)\end{split}

(7.26)

By Proposition
7.1
and (
7.16
),
\forall r\geq r_{0}

\begin{split}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})&\geq\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})-\zeta_{S}\,r^{-1/4}\\
&\geq\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}(\tilde{C}_{0}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}+\vec{t}}})\,dt-\|\nu\|_{\infty}\varepsilon_{T}(r)-\zeta_{S}\,r^{-1/4}\\
&\geq-\|\nu\|_{\infty}\,\varepsilon_{T}(r)-\zeta_{S}\,r^{-1/4}.\end{split}

By definition, for each pair of
i,k
,
\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})\geq|\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}|\varepsilon_{0}/2
. By Lemma

7.2
, for each
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}

\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})\geq-\zeta_{i}^{\prime\prime}e^{-\kappa_{i}L_{T}(r)}

Combine the above bounds with (
7.26
) to get:

\begin{split}&\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{\Lambda_{i,r}}\,|\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}|\,\varepsilon_{0}/2\\
&\qquad\leq\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{\Lambda_{i,r}}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})\\
&\qquad\leq\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a})(\text{{\small c}}_{r})+\|\nu\|_{\infty}\varepsilon_{T}(r)+\zeta_{S}r^{-1/4}+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\zeta_{i}^{\prime\prime}e^{-\kappa_{i}L_{T}(r)}.\end{split}

(7.27)

By Lemma
7.3
, the right hand side of the preceding
inequality has an
r
-independent upper bound. This in turn gives an
r
-independent upper
bound on the left hand side for all
r\geq r_{0}
. Recalling that every

|\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}|
is a positive even integer, this
gives an upper bound for each
\Lambda_{i,r}
and
|\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}|
.

The preceding uniform bounds on
\Lambda_{i,r}
enables us to pass
to a subsequence
\Gamma_{1}
of
\Gamma_{0}
,
such that
\Lambda_{i,r}=\Lambda_{i}

\forall r\in\Gamma_{1}
for some
\Lambda_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0}
. Given
k,k^{\prime}
,
1\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq\Lambda_{i}
, Let

\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}_{i,r}(k,k^{\prime})
denote the distance between the centers of
intervals

\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}
and

\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k^{\prime})}
. Let

\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}_{i,r}(0,k)
denote the distance between the center of

\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}
and
L_{T}(r)
.
We choose the subsequence
\Gamma_{1}
so that:

\hss\left\{\mkern-20.0mu\parbox{310.4979pt}{\begin{itemize}\par\itemize@item@For every $i$ and $k\in\Lambda_{i}$, the t-orbit $\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,r}^{(k)}$ is independent of $r$. This is possible because
${\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{i})$ consists of finitely many elements.
\par\itemize@item@Given $i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$, and any pair $k,k^{\prime}$, $0\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq\Lambda_{i}$, the distance
$\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}_{i,r}(k,k^{\prime})$ is non-decreasing with respect to $r$.
\par\end{itemize}}\right.

(7.28)

Fix
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
. Let
k_{1}
be the smallest
k
such that
\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}_{i,r}(0,k)
is
unbounded with respect to
r
. Let
{\mathfrak{I}}_{1}
denote the finite interval
{\mathfrak{I}}_{1}\supset\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(1)}\cup\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k_{1})}
with
\partial{\mathfrak{I}}_{1}\subset\partial\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(1)}\cup\partial\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k_{1})}
if
k_{1}
exists; otherwise
set
{\mathfrak{J}}_{1}=\emptyset
. Given
any pair
k
,
k^{\prime}
such that

k<k^{\prime}\leq\Lambda_{i}
and
\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}_{i,r}(k,k^{\prime})
is
bounded with respect to
r
, we form a finite interval
{\mathfrak{I}}_{*}\supset\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}\cup\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k^{\prime})}
with
\partial{\mathfrak{I}}_{*}\subset\partial\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}\cup\partial\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k^{\prime})}
. Given

i
and
r
, let
{\mathfrak{J}}_{i,r}\subset[L_{T}(r)-1/2,\infty)
denote

\bigcup_{*}{\mathfrak{J}}_{*}\cup{\mathfrak{J}}_{1}\cup\bigcup_{k}\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}
,
where the index
k
runs over all
k
such that

\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}\subset[L_{T}(r)-1/2,\infty)-\bigcup_{*}{\mathfrak{J}}_{*}-{\mathfrak{J}}_{1}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}
denote the number of connected components of

{\mathfrak{J}}_{i,r}
, and when
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>0
, let

\{{\mathfrak{I}}_{i,r}^{k}\}_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}
denote the set of connected components of

{\mathfrak{J}}_{i,r}
. These connected components are ordered such that

{\mathfrak{I}}_{i,r}^{k^{\prime}}
lies to the right of
{\mathfrak{I}}_{i,r}^{k}\subset[L_{T}(r)-1/2,\infty)
for any
k,k^{\prime}
such that
1\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}
. For any

1\leq k<{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}
, the intervals
{\mathfrak{I}}_{i,r}^{k}

and
{\mathfrak{I}}_{i,r}^{k+1}
are separated by one of the connected
components of
\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
, say
\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k^{\prime})}
.
We rename the
t-orbit associated to this connected component,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,r}^{(k^{\prime})}
, by
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{k}
. (Recall the second bullet of (
7.28
).) Meanwhile,
{\mathfrak{J}}_{i,r}^{1}
is adjacent to the first
connected component of
\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
, i.e.

\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(0)}
, on the left; and
{\mathfrak{J}}_{i,r}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}

is adjacent to the last connected component of

\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}
, i.e.
\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(\Lambda_{i})}
, which is a half-infinite interval of the form

[L_{+},\infty)
. The t-orbits associated to

\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(0)}
and
\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(\Lambda_{i})}
are respectively
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0,i}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
. (
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
is as in Condition (1) in the
statement of Theorem
1.8
.) Let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{0}:=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0,i}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}:=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
. Note that when
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}=0
,

{\mathfrak{J}}_{i,r}=\emptyset
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0,i}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
.

Recall also that by (
7.27
), the length

|\invbreve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(k)}|
has an upper bound independent of

r
,
i
, and
k
. This means that there is a finite number

\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}\geq 1
so that
|{\mathfrak{I}}_{i,r}^{k}|\leq 2\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}
for all
i,k

and
r
.
Let
L^{i}_{k,r}\in[L_{T}(r)-1/2,\infty)
denote the mid point of the
interval
{\mathfrak{I}}_{i,r}^{k}
, and let
{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}=\{i\,|\,i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m},{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>0\}
. By construction, we have:

\hss\left\{\mkern-20.0mu\parbox{310.4979pt}{\begin{itemize}\par\itemize@item@Given $i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}$, for every $k$ with $1\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1$,
$\Delta^{i}_{k,r}:=L^{i}_{k+1,r}-L^{i}_{k,r}$ is non-decreasing with respect to
$r$. Also, $\Delta^{i}_{0,r}:=L^{i}_{1,r}-L_{T}(r)$ is non-decreasing with respect to
$r$. (cf. the second bullet in (\ref {def:Gamma1}).)
\par\itemize@item@Given $i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}$, for every $k$ with $0\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1$,
$\lim_{r\to\infty}\Delta^{i}_{k,r}\to\infty$ as $r\to\infty$.
\par\end{itemize}}\right.

(7.29)

In particular, there exists an
R_{1}\geq R_{0}
such that

\Delta^{i}_{k,r}>2\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}+4
for all
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1\}
,
\forall r\geq R_{1}
. Assume that
r\geq R_{1}
from now on.

Fix
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}
. Given
r
and
k
,
1\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}
, let

I^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}\supset{\mathfrak{J}}^{k}_{i,r}
be defined by

I^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}:=\begin{cases}[L^{i}_{k-1,r}+\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}},L^{i}_{k+1,r}-\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}]&\text{when $2\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1$};\\
[L_{T}(r)-3,L^{i}_{2,r}-\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}]&\text{when $k=1$};\\
[L^{i}_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1,r}+\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}},\infty)&\text{when $k={\mathfrak{K}}_{i}$}.\end{cases}

Let
I_{k,r}^{i}:=\tau_{-L^{i}_{k,r}}I^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}
. That is,

I^{i}_{k,r}:=\begin{cases}[-\Delta^{i}_{k-1,r}+\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}},\Delta^{i}_{k,r}-\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}]&\text{when $2\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1$};\\
[-\Delta^{i}_{0,r}-3,\Delta^{i}_{1,r}-\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}]&\text{when $k=1$};\\
[-\Delta{}^{i}_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1,r}+\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}},\infty)&\text{when $k={\mathfrak{K}}_{i}$}.\end{cases}

Then by (
7.29
),
for every fixed
1\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}
,
I^{i}_{k,r}\subset I^{i}_{k,r^{\prime}}
when

r^{\prime}>r
, and together they
form a nested sequence of intervals exhausting
{\mathbb{R}}=I^{i}_{k,\infty}
. Let

\begin{split}&\breve{{\mathfrak{I}}}^{i}_{k,r}:=\\
&\quad\begin{cases}I^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}\cap I^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k+1,r}=[L_{k,r}^{i}+\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}},L_{k+1,r}^{i}-\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}]\subset\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}&\text{when $1\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1$;}\\
[L_{T}(r)-5/2,L_{1,r}^{i}-\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}}]\subset\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(0)}&\text{when $k=0$;}\\
[L_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i},r}^{i}+\operatorname{\text{{\small m}}},\infty)\subset\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}^{(\Lambda_{i})}&\text{when $k={\mathfrak{K}}_{i}$.}\\
\end{cases}\end{split}

Then by construction (recall (
7.25
)), for
r\geq R_{1}
,

\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,I^{*}}}(\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{k})\leq\epsilon\quad\forall\,I^{*}\subset\breve{{\mathfrak{I}}}_{k,r}^{i},\,|I^{*}|=2.

(7.30)

Consider now the sequence
\{\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{r}:=\tau_{-L^{i}_{k,r}}\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r}|_{\hat{Y}_{i,I_{k,r}^{\prime}}}\}_{r\in\Gamma_{1}}
.
\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{r}
is defined
on
I_{k,r}^{i}\times Y_{i}
. Apply Lemma

7.4
to
\{\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{r}\}_{r\in\Gamma_{1}}
to get a t-curve
{\bf C}_{i,k}
on
({\mathbb{R}}\times Y_{i},\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i})
and a subsequence
\Gamma^{i}_{k}
of
\Gamma_{1}
so
that
\{\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{r}\}_{r\in\Gamma_{k}^{i}}
t-converges to
{\bf C}_{i,k}
. More specifically, the
subsequence
\Gamma^{i}_{k}
is chosen via diagonalization so that

\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{I^{*}\times Y_{i}}(\text{{\scriptsize b}}_{r_{n}},{\bf C}_{i,k})\leq e^{-n}\quad\forall I^{*}\subset I^{i}_{k,r_{n}},\forall n,

(7.31)

where
r_{n}
now denotes the
n
-th element in the sequence
\Gamma^{i}_{k}=\{r_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{+}}
. Comparing the preceding inequality with
(
7.30
), we see that the
-\infty
-limit of

{\bf C}_{i,k}
is
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{k-1}
, and its
+\infty
-limit
is
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{k}
. Thus, when
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>0
,
\{{\bf C}_{i,k}\}_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}

forms a chain of t-curves on
({\mathbb{R}}\times Y_{i},\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i})
,
{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}
, with
-\infty
-limit
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{0}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,0}
, and with
+\infty
-limit
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
. When
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}=0
,

\breve{{\mathscr{I}}}_{i,r}=[L_{T}(r)-5/2,\infty)
is connected; by
(
7.25
),

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0,i}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i},
and

\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,I^{*}}}(\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i})\leq\epsilon\quad\forall\,I^{*}\subset[L_{T}(r)-5/2,\infty),\,|I^{*}|=2.

In this case, let
{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}
be the 0-component chain of t-curves with
a single rest orbit
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0,i}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
. Now

{\mathfrak{C}}:=\{{\bf C}_{0},\{{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\}
forms a chain of t-curves on
(X^{{}^{\prime}a},\nu)
,
with
Y_{i}
-end limit
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
for each
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
. Finally, choose an order of the finite set

\{(i,k)\,|\,\text{$i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}$ with ${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>0$},\,1\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}\},

and choose
consecutive subsequences
\Gamma^{i}_{k}\subset\Gamma_{1}
in this order:

\Gamma^{i}_{k}\subset\Gamma^{i^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}
whenever
(i,k)>(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})
to get a subsequence
\Gamma^{\prime}=\bigcap_{\{(i,k)\}}\Gamma^{i}_{k}\subset\Gamma_{1}
. Then
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
{\mathfrak{C}}
together satisfies Item (a) asserted by Theorem

1.10
.

(b) and (d):

Recall that

{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}({\mathfrak{C}})={\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}({\bf C}_{0})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m},{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>0}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}({\bf C}_{i,k})
. We already
provided a bound on
{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}({\bf C}_{0})
in
the proof of Theorem
1.8
(b). Thus, to verify Item (b) in
the statement of the theorem, it suffices to bound
each
{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}({\bf C}_{i,k})
. As in the proof of Theorem

1.8
(b), this reduces to the following two tasks:
(i) obtaining a
l
-independent bound on

{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}({\bf C}_{i,k}|_{[l,l+1]})
; (ii) bounding
{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{i,k})
.

(i): Fix
i
with
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>0
and
k
. Given
l\in{\mathbb{R}}
, there exists
{\mathfrak{r}}_{l}\geq R_{1}

such that
[l,l+1]\subset I^{i}_{k,r}

\forall r\geq{\mathfrak{r}}_{l}
. Let

r_{n}
denote the
n
-th element of
\Gamma_{k}^{i}
, and consider an
r_{n}\geq{\mathfrak{r}}_{l}
. Then
according to (
7.31
) and (
6.11
),

\begin{split}&{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}({\bf C}_{i,k}|_{[l,l+1]})-\frac{1}{2}c_{1}({\mathfrak{s}}_{i})\cdot[\theta]+\zeta_{\theta}\\
&\qquad\leq\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,[L^{i}_{k,r_{n}}+l,L^{i}_{k,r_{n}}+l+1]}}\frac{iF_{A_{r_{n}}^{E}}}{2\pi}\wedge\nu_{i}+e^{-n}\|\nu_{i}\|_{\infty}\\
&\qquad\leq\zeta^{\prime}+e^{-n}\|\nu_{i}\|_{\infty},\end{split}

where
\theta=*\nu_{i}
and
\zeta_{\theta}
is as in Proposition

3.15
, and
\zeta^{\prime}>0
is independent of
n

(equivalently,
r=r_{n}
),
k
, and

l
. This gives us an
l
-independent bound

{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}({\bf C}_{i,k}|_{[l,l+1]})\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{1}({\mathfrak{s}}_{i})\cdot[\theta]+\zeta^{\prime}-\zeta_{\theta}.

(7.32)

(ii): When
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>1
, let
l^{i}_{k,r}:=(L^{i}_{k,r}+L^{i}_{k+1,r})/2
denote the mid point of the interval

\breve{{\mathfrak{I}}}^{i}_{k,r}
for
1\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1
. Define
J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}
,

I^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}\supset J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}\supset{\mathfrak{I}}^{k}_{i,r}
by

J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}:=\begin{cases}[l^{i}_{k-1,r},l^{i}_{k,r}]&\text{when
${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>1$ and $2\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1$;}\\
[L_{T}(r)-\frac{5}{2},l^{i}_{1,r}]&\text{when ${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>1$ and $k=1$;}\\
[l^{i}_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1,r},\infty\big)&\text{when ${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>1$ and $k={\mathfrak{K}}_{i}$;}\\
[L_{T}(r)-\frac{5}{2},\infty)&\text{when ${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}=1$ and $k=1$.}\end{cases}

Let
J^{i}_{k,r}=\tau_{-L^{i}_{k,r}}J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}
. In other words,

J^{i}_{k,r}:=\begin{cases}\big[-\frac{\Delta^{i}_{k-1,r}}{2},\frac{\Delta^{i}_{k,r}}{2}\big]&\text{when ${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>1$ and $2\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1$;}\\
\big[-\Delta^{i}_{0,r}-\frac{5}{2},\frac{\Delta^{i}_{1,r}}{2}\big]&\text{when when ${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>1$ and $k=1$;}\\
\big[-\frac{\Delta^{i}_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1,r}}{2},\infty\big)&\text{when ${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}>1$ and $k={\mathfrak{K}}_{i}$.}\\
\big[-\Delta^{i}_{0,r}-\frac{5}{2},\infty)&\text{when when ${\mathfrak{K}}_{i}=1$ and $k=1$;}\\
\end{cases}

Then
J^{i}_{k,r}\subset I^{i}_{k,r}
, yet
according to (
7.29
),

\{J^{i}_{k,r}\}_{r\in\Gamma^{\prime}}
still forms a nested sequence of
intervals exhausting
{\mathbb{R}}
. Meanwhile, for every fixed
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}
and

r
,
\bigcup_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}=[L_{T}(r)-5/2,\infty)
,
and the interiors of
J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}
are mutually disjoint. Now

\begin{split}&\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a})\\
&=\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}-{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,L_{T}(r)-\frac{5}{2}})\\
&=\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}-{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\bar{{\mathscr{V}}}_{r}(\hat{Y}_{i,L_{T}(r)-\frac{5}{2}})\\
&\qquad+{\mathscr{O}}(r^{-1/4})\\
&=\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{0}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}}})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{i,k}|_{J^{i}_{k,r}\times Y_{i}})+{\mathscr{O}}(e^{-n_{r}})+{\mathscr{O}}(r^{-1/4})\\
&\qquad+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{\tau_{t}\hat{Y}_{i,J^{{}^{\prime}i}_{k,r}}}\frac{i}{2\pi}F_{A^{E}}\wedge(\nu-\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i})\,dt\\
&\qquad+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}-{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{\hat{Y}_{i,L_{T}(r)-\frac{5}{2}+t}}\frac{i}{2\pi}F_{A^{E}}\wedge(\nu-\pi_{2}^{*}\nu_{i})\,dt,\\
\end{split}

(7.33)

where
r\in\Gamma^{\prime}
and
n_{r}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{+}
are related as follows:

r
is the
n_{r}
-th element in
\Gamma^{\prime}
. In the above, the
second equality follows from
Proposition
7.1
, and the third equality is a consequence of
the construction of
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
{\bf C}_{i,k}
; see in particular
(
7.16
) and (
7.31
).
Using (
6.11
) and (
4.29
) to estimate the last two
terms above, we have:

\begin{split}\bar{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{r}(X^{{}^{\prime}a})&=\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{0}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}}})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}^{\prime}}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{i,k}|_{J^{i}_{k,r}\times Y_{i}})\\
&\qquad\quad+{\mathscr{O}}(e^{-n_{r}})+{\mathscr{O}}(r^{-1/4})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}{\mathscr{O}}(e^{-\kappa_{i}L_{T}(r)})\end{split}

(7.34)

Together with Lemma
7.3
, the preceding identity then
implies that

\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{0}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}}})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{i,k}|_{J^{i}_{k,r}\times Y_{i}})\leq\frac{\textsc{e}}{\pi}+\zeta

for all sufficiently large
r
.
Note that
as
functions of
r
,
\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{0}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}}})
,
\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{i,k}|_{J^{i}_{k,r}\times Y_{i}})
are all nondecreasing, nonnegative functions, and recall
that
\{J^{i}_{k,r}\}_{r}
exhausts
{\mathbb{R}}
when
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}^{\prime}
. Consequently,

\lim_{r\to\infty}\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{0}|_{X^{{}^{\prime}a}_{\vec{L}_{T}(r)-\vec{\frac{5}{2}}}})=\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{0})
,
and for all
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m},k
, the limit

\lim_{r\to\infty}\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{i,k}|_{J^{i}_{k,r}\times Y_{i}})
exists and equals
{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{i,k})
.
Take the
r\to\infty
limit on both sides of the
identity (
7.34
) and apply Lemma
7.3
. We get:

\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\mathfrak{C}}):=\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}(\mathbf{C}_{0})+\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}}{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}({\bf C}_{i,k})\leq\textsc{e}/\pi+\zeta,

(7.35)

where
\zeta
is as in Lemma
7.3
. Also, when
X
has
no vanishing ends, by (
4.11
)

\begin{split}{\mathscr{F}}_{\nu}({\mathfrak{C}})&=\bar{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\nu}({\mathfrak{C}})\\
&=\lim_{r\to\infty}\big((\pi r)^{-1}{\mathscr{E}}_{top}^{\mu_{r}}(A_{r},\Psi_{r})\big)-\int_{X}\frac{iF_{A_{K}}}{4\pi}\wedge\nu\\
&=\frac{i}{2\pi}\int_{X}F_{A_{0}^{E}}\wedge\nu+\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}[\nu_{i}]\cdot\jmath_{h}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i})+\frac{1}{2\pi}i^{*}[\nu]\cdot h_{A_{0}}(\operatorname{\text{{\scriptsize h}}}),\end{split}

(7.36)

as claimed in Item (d) in the statement of the theorem. Meanwhile,
Item (b) in the statement of the theorem follows from a combination of
(
7.35
), (
7.32
), and Theorem
1.8

(b).

(c):

Let

l^{i}_{0,r}:=L_{T}(r)-3/2
. Let

u_{r}\in{\mathscr{G}}
be such that

(A_{r}^{\prime},\Psi_{r}^{\prime}):=u_{r}\cdot(A_{r},\Psi_{r})
is in a temporal gauge on
\hat{Y}_{i,\breve{{\mathfrak{I}}}^{i}_{k,r}}

\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
and
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}\}
. Given
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}
and
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1\}
, let
B^{i}_{k,r}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Conn}}\nolimits(Y_{i})
,
\Phi^{i}_{k,r}\in\Gamma({\mathbb{S}}_{i})
be

B^{i}_{k,r}:=B_{0}+\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}(B_{r}(l^{i}_{k,r}+t)-B_{0})\,dt,\quad\Phi^{i}_{k,r}:=\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\Phi_{r}(l^{i}_{k,r}+t)\,dt,

where

(B_{r}(s),\Phi_{r}(s))=(A_{r}^{\prime},\Psi^{\prime}_{r})|_{Y_{i:s}}
.
When
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{v}
, set
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}=0
, and for all
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}
, let

{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{i},r}:={\mathfrak{c}}_{i,r}
. When
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}
,
let
{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{Y_{i}}
denote the gauge-equivalence class
of
(B^{i}_{k,r},\Phi^{i}_{k,r})
when
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1\}
.
Recall that given
{\mathfrak{c}}=[(B,\Phi)]\in{\mathscr{B}}_{Y_{i}}
,
\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}

denotes the 1-current

\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}=\frac{iF_{B^{E}}}{2\pi}=\frac{iF_{B}}{4\pi}-\frac{iF_{B^{K_{i}}}}{4\pi},

where
K_{i}^{-1}
denotes the anti-canonical bundle over
Y_{i}
defined
by
\nu_{i}
, and
B^{K_{i}}
denotes the connection on
K_{i}^{-1}
determined by
the Levi-Civita connection.
Let
[x]_{a}\subset{\mathbb{R}}
denote the length
a
interval centered
at
x
. Given a 1-form
\mu
on

Y_{i}
, the evaluation of the exact 1-current

\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}_{k,r}^{i}-\tilde{\gamma}_{i}^{k}

on
\mu
is equal to the evaluation of the 2-current

\frac{iF_{A_{r}}}{4\pi}-\frac{iF_{B^{K_{i}}}}{4\pi}-\pi_{2}^{*}\tilde{\gamma}_{i}^{k}
on the 2-form
\pi_{2}^{*}\mu\wedge ds
over
\hat{Y}_{i,[l^{i}_{k,r}]_{1}}
. The absolute value of this is bounded by

\|\mu\|_{\infty}\Big(\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l^{i}_{k,r}]_{1}}}(\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{k}_{i})+\Big\|\frac{iF_{A_{K}}}{4\pi}-\frac{iF_{B^{K_{i}}}}{4\pi}\Big\|_{L^{1}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l^{i}_{k,r}]_{1}})}\Big),

and therefore

\begin{split}&\|\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}_{k,r}^{i}-\tilde{\gamma}_{i}^{k}\|_{op}\leq\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[l^{i}_{k,r}]_{1}}}(\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{k}_{i})+\Big\|\frac{iF_{A^{K}}}{4\pi}-\frac{iF_{B^{K_{i}}}}{4\pi}\Big\|_{L^{1}(\hat{Y}_{i,[l^{i}_{k,r}]_{1}})}\\
&\qquad\leq\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[-s^{i}_{k,r}]_{1}}}(\tau_{-L^{i}_{k+1,r}}\operatorname{\text{{\small c}}}_{r},{\bf C}_{i,k+1})+\operatorname{\text{{\small d}}}_{\hat{Y}_{i,[-s^{i}_{k,r}]_{1}}}({\bf C}_{i,k+1},{\mathbb{R}}\times\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{k}_{i})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\zeta_{i}e^{-\kappa_{i}l^{i}_{k,r}}\\
&\qquad\to 0\quad\text{as $r\to\infty$},\end{split}

where
s^{i}_{k,r}=\Delta^{i}_{k,r}/2
when
k\in\{1,\ldots,{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}-1\}
, and
s^{i}_{0,r}:=\Delta^{i}_{0,r}+3/2
. In the above, we
used (
4.29
), (
7.31
) and the facts that
s^{i}_{k,r}
,
s^{i}_{k,r}

both go to
\infty
as

, and that the

-\infty
-limit of

is
\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{k}_{i}
. Given that
\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}_{k,r}^{i}=\frac{i}{2\pi}F_{B^{i}_{k,r}}

converges to

, The arguments in the proof of Theorem
1.3
in

may

) to show that

converges in the current topology. We
denote this limit by
[B^{i}_{k,\infty}]
.
Thus, the arguments in Lemma
3.19
shows that there
exists an
R_{2}\geq R_{1}
such that for all

satisfying

, we have canonical isomorphisms of affine spaces

\begin{split}&\pi_{Y_{i}}({\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k-1,r^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r^{\prime}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{Y_{i}}({\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k-1,r},{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r})\quad\forall i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m},\,k\in\{1,\ldots,{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}\};\\
&\pi_{0}({\mathscr{B}}_{X}(\{{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{0,r^{\prime}}\}_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{0}({\mathscr{B}}_{X}(\{{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{0,r}\}_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}})\end{split}

via concatenations with the distinguished elements

o_{Y_{i}}({\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r})

\in\pi_{Y_{i}}({\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r})
for

,
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}
. (When

,
{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{0,r}={\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{0,r^{\prime}}:=[(B_{i},\Phi_{i})]

-end limit of

, and

the definition of
{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}
; and that

. Each of the preceding maps fits into its respective version of the diagram

):

\textstyle{\pi_{0}({\mathscr{B}}_{X}(\{{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{0,r^{\prime}}\}_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}({\mathscr{B}}_{X}(\{{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{0,r}\}_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathfrak{h}}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{H}}^{\mathbb{R}}\big((X^{{}^{\prime}a},\nu),\{\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{i}_{0,r^{\prime}}\}_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{r^{\prime},r}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{H}}^{\mathbb{R}}\big((X^{{}^{\prime}a},\nu),\{\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{i}_{0,r}\}_{i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{m}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\infty}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{H}}^{\mathbb{R}}\big((X^{{}^{\prime}a},\nu),\{\tilde{\gamma}^{0}_{i}\}_{i}\big);}

and for
i\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\prime}_{m}
,

,

\textstyle{\pi_{Y_{i}}({\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k-1,r^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{*}
\scriptstyle{{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}}

\scriptstyle{{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}}

\textstyle{{\mathscr{H}}^{\mathbb{R}}(Y_{i},\nu_{i},{\mathfrak{s}}_{i};\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{i}_{k-1,r^{\prime}},\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{i}_{k,r^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{r^{\prime},r}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{H}}^{\mathbb{R}}(Y_{i},\nu_{i},{\mathfrak{s}}_{i};\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{i}_{k-1,r},\tilde{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{i}_{k,r})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\infty}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{H}}^{\mathbb{R}}(Y_{i},\nu_{i},{\mathfrak{s}}_{i};\tilde{\gamma}_{i}^{k-1},\tilde{\gamma}_{i}^{k}),}

where the horizonal map in the top row labeled
*
is given by

c\mapsto o_{Y_{i}}({\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k-1,r},{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k-1,r^{\prime}})*c*o_{Y_{i}}({\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{c}}^{i}_{k,r})
. Recalling that in the cylindrical
case,
{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}
factors as
\underline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\circ\Pi_{*}
and combing this

{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}
,
{\mathfrak{h}}
,
\underline{{\mathfrak{h}}}
preserve
